<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The convergence rate of the implicit midpoint method for the stochastic Korteweg-de Vries equation had remained an open question since the work of Debussche and Printems; this paper addresses that gap.

Method: Uses coercivity of a classical KdV invariant to obtain exponential integrability estimates for exact and comparison solutions, controls nonlinear error growth, derives local error bounds from comparison solutions, and controls error accumulation via an L² error propagation identity isolating a martingale increment.

Result: Under H_0^{9/2} regularity on noise and deterministic initial data, the maximum L²(T) error at time points is O(h) in L^p(Ω) for every 1≤p<∞ on each fixed finite time interval. Results hold for every damping coefficient λ≥0, including the undamped case λ=0. Numerical experiments confirm first-order convergence.

Conclusion: The implicit midpoint method achieves strong first-order convergence for the periodic stochastic KdV equation, closing a long-standing open question, with exponential integrability and convergence holding even in the undamped case.

Abstract: We establish a strong convergence rate for the implicit midpoint method applied to the periodic stochastic Korteweg-de Vries equation with additive noise, addressing a question that has remained open since the work of Debussche and Printems. To control nonlinear error growth, we obtain exponential integrability estimates for the exact and comparison solutions using the coercivity of a classical KdV invariant. We then derive local error bounds from estimates for the comparison solutions and control error accumulation through an $L^2$ error propagation identity that isolates a martingale increment. Under $H_0^{9/2}$ regularity assumptions on the noise and the deterministic initial data, we prove that, on every fixed finite time interval, the maximum $L^2(\mathbb{T})$ error at the time points is $O(h)$ in $L^p(Ω)$ for every $1\le p<\infty$. Notably, the exponential integrability and the convergence rate hold for every damping coefficient $λ\ge0$, including the undamped case $λ=0$. Numerical experiments confirm the predicted first-order convergence.

</details>


### [56] [Least-Squares Finite Element Methods with Weakly Imposed Boundary Conditions: Norm and Error Equivalences, Over-Penalization, and Fully Discontinuous Discretizations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17678)
*Shun Zhang*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper resolves long-open questions on weakly imposed boundary conditions in first-order system least-squares methods for general (possibly indefinite) second-order elliptic equations, proving norm/error equivalences with the right weights ($h_F^{-1}$ Dirichlet, 1 Neumann) and showing these weights are optimal.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Weakly imposed boundary conditions in first-order system least-squares methods date to Jespersen (1977), but two questions remained open: the correct weights (especially for the Neumann residual, whose natural $H^{-1/2}$ norm suggests $h_F$) and norm/error equivalences, especially for indefinite equations.

Method: For general possibly indefinite second-order elliptic equations with non-homogeneous mixed boundary conditions, the authors use weights $h_F^{-1}$ on the Dirichlet and $1$ on the Neumann residual. The key idea is applying the stability of the boundary value problem to pairs discrete only in their Dirichlet traces, whose $H^{1/2}$ norm is bounded by an inverse inequality via the weighted penalty. They also analyze a fully discontinuous counterpart: a pure penalty method with both jumps weighted by $h_F^{-1}$.

Result: Mesh-independent norm and error equivalences hold for every discrete pair: the error in a mesh-dependent norm equals the square root of the functional plus a Dirichlet data term. The least-squares solution converges optimally in $H(\mathrm{div};\Omega)\times H^{1}(\Omega)$, and the functional is a reliable and efficient error estimator with recovered flux, even for approximations from other methods violating boundary conditions. Weaker weights (including $h_F$ on the Neumann boundary) destroy the norm equivalence, while stronger weights are admissible. For the discontinuous counterpart, error equivalence holds for every discrete pair.

Conclusion: The weights $h_F^{-1}$ (Dirichlet) and $1$ (Neumann) are the right ones: weaker powers break norm equivalence, stronger weights remain admissible. Optimal convergence, reliable/efficient estimation, and flux recovery hold for general indefinite problems; the discontinuous pure penalty method preserves error equivalence even for exact fluxes with non-square-integrable interior-face normal traces.

Abstract: Weakly imposed boundary conditions in first-order system least-squares methods go back to Jespersen (1977), but two questions have remained open for decades: the right weights, especially for the Neumann residual, whose natural norm $H^{-1/2}$ suggests $h_F$, and norm and error equivalences, especially for indefinite equations. We answer both for general, possibly indefinite, second-order elliptic equations with non-homogeneous mixed boundary conditions. First, for the weights $h_F^{-1}$ on the Dirichlet and $1$ on the Neumann residual, we prove norm and error equivalences with mesh-independent constants for every discrete pair: the error in a mesh-dependent norm is equivalent to the square root of the functional plus a Dirichlet data term. The least-squares solution converges optimally in $H(\mathrm{div};Ω)\times H^{1}(Ω)$, and the functional is a reliable and efficient error estimator, with a recovered flux also for approximations from other methods, even ones violating the boundary conditions. The key idea is to apply the stability of the boundary value problem to pairs that are discrete only in their Dirichlet traces, whose $H^{1/2}$ norm an inverse inequality bounds by the weighted penalty. Second, these weights are the right ones: weaker powers, including $h_F$ on the Neumann boundary, destroy the norm equivalence, and stronger weights are admissible, since the penalty multiplies zero at a comparison function meeting the projected data. Third, for the fully discontinuous counterpart, a pure penalty method with both jumps weighted by $h_F^{-1}$, the error equivalence holds for every discrete pair, also for exact fluxes with normal traces not square integrable on interior faces.

</details>


### [57] [Semi-Lagrangian Approximation of Master Equations with Individual Noise on Finite Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17700)
*Jianbo Cui,Tonghe Dang*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: A fully discrete semi-Lagrangian method for vector master equations of MFGs on finite graphs, with interior localization handling boundary degeneracy, achieving first-order accuracy and convergent characteristics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Mean field games with individual noise on finite graphs lead to vector master equations whose transport coefficients degenerate near the open simplex boundary, where no boundary condition is given—making numerical analysis difficult.

Method: Fully discrete semi-Lagrangian scheme discretizing characteristic evolution in time and the population variable on the probability simplex. An interior localization based on exact density characteristics keeps numerical arrivals in compact regions of uniform coefficient control; a closure argument for the coupled numerical recursion yields local solvability and quantitative error bounds.

Result: Local solvability of updates and error bounds on compact subsets of the open simplex; under C^2-regularity and suitable space-time mesh coupling, first-order accuracy; density and value trajectories converge to MFG characteristics at the same rate; in the potential setting the reconstructed density-flux pair is admissible and asymptotically optimal with a quadratic action gap; numerical experiments confirm rates, feedback responses, and graph topology effects.

Conclusion: The proposed semi-Lagrangian method effectively overcomes boundary degeneracy and provides rigorous convergence guarantees and near-optimal planning behavior for MFGs on finite graphs.

Abstract: We develop and analyze a fully discrete semi-Lagrangian method for vector master equations associated with mean field games (MFGs) with individual noise on finite graphs. The method discretizes both the characteristic evolution in time and the population variable on the probability simplex. A central challenge is the degeneracy of the transport coefficients near the boundary of the open simplex, where no boundary condition is prescribed. To overcome this difficulty in the analysis, we introduce an interior localization based on exact density characteristics, which keeps numerical arrivals within compact regions of uniform coefficient control. Combined with a closure argument for the coupled numerical recursion, this localization yields local solvability of the updates and quantitative error bounds on compact subsets of the open simplex. Under additional $C^2$-regularity of the exact solution and suitable space--time mesh coupling, these bounds imply first-order accuracy. Furthermore, the density and value trajectories generated by the numerical master feedback converge to the corresponding MFG characteristics at the same rate. In the potential setting, the associated reconstructed density--flux pair is admissible and asymptotically optimal for the planning problem, with an action gap quadratic in the feedback error. Numerical experiments illustrate the convergence rates, feedback responses to population redistribution, and the effects of graph topology.

</details>


### [58] [POD-accelerated high-order multiscale method with corrected-basis reconstruction for transient multi-continuum problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17758)
*Jiayuan Peng,Hao Dong,Jiale Linghu*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This work develops a POD-accelerated HOMS framework for transient multi-continuum problems, reducing the cost of repeated homogenized solves and high-order reconstruction in long-time, large-scale 3D simulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Multi-continuum models efficiently describe transient flow in fractured and highly heterogeneous porous media, and HOMS methods capture microscopic heterogeneities with local oscillations. However, repeated solution of the coupled homogenized system and repeated high-order reconstruction remain expensive for long-time and large-scale 3D simulations.

Method: A vector-valued POD space is built from snapshots of the coupled homogenized solution, and the resulting block system is projected onto a low-dimensional space. Two recovery strategies are used: (1) reconstruct reduced homogenized fields with full HOMS assembly at each output time, and (2) apply the spatial part of the multiscale recovery operator to retained POD modes offline, then evaluate the online solution via direct linear combinations of corrected bases and time-derivative bases.

Result: An error estimate between the fully discrete homogenized solution and its POD-reduced approximation is established for the coupled system. A recovered POD-HOMS estimate combines HOMS consistency error with POD truncation errors propagated through value, gradient, inter-continuum exchange, second-derivative, and time-derivative recovery terms. Numerical experiments in 2D and 3D show reduced solutions retain accuracy and local oscillatory behavior while substantially lowering computational cost.

Conclusion: The POD-accelerated HOMS framework provides an accurate and efficient approach for transient multi-continuum problems, preserving local oscillatory features of HOMS solutions while significantly reducing computational expense.

Abstract: Multi-continuum models provide an effective macroscopic description of transient flow in fractured and highly heterogeneous porous media by introducing several interacting state variables at each macroscale location. Although high-order multiscale (HOMS) methods reduce the cost of resolving microscopic heterogeneities while recovering local oscillatory features, repeated solution of the coupled homogenized system and repeated high-order reconstruction remain expensive in long-time and large-scale three-dimensional simulations. This work develops a proper orthogonal decomposition (POD) accelerated HOMS framework for transient multi-continuum problems. A vector-valued POD space is constructed from snapshots of the coupled homogenized solution, and the resulting block system is projected onto a low-dimensional space. Two recovery strategies are considered. The first reconstructs the reduced homogenized fields and performs full HOMS assembly at every output time. The second applies the spatial part of the multiscale recovery operator to retained POD modes offline and evaluates the online solution by direct linear combinations of corrected bases and time-derivative bases. An error estimate between the fully discrete homogenized solution and its POD-reduced approximation is established for the coupled system. A recovered POD-HOMS estimate is then obtained by combining the HOMS consistency error with POD truncation errors propagated through value, gradient, inter-continuum exchange, second-derivative, and time-derivative recovery terms. Numerical experiments for two- and three-dimensional heterogeneous configurations show that the reduced solutions retain the accuracy and local oscillatory behavior of the corresponding HOMS solution while substantially decreasing computational cost.

</details>


### [59] [Learning Hamiltonians from Trajectory Data with Kernel Methods](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17835)
*Jianyu Hu,David Martin de Diego,Juan-Pablo Ortega,Miguel Vaquero*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper studies recovering a Hamiltonian from discrete trajectory observations via structure-preserving kernel methods, showing that fixed-step learning targets a scheme-dependent inverse modified Hamiltonian, with finite-sample bounds and a multistride Richardson correction to raise accuracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: When fitting exact-flow data through a symplectic one-step scheme, the fixed-step learning target is generally not the physical Hamiltonian but a scheme-dependent inverse modified Hamiltonian, which must be characterized to understand what is actually learned.

Method: RKHS-based structure-preserving kernel learning; analysis of implicit midpoint and p-implicit symplectic Euler methods; existence of analytic inverse targets; finite-sample reconstruction bounds for random trajectories allowing within-trajectory dependence; error decomposition into estimation, approximation, and integrator-induced components; stability under noise; multistride Richardson correction.

Result: Inverse-target discrepancies from the physical Hamiltonian are second order (implicit midpoint) and first order (p-implicit symplectic Euler) in the observation step; finite-sample bounds derived; stability under noisy observations established; Richardson correction raises the integrator-induced error order without extra trajectories; numerical experiments confirm predicted orders and method applicability.

Conclusion: Structure-preserving kernel Hamiltonian recovery learns a scheme-dependent inverse modified Hamiltonian, whose order can be systematically improved via a multistride Richardson correction, with theoretical guarantees and empirical validation.

Abstract: We study the recovery of a Hamiltonian from discrete trajectory observations using structure-preserving kernel methods. When exact-flow data are fitted through a symplectic one-step scheme, the fixed-step learning target is generally not the physical Hamiltonian itself, but a scheme-dependent inverse modified Hamiltonian. For the implicit midpoint and $p$-implicit symplectic Euler methods, we establish the existence of analytic inverse targets and show that their discrepancies from the physical Hamiltonian are second and first order in the observation step, respectively. Within an RKHS framework, we derive finite-sample reconstruction bounds for random trajectories, allowing dependence among observations from the same trajectory, and separate the error into estimation, approximation, and integrator-induced components. We further establish stability under noisy trajectory observations and develop a multistride Richardson correction that systematically raises the order of the integrator-induced error without requiring additional trajectories. Numerical experiments confirm the predicted scheme-dependent orders, the effect of Richardson correction, and the applicability of the method across several Hamiltonian systems.

</details>


### [60] [Provable Method of Moments for Cryo-EM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17892)
*Amnon Balanov,Tamir Bendory,Dan Edidin,Josh Katz,Shay Kreymer,Nir Sharon*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: The paper shows the first three moments generically determine a 3D volume in cryo-EM from noisy projections with unknown SO(3) rotations, yielding recovery with sample complexity n=ω(σ^6).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Recovering 3D volumes in single-particle cryo-EM from projections at unknown viewing rotations is challenging, especially in high-noise regimes; the paper seeks provable recovery guarantees and sample complexity.

Method: Uses a spherical-shell representation with an explicit shell-separation condition; proves the first three moments generically determine the volume up to the global O(3) ambiguity via a constructive frequency-marching recovery procedure. Validated with numerical experiments under finite sampling and additive noise.

Result: Consistent recovery in the high-noise regime requires sample complexity n=ω(σ^6); experiments confirm the constructive scheme and show direct third-moment optimization can succeed beyond the structural assumptions of the proof.

Conclusion: Three-moment methods give provable, constructive recovery of cryo-EM volumes with ω(σ^6) sample complexity, and practical optimization may outperform the theoretical assumptions.

Abstract: We study the recovery of a three-dimensional volume in single-particle cryo-electron microscopy (cryo-EM) from noisy tomographic projections acquired at unknown viewing rotations, drawn independently and uniformly from $\mathrm{SO}(3)$. We show that, in a spherical-shell representation satisfying an explicit shell-separation condition, the first three moments generically determine the underlying real volume up to its intrinsic global $\mathrm{O}(3)$ ambiguity. The proof is constructive and yields a frequency-marching procedure that successively recovers the volume. Consequently, consistent recovery in the high-noise regime has sample complexity $n=ω(σ^6)$, where $n$ is the number of observations and $σ^2$ is the noise variance. Numerical experiments validate the constructive recovery scheme under finite sampling and additive noise, and indicate that direct third-moment optimization can succeed beyond the structural assumptions required by the constructive proof.

</details>


### [61] [Block diagonal preconditioners for arrow-shaped multiple saddle-point linear systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17940)
*Luca Bergamaschi,Michele Bergamaschi*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: The paper analyzes exact and inexact Schur complement based block diagonal preconditioners for block-arrow structured multiple saddle-point linear systems, characterizing preconditioned eigenvalues via rational function zeros and validating bounds numerically.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Multiple saddle-point linear systems with block-arrow structure arise in applications such as liquid crystal modeling and PDE-constrained optimization, and efficient preconditioners are needed for solving them.

Method: The authors analyze exact and inexact Schur complement based block diagonal preconditioners by characterizing the eigenvalues of the preconditioned matrices in terms of the zeros of specific rational functions.

Result: Numerical results on large test problems from liquid crystal modeling and PDE-constrained optimization validate the obtained eigenvalue bounds.

Conclusion: The proposed eigenvalue analysis provides effective bounds for Schur complement based block diagonal preconditioners, confirmed by large-scale numerical experiments.

Abstract: We consider multiple saddle-point linear systems with block-arrow structure. We analyze exact and inexact Schur complement based block diagonal preconditioner, by characterizing the eigenvalues of the preconditioned matrices in terms of the zeros of specific rational functions. Numerical results onto large tests coming from liquid crystal modeling and PDE-constrained optimization validate the obtained eigenvalue bounds

</details>


### [62] [Space-Time Parallel Direct Numerical Simulation of Rayleigh-Bénard Convection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17942)
*Thomas Saupe,Thibaut Lunet,Mathis Bode,Robert Speck,Sebastian Götschel,Daniel Ruprecht*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper investigates parallel spectral deferred correction (SDC) as a high-order time-stepping method to reduce wall-clock time of direct numerical simulation of Rayleigh-Bénard convection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: RBC is a model for turbulent flow that is challenging to simulate by DNS. Adequate resolution of all relevant length scales requires very fine spatial grids, and stability restrictions can dictate small time step sizes.

Method: The authors apply parallel spectral deferred correction (SDC) as a high-order time-stepping method and evaluate it with two different codes on JUSUF (CPUs) and JUPITER (GPUs).

Result: Performance results show parallel SDC can offer additional concurrency along the temporal axis and larger maximum stable time steps, reducing wall-clock time for simulations over one free-fall time.

Conclusion: Fewer required steps combined with temporal parallelization to mitigate higher per-step cost enables reduced wall-clock time for RBC DNS.

Abstract: Rayleigh-Bénard convection (RBC) is a model for turbulent flow and challenging to simulate by direct numerical simulation (DNS). Adequate resolution of all relevant length scales requires very fine grids in space and stability restrictions can dictate small step sizes. In this paper, we investigate the use of parallel spectral deferred correction (SDC) as a high-order time-stepping method to reduce wall-clock times of direct numerical simulation of RBC. Performance results on JUSUF (CPUs) and JUPITER (GPUs) with two different codes show parallel SDC can offer some concurrency along the temporal axis plus larger maximum stable time steps. Taken together, this allows for a reduction of wall-clock time required for simulations over one free-fall time by having to compute fewer steps and being able to mitigate the higher cost per time step through temporal parallelization.

</details>


### [63] [Numerical simulation of biological capsules based on interface-fitted polygonal meshes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18172)
*Daniele A. Di Pietro,Simon Mendez,Aurelio E. Spadotto*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: A numerical framework simulates biological capsules using iteration-wise fitted polygonal meshes and a Hybrid High Order (HHO) method for the interface-driven Stokes problem, validated on a shear-flow capsule deformation benchmark.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Simulating biological capsules requires resolving the membrane interface accurately, which is difficult with fixed meshes. The authors seek a scheme that adapts to the interface each iteration without adding bulk degrees of freedom.

Method: The framework uses meshes that adapt iteration-wise to the membrane shape. A Hybrid High Order (HHO) method solves a Stokes problem with a source term distributed on the internal/external interface. HHO supports general polygonal meshes, enabling fitted meshes generated by cutting interface-intersected elements and allowing accurate interface discretization without extra bulk DOFs.

Result: A proof-of-concept numerical test on a well-established benchmark—deformation of an elastic capsule immersed in a shear flow—demonstrates the framework.

Conclusion: The proposed HHO-based adaptive-mesh framework is a viable approach for simulating biological capsules, with the shear-flow capsule benchmark as initial validation.

Abstract: In this work we introduce a numerical framework for the simulation of biological capsules based on meshes that adapt iteration-wise to the shape of the membrane. The discretization leverages a Hybrid High Order method (HHO) to solve a Stokes problem with a source term distributed on the interface between the internal and the external phases. The HHO discretization supports meshes composed of general polygons, permitting to use fitted meshes that can be easily generated by cutting the elements intersected by the interface. Moreover, polygonal meshes allow arbitrarily accurate interface discretizations without introducing bulk degrees of freedom. The presentation of the simulation algorithm is completed with a proof-of-concept numerical test on a well-established benchmark: the deformation of an elastic capsule immersed in a shear flow.

</details>


### [64] [Hyperelastic Modeling of Cardiac Tissue Deformation for Data Registration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18207)
*Joyce Ghantous,Yves Coudiere,Michael Leguèbe*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: A biomechanical registration framework estimates the deformation between stretched (optical mapping) and resting (MRI) cardiac tissue configurations by modeling tissue as a nearly incompressible transversely isotropic hyperelastic material and solving an inverse problem for traction amplitude.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Optical mapping and high-resolution MRI of cardiac tissue are acquired in different mechanical configurations (stretched vs. fixed resting state), creating a spatial mismatch that must be corrected to combine functional and structural data.

Method: The tissue is modeled as a nearly incompressible transversely isotropic hyperelastic material. The traction amplitude is identified from optical-map marker positions via an inverse problem, with 2D and 3D simulations.

Result: Two- and three-dimensional simulations yield physically admissible deformations, providing a first proof of concept.

Conclusion: The proposed strategy is validated as a proof of concept for biomechanical registration linking optical mapping and MRI of cardiac tissue.

Abstract: This work proposes a biomechanical registration framework designed to combine functional optical-mapping data with structural high-resolution MRI of cardiac tissue. The two acquisitions are performed in different mechanical configurations: optical mapping is obtained after stretching the sample, whereas MRI is acquired in a fixed resting state. To estimate the deformation between these configurations, the tissue is modeled as a nearly incompressible transversely isotropic hyperelastic material, and the traction amplitude is identified from optical-map marker positions through an inverse problem. Two-and threedimensional simulations show physically admissible deformations and provide a first proof of concept for the proposed strategy.

</details>


### [65] [Low-cost non-iterative Sylvester solvers for dynamical low-rank approximation of reaction-diffusion equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18419)
*Joy Saha,Jun Yang,Peimeng Yin*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: Low-cost non-iterative Sylvester solvers for dynamical low-rank approximation of reaction-diffusion equations, integrated into a BUG-MLFEM scheme, achieving reduced complexity and large speedups.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Dynamical low-rank approximation of reaction-diffusion equations faces a severe computational bottleneck from standard O(m^3+n^3) Sylvester solvers. The goal is to develop low-cost, non-iterative solvers exploiting structural sparsity while preserving theoretical and numerical guarantees.

Method: Mass lumping via Gauss-Lobatto quadrature yields diagonal positive-definite mass matrices, giving a matrix differential equation. The work establishes weighted dynamical low-rank approximation (WDLRA) in the Galerkin MLFEM framework, proves equivalence between the BUG integrator and MLFEM on low-rank subspaces (ensuring discrete well-posedness of implicit BUG steps). Tailored non-iterative Sylvester solvers exploit sparse-by-small structure: banded large matrices enable diagonalization of the small r×r factor matrix, cutting per-step complexity to O((m+n)r^2) and storage to O((m+n)r). For periodic BCs, a rank-2 Sherman-Morrison-Woodbury update handles wrap-around corner entries.

Result: Complexity reduced to O((m+n)r^2) per step and O((m+n)r) storage. Numerical experiments show first-order temporal and (k+1)th-order spatial convergence in the discrete L^2 norm (k = polynomial degree), with significant speedups over existing low-rank integrators.

Conclusion: The proposed BUG-MLFEM with tailored non-iterative Sylvester solvers is well-posed, efficient, and accurate, delivering substantial computational gains over existing low-rank integrators for reaction-diffusion problems under Dirichlet, Neumann, and periodic BCs.

Abstract: In this work, we develop low-cost non-iterative Sylvester solvers for dynamical low-rank approximation of reaction-diffusion equations and incorporate them into a basis-update \& Galerkin mass-lumped finite element method (\mbox{BUG-MLFEM}). By incorporating mass lumping via Gauss-Lobatto quadrature, the spatial discretization yields a matrix differential equation equipped with diagonal positive-definite mass matrices. We establish the weighted dynamical low-rank approximation (WDLRA) within the Galerkin framework of the MLFEM system, proving the theoretical equivalence between the BUG integrator and MLFEM on low-rank subspaces, which guarantees the discrete well-posedness of the implicit BUG steps. To overcome the severe computational bottleneck of standard $O(m^3+n^3)$ Sylvester solvers, we develop tailored, low-cost non-iterative solvers that exploit the inherent sparse-by-small algebraic structure. Under Dirichlet and Neumann boundary conditions (BCs), the large coefficient matrices are banded, enabling efficient diagonalization of the small $r \times r$ factor matrix and reducing the per-step computational complexity to $O((m+n)r^2)$ and storage to $O((m+n)r)$. For periodic BCs, we combine this diagonalization strategy with a rank-2 Sherman-Morrison-Woodbury update to handle wrap-around corner entries at negligible additional cost. Numerical experiments demonstrate first-order temporal and $(k+1)$th-order spatial convergence of the proposed BUG-MLFEM in the discrete $L^2$ norm, where $k$ denotes the polynomial degree of the finite element space, together with significant computational speedups over existing low-rank integrators.

</details>


### [66] [Two-level additive Schwarz preconditioners with GDSW coarse spaces for high-order discretizations in 2D](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18502)
*Tommaso Bevilacqua,Axel Klawonn,Martin Lanser,Adam Wasiak*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: Two-level overlapping Schwarz preconditioners with GDSW and RGDSW coarse spaces are studied for high-order FEM and VEM applied to the 2D Poisson problem, with condition number bounds and scalability up to polynomial degree k=9.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: GDSW coarse spaces are robust but have large coarse problem dimensions; RGDSW offers a smaller coarse space while maintaining robustness. This work investigates and compares both for high-order FEM and VEM.

Method: Two-level overlapping Schwarz preconditioners using GDSW and RGDSW coarse spaces, applied to the Poisson problem in 2D with conforming P_k/Q_k FEM and VEM. A condition number bound is proved with explicit dependence on polynomial degree k.

Result: Condition number bound proved for both preconditioners with explicit k-dependence. Numerical tests on triangular/quadrilateral FEM meshes and polygonal VEM meshes show scalability up to k=9. RGDSW reduces coarse problem dimension by ~3x versus GDSW with competitive iteration counts.

Conclusion: Both GDSW and RGDSW provide scalable preconditioners for high-order FEM and VEM. RGDSW is a competitive alternative, achieving substantial coarse-space dimension reduction while preserving robustness and iteration performance.

Abstract: Two-level overlapping Schwarz preconditioners with Generalized Dryja-Smith-Widlund (GDSW) and Reduced GDSW (RGDSW) coarse spaces for high-order finite element methods (FEM) and virtual element methods (VEM) are investigated and applied to the Poisson problem in 2D. The GDSW coarse space is a well-established robust choice for preconditioners in domain decomposition, while the RGDSW coarse space offers a competitive alternative by reducing the coarse problem dimension while maintaining robustness. On the theoretical side, a condition number bound for both preconditioners is proved for conforming $\mathbb{P}_k$ and $\mathbb{Q}_k$ finite element spaces with explicit dependence on the polynomial degree $k$. Through numerical experiments on structured triangular and quadrilateral meshes for FEM and on polygonal meshes for VEM, it is demonstrated that both GDSW and RGDSW maintain scalability properties across polynomial orders up to $k=9$ for selected degrees. The RGDSW approach achieves a reduction in the coarse problem dimension by a factor of approximately three compared to GDSW while maintaining competitive iteration counts.

</details>


### [67] [A two-level preconditioner for discontinuous virtual element methods with irregular subdomains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18580)
*Juan G. Calvo,César Herrera*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: The paper introduces and analyzes a two-level overlapping additive Schwarz preconditioner for discontinuous Galerkin (DG) discretizations of virtual element spaces on general polygonal meshes for a second-order elliptic problem with jumping coefficients. It proves a condition number bound independent of the number of subdomains and coefficient contrast (under quasi-monotonicity), matching the conforming case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Virtual element methods on general polygonal meshes combined with DG discretizations require robust, scalable solvers. Extending overlapping Schwarz preconditioning theory—well established for conforming methods—to the DG virtual element setting, especially for problems with highly heterogeneous (jumping) coefficients, is nontrivial and needed to handle irregular subdomains from graph partitioners.

Method: A two-level overlapping additive Schwarz preconditioner is developed for a weighted symmetric interior penalty DG formulation of a second-order elliptic problem. Averages carry the coefficient and the penalty is scaled by its harmonic mean per edge. The coarse space uses a virtual, continuous partition of unity with one degree of freedom per subdomain vertex, applicable to irregular subdomains; the discrete harmonic extension can be replaced by a cheaper low-degree polynomial projection.

Result: The condition number is bounded by C(1+log(H/h))(1+H/δ), where H, h, δ are subdomain diameter, mesh size, and overlap; C is independent of the number of subdomains and of coefficient contrast, provided the coefficient is quasi-monotone. This matches the conforming-case bound and permits hanging nodes. An explicit example shows quasi-monotonicity cannot be removed.

Conclusion: Numerical experiments on structured triangular, hexagonal, and Voronoi meshes, with subdomains from uniform partitioning and METIS, confirm the theoretical bounds and robustness of the proposed preconditioner.

Abstract: We introduce and analyze a two-level overlapping additive Schwarz preconditioner for discontinuous Galerkin discretizations of virtual element spaces on general polygonal meshes in two dimensions, for a second-order elliptic problem whose coefficient is constant on each subdomain and may have large jumps across the interface. The discretization is a weighted symmetric interior penalty formulation: the averages carry the coefficient and the penalty is scaled by its harmonic mean across each edge, which keeps the method consistent and makes the constants of the analysis independent of the coefficient. The coarse space is obtained from a virtual, continuous partition of unity with a single degree of freedom per subdomain vertex, and is therefore applicable to the irregular subdomains produced by a graph partitioner; the discrete harmonic extension used to build it may be replaced by a cheaper projection onto polynomials of low degree. We prove that the condition number of the preconditioned system is bounded by $C(1+\log(H/h))(1+H/δ)$, where $H$, $h$ and $δ$ denote the subdomain diameter, the mesh size and the overlap, and where $C$ is independent of the number of subdomains and of the contrast of the coefficient, provided the latter is quasi-monotone. This is the same bound known for the conforming case and the analysis allows for hanging nodes. We also show, by an explicit example, that the quasi-monotonicity assumption cannot be dispensed with. Numerical experiments with structured triangular, hexagonal and Voronoi meshes, and with subdomains obtained both by uniform partitioning and by METIS, confirm the theory.

</details>


### [68] [Fully discrete multi-entropy-stable compact Runge--Kutta discontinuous Galerkin methods for hyperbolic conservation laws](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18837)
*Zheng Sun*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: The paper develops fully discrete multi-entropy-stable high-order compact Runge-Kutta discontinuous Galerkin (cRKDG) methods for hyperbolic conservation laws, proving weak cell entropy inequalities after a full RK step without needing SSP decompositions or stagewise entropy corrections.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard entropy stability analyses for high-order DG schemes often require strong stability preserving decompositions or stagewise entropy corrections, which are restrictive. The authors aim to establish entropy stability directly for compact RKDG methods, including for multiple entropy pairs, and to connect this to convergence toward entropy-satisfying weak solutions.

Method: The approach exploits cell-local intermediate stages of cRKDG, controlling their perturbations via initial in-cell variation. Using a sufficiently dissipative Lax-Friedrichs flux and explicit RK methods with nonnegative output weights, the authors prove a weak cell entropy inequality under a suitable time-step restriction for strongly convex entropy pairs. For a uniformly conditioned family of entropies, common dissipation and time-step conditions yield weak cell entropy stability across all pairs. A cell-average-preserving scaling at each step end enforces strong cell entropy inequalities simultaneously. A Lax-Wendroff-type convergence argument is also established.

Result: The authors prove a weak cell entropy inequality for multidimensional systems after one full high-order RK step, valid for sufficiently dissipative Lax-Friedrichs flux and explicit RK methods with nonnegative output weights under a time-step restriction. They show simultaneous strong cell entropy inequalities via end-of-step scaling, and a Lax-Wendroff-type theorem guaranteeing convergence to a weak solution satisfying all prescribed entropy inequalities. Numerical experiments in 1D and 2D confirm high-order accuracy, benefits of multiple entropy constraints for nonconvex scalar problems, and robust shock performance.

Conclusion: Fully discrete multi-entropy-stable high-order cRKDG methods are established without SSP decomposition or stagewise corrections. The framework supports multiple entropy constraints and guarantees convergence to entropy-satisfying weak solutions, validated by numerical experiments.

Abstract: We develop fully discrete multi-entropy-stable high-order compact Runge--Kutta discontinuous Galerkin (cRKDG) methods for hyperbolic conservation laws. Our main result is a weak cell entropy inequality for multi-dimensional systems after one full high-order Runge--Kutta (RK) time step. The proof exploits the cell-local intermediate stages of cRKDG: their perturbations are controlled by the initial in-cell variation. The analysis does not require a strong stability preserving decomposition or stagewise entropy correction. For a sufficiently dissipative Lax--Friedrichs flux and explicit RK methods with nonnegative output weights, the inequality holds under a suitable time-step restriction for strongly convex entropy pairs. For a prescribed, uniformly conditioned family of such entropies, common dissipation and time-step conditions ensure weak cell entropy stability for all entropy pairs. A single cell-average-preserving scaling at the end of each step then enforces all corresponding strong cell entropy inequalities simultaneously. We also prove a Lax--Wendroff-type theorem: under the stated assumptions, any strongly convergent sequence of numerical solutions converges to a weak solution satisfying all prescribed entropy inequalities. Numerical experiments in one and two dimensions demonstrate high-order accuracy, the benefit of multiple entropy constraints for nonconvex scalar problems, and robust performance on shock problems.

</details>

### [69] [Calabi-Bernstein type theorems for entire solutions of certain nonlinear elliptic equations in the plane](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14319)
*Michael Bildhauer,Martin Fuchs*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper extends the classical Calabi-Bernstein theorem to entire solutions of certain nonlinear elliptic equations in two variables via Calabi duality between minimal graphs and maximal spacelike graphs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to generalize the classical Calabi-Bernstein theorem beyond its original scope to broader classes of entire solutions of nonlinear elliptic equations in two variables.

Method: They employ an appropriate version of the Calabi correspondence, a duality linking minimal graphs in R^3 with maximal spacelike graphs in L^3, reducing the problem to known Bernstein-type results.

Result: Extensions of the Calabi-Bernstein theorem are obtained for entire solutions of certain nonlinear elliptic equations in two variables, derived as consequences of recent Bernstein-type results.

Conclusion: The results demonstrate the effectiveness of the Calabi correspondence in transferring Bernstein-type theorems to new settings.

Abstract: We provide some extensions of the classical Calabi-Bernstein theorem to the case of entire solutions of certain nonlinear elliptic equations in two variables. Using an appropriate version of the Calabi correspondence, which formulates a duality between minimal graphs in $\mathbb{R}^3$ and maximal spacelike graphs in $\mathbb{L}^3$, our results are a consequence of recent Bernstein type results.

</details>


### [70] [The Liouville property for entire local minimizers from the space $BV_{\mathrm{loc}}(\R^2,\R^N)$ of variational integrals with linear growth](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14321)
*Michael Bildhauer,Martin Fuchs*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Bounded entire local minimizers of a relaxed variational integral with strictly convex, linearly growing density from $W^{1,\infty}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}^2,\mathbb{R}^N)$ are constant.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the rigidity/regularity behavior of local minimizers of variational integrals with linear (rather than superlinear) growth in the relaxed setting, where classical regularity theory for superlinear growth does not directly apply.

Method: Analyze functions $u:\mathbb{R}^2\to\mathbb{R}^N$ in $W^{1,\infty}_{loc}$ that locally minimize the relaxed version of a variational integral whose strictly convex density has linear growth; study the consequences of assuming boundedness of entire local minimizers.

Result: Boundedness of entire local minimizers forces them to be constant.

Conclusion: Entire bounded local minimizers of such relaxed linear-growth variational integrals in $\mathbb{R}^2$ exhibit strong rigidity, being necessarily constant.

Abstract: We consider functions $u : \R^2 \rightarrow \R^N$ from the class $\Bloc$ which locally minimize the relaxed version of a variational integral with a strictly convex density being in addition of linear growth. It turns out that the boundedness of entire local minimizers $u$ implies their constancy.

</details>


### [71] [On Dolbeault's conjecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14359)
*Juncheng Wei,Zetian Yan,Zikai Ye*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves sharp improved Sobolev inequalities on the sphere S^n (n≥3) for two symmetry classes of functions, achieving the optimal improvement factor 2^{-2/n}, with equality only for constants, thereby resolving a conjecture of Dolbeault (2021).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Improved Sobolev inequalities with sharp constants are of central interest in analysis; a specific conjecture of Dolbeault (2021) concerning the antipodally even case remained open.

Method: Establish sharp improved Sobolev inequalities on S^n for antipodally even functions and for axially symmetric functions whose critical density has zero barycenter, determining the optimal constants.

Result: In both cases the Dirichlet coefficient improves by the optimal factor 2^{-2/n}, while the L^2 coefficient stays one, and equality holds only for constant functions.

Conclusion: The results provide sharp improved Sobolev inequalities under symmetry assumptions and resolve Dolbeault's conjecture for the even case.

Abstract: We prove sharp improved Sobolev inequalities on $\mathbb S^n$, $n\ge3$, for antipodally even functions and for axially symmetric functions whose critical density $|u|^{2n/(n-2)}$ has zero barycenter. In both cases, the Dirichlet coefficient improves by the optimal factor $2^{-2/n}$, while the coefficient of the $L^2$ term remains one. Equality holds precisely for constant functions. The even case resolves a conjecture of Dolbeault (2021).

</details>


### [72] [Uniqueness and stability of entropy waves among vanishing dissipation limits from the Navier--Stokes--Fourier System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14383)
*Cooper Faile,Ingrid Lacroix-Violet,Alexis F. Vasseur*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves stability and uniqueness of entropy wave solutions (constant velocity and pressure profiles) of the compressible Euler equations within the vanishing dissipation limit class from the Navier-Stokes-Fourier system.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Entropy waves are fundamental solutions of the compressible Euler equations, and understanding their behavior under vanishing dissipation is important for both physical and mathematical reasons.

Method: The proof is based on the a-contraction theory.

Result: When the initial data of a sequence of Navier-Stokes-Fourier solutions converges to the initial data of the entropy wave solution, the solutions converge to the entropy wave itself.

Conclusion: This establishes uniqueness and stability of the entropy wave in the vanishing dissipation limit class.

Abstract: In this paper, we establish the stability of general entropy wave solutions of the compressible Euler equations (that is, profiles with constant velocity and pressure) in the class of vanishing dissipation limits from the Navier--Stokes--Fourier system. If the initial data of our sequence of solutions to the Navier--Stokes--Fourier system converges to the initial data of the entropy wave solution, we further have convergence of the solutions to the entropy wave. This gives uniqueness and stability of the entropy wave in this class. The proof is based on the $a$-contraction theory.

</details>


### [73] [Persistence of conormal analyticity for the Euler equations in the half-space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14408)
*Benjamin Ingimarson,Igor Kukavica,Marco Sammartino*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves persistence of conormal analyticity for the 3D incompressible Euler equations in the half-space, with a lower bound on the analyticity radius depending on the $H^r$ norm and initial data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding regularity and analyticity persistence for the 3D incompressible Euler equations in domains with boundaries is important, particularly in the half-space where conormal analysis is natural.

Method: The authors work through the $H^r$ norm ($r \geq 4$) and use two conormal operators: $Z_k = \phi^k \partial_3^k$ and $L^k = (\phi\partial_3)^k$, establishing persistence in the spaces $X_\tau^{\rm co}$ and $A_\tau^{\rm co}$, using explicit identities relating the two operators.

Result: Persistence of conormal analyticity is established in both $X_\tau^{\rm co}$ and $A_\tau^{\rm co}$, with a lower bound on the radius of conormal analyticity depending on $\|u\|_{H^r}$ and initial data. The two norms are shown to be equivalent up to a small loss in radius.

Conclusion: Conormal analyticity persists for the 3D Euler equations in the half-space, and the two conormal-based norms are equivalent up to a small radius loss.

Abstract: We prove the persistence of conormal analyticity through the $H^r$ norm, with $r \geq 4$, for the 3D incompressible Euler equations in the half-space $Ω= \mathbb{R}_+^3$. We also establish a lower bound on the radius of conormal analyticity which depends on $\|u\|_{H^r}$ and initial data. In particular, persistence is established in both of the spaces $X_τ^{\rm co}$ and $A_τ^{\rm co}$, the former being based on the conormal operator $Z_k = φ^k \partial_3^k$ and the latter being based on $L^k = (φ\partial_3)^k$. Finally, we show that the norms $\|\cdot \|_{X_τ^{\rm co}}$ and $\|\cdot \|_{A_τ^{\rm co}}$ are equivalent up to a small loss in radius, the proof of which uses explicit identities relating the two conormal operators.

</details>


### [74] [Global existence and convergence of a density heat flow for strictly subcritical Liouville systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14455)
*Zetao Cheng,Haoyu Li,Woongbae Park,Lei Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: A density heat flow for normalized Liouville systems on closed Riemannian surfaces globally exists and converges to stationary solutions under a strict subcriticality condition, without any invertibility, irreducibility, or sign assumptions on the coupling matrix.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existence results for Liouville systems typically require invertibility, irreducibility, or inverse-matrix sign hypotheses on the coupling matrix. This work aims to remove these restrictive assumptions by using a parabolic density heat flow approach.

Method: Study a density heat flow associated with normalized Liouville systems. The proof employs entropy coercivity and a gradient inequality in the density variables, valid for symmetric entrywise-nonnegative coupling matrices without invertibility or irreducibility.

Result: Under strict subcriticality conditions 8π Σ_{i∈J} ρ_i > Σ_{i,j∈J} a_{ij} ρ_i ρ_j for every nonempty component set J, every smooth positive initial density with masses ρ_i>0 yields a unique global smooth positive solution converging in all C^m norms to a stationary density. Algebraic or exponential convergence rates are also obtained.

Conclusion: The flow establishes existence of solutions for subcritical Liouville systems, and the limiting potentials solve the stationary Liouville system. The approach yields convergence rates and relaxes standard structural assumptions on the coupling matrix.

Abstract: We study a density heat flow associated with normalized Liouville systems on a closed connected Riemannian surface, and we show that this flow leads to the existence of solutions for the corresponding subcritical Liouville systems. The coupling matrix is symmetric and entrywise nonnegative, with no invertibility, irreducibility or inverse-matrix sign assumption. Under the strict subcriticality conditions $8π\sum_{i\in J}ρ_i>\sum_{i,j\in J}a_{ij}ρ_iρ_j$ for every nonempty set of components $J$, every smooth positive initial density of masses $ρ_i>0$ generates a unique global smooth positive solution converging in every $C^m$ norm to a stationary density. The limiting potentials solve the stationary Liouville system. Our proof uses entropy coercivity and a gradient inequality in the density variables, which also yields algebraic or exponential convergence rates.

</details>


### [75] [Domain dependence of Sobolev-curl constants and structure of critical curl-curl solutions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14529)
*Yong Liu,Jun Wang,Xuan Wang,Wen Yang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Studies intrinsic critical Sobolev-curl constants on bounded domains and finite-energy solutions of the critical curl-curl equation in R^3, proving bounds, constructing explicit examples, and addressing several open problems of Mederski-Szulkin.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivated by open problems of Mederski and Szulkin on intrinsic critical curl constants and finite-energy solutions to the critical curl-curl equation.

Method: For 1<p<3, uses an aligned test field whose optimal curl-free correction vanishes to bound the intrinsic p-curl constant on (0,a)×D by a^{p/3} times a Sobolev quotient on D; for p=2 constructs explicit convex and nonconvex domains, and derives an implicit Newtonian representation of finite-energy solutions.

Result: Intrinsic critical p-curl constants vanish along thin convex boxes; explicit domains with intrinsic constant below the sharp scalar Sobolev constant exist, resolving the domain-existence question (Problem 5) and disproving a stated convex lower bound; two special solution forms are excluded.

Conclusion: The results partially answer the Mederski-Szulkin problems but leave open an explicit ground state and unconditional ground-state decay rate (Problem 2).

Abstract: This paper investigates intrinsic critical Sobolev-curl constants on bounded domains and the structure of finite-energy solutions to the critical curl-curl equation in R^3. First, for 1< p < 3, we prove that the intrinsic critical p-curl constant on (0, a)\times D is bounded above by a^{p/3} times a Sobolev quotient on D. The proof uses an aligned test field whose optimal curl-free correction vanishes. Consequently, these constants tend to zero along thin convex boxes. For p = 2, we construct explicit convex and nonconvex domains with intrinsic constant below the sharp scalar Sobolev constant. The nonconvex example gives an affirmative answer to the domain-existence question in Problem 5 of Mederski and Szulkin(Arch. Ration. Mech. Anal. 241 (2021), 1815-1842), while the convex example disproves the lower bound stated there for convex domains. Concerning their Problem 2, we obtain an implicit Newtonian representation of finite-energy solutions and exclude two special forms of solutions. These structural results do not yet provide an explicit ground state or an unconditional ground-state decay rate as asked in Problem 2.

</details>


### [76] [Global Well-Posedness and Persistence of H2 Regularity for the Three-Dimensional Primitive Equations with Horizontal and Topography-Adapted Thermal Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14555)
*Qimin Wang,Mohammed Ziane*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves global well-posedness and H^2 regularity persistence for 3D primitive equations with full viscosity and horizontal thermal diffusion on bounded domains, including flat-bottom cylinders and variable-depth domains via a topography-adapted diffusion operator.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Establish strong well-posedness and higher regularity for the 3D primitive equations in bounded domains, extending analysis to variable-depth topography where Cartesian horizontal Laplacian is inadequate.

Method: Combines fractional hydrostatic Stokes regularity with anisotropic estimates of vertical transport; introduces a diffusion operator adapted to topography compatible with layer coordinates z/h(x,y)=constant, formulated in a weighted setting.

Result: Global well-posedness for admissible H^1 data and persistence of H^2 regularity on flat-bottom cylinders; global strong solutions for admissible H^1 data on variable-depth domains, with H^2 persistence under ∂_νh=0 on the boundary.

Conclusion: The primitive equations are globally well-posed with higher regularity in bounded domains, and the topography-adapted operator generalizes the horizontal Laplacian, enabling strong solutions on variable-depth domains.

Abstract: We study the three-dimensional primitive equations with full viscosity and horizontal thermal diffusion in bounded domains. We establish global well-posedness for admissible $H^1$ initial data and persistence of $H^2$ regularity on the flat-bottom cylinder. The higher regularity analysis combines fractional hydrostatic Stokes regularity with anisotropic estimates of the vertical transport. We also consider variable-depth domains with positive $h\in C^3(\overline M)$ and a diffusion operator adapted to the topography compatible with layers $z/h(x,y)=\mathrm{constant}$. This operator reduces to $Δ_H$ at constant depth and generally differs from the Cartesian horizontal Laplacian over a varying bottom. Its weighted formulation yields global strong solutions for admissible $H^1$ data. Persistence of $H^2$ regularity is proved under the additional assumption $\partial_νh=0$ on $\partial M$, where $ν$ is the outward horizontal unit normal.

</details>


### [77] [Spectral-Edge Continuation, Dimensional Crossover, and Endpoint Stability for Partially Confined Nonlinear Schrödinger Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14585)
*Xuexiu Zhong*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies global geometry and endpoint dynamics of positive solutions to a Schrödinger-type equation with a trapping potential |y|^2 and frequency λ on R^d×R^m. It constructs a connected branch of centered positive solutions over λ∈(-d,∞), analyzes both endpoints via anisotropic rescaling (λ→-d^+) and isotropic blow-up (λ→∞), and derives stability, uniqueness, Morse indices, and normalized standing wave results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The equation loses coercivity at λ=-d, which prevents standard variational/bifurcation approaches. The goal is to understand the global geometry, endpoint behavior, and stability of positive solutions despite this degeneracy, and to connect these to normalized standing wave existence and multiplicity.

Method: The proof combines Gaussian–exponential localization on compact frequency strips, Lyapunov–Schmidt reduction, compact spectral perturbation, and Leray–Schauder continuation. It uses anisotropic rescaling at the lower edge and isotropic blow-up at the upper edge, plus a slope criterion for orbital stability under Hamiltonian-flow hypotheses.

Result: A connected component of centered positive solutions with frequency projection (-d,∞) is constructed. At λ+d→0^+, anisotropic rescaling selects the lowest confined harmonic mode coupled to an m-dimensional scalar ground state; as λ→∞, isotropic blow-up yields the N-dimensional scalar ground state, revealing a dimensional crossover. Uniqueness, differentiability, Morse indices, symmetry kernels, and sharp mass-slope asymptotics are established. The slope criterion classifies orbital stability/instability away from mass-critical powers, and the mass map yields existence, nonexistence, and multiplicity for normalized standing waves involving both critical exponents 2+4/m and 2+4/N.

Conclusion: The centered branch of positive solutions is globally characterized with sharp endpoint profiles and mass laws valid for all centered positive solutions, not just along the branch. The results classify stability and provide normalized standing wave existence, nonexistence, and multiplicity, highlighting the roles of the two critical exponents 2+4/m and 2+4/N.

Abstract: We study the global geometry and endpoint dynamics of positive solutions of $-Δu+|y|^2u+λu=g(u)$, $(y,z)\in\mathbb R^d\times\mathbb R^m$, $m=N-d$. Despite the loss of coercivity at $λ=-d$, we construct a connected component of centered positive solutions with frequency projection $(-d,\infty)$, assuming subcritical asymptotics of $g$ at zero and infinity, a zero-mass Liouville condition, and a strict Hamiltonian sign condition. At the lower edge, $λ+d\rightarrow 0^+$, anisotropic rescaling selects the lowest confined harmonic mode coupled to an $m$-dimensional scalar ground state. As $λ\rightarrow\infty$, isotropic blow-up yields the $N$-dimensional scalar ground state, revealing a dimensional crossover. The endpoint profiles and mass laws hold for every sequence of centered positive solutions, not merely along this component. The proof combines Gaussian--exponential localization on compact frequency strips, Lyapunov--Schmidt reduction, compact spectral perturbation, and Leray--Schauder continuation. At both ends, we establish uniqueness and differentiability of the centered branch, identify the Morse indices and symmetry kernels of the real and imaginary linearizations in the full energy space, and derive sharp mass-slope asymptotics. Under standard Hamiltonian-flow hypotheses, the resulting slope criterion classifies orbital stability and instability away from the mass-critical powers. The mass map on this component, combined with endpoint control on the full centered solution set, yields existence, nonexistence, and multiplicity for normalized standing waves, revealing the roles of both critical exponents $2+4/m$ and $2+4/N$.

</details>


### [78] [The Strauss conjecture for the wave equation with critical damping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14610)
*Elliot Adamson,Jason Metcalfe,Chengbo Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper extends the Strauss conjecture to damped wave equations with a critically-scaled damping coefficient. It shows that for small constant damping multiple a, the critical power becomes p_c(3+a), generalizing the classical 3D result.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Strauss conjecture, concerning the optimal power range for global solutions of power-type nonlinear wave equations with small initial data, is well understood in n dimensions (e.g., p_c(3)=1+sqrt(2) in 3D). However, the analogous problem for damped wave equations with a damping coefficient at the critical scaling remains open.

Method: The problem is studied for damped wave equations where the damping coefficient is at the critical scaling. Letting a denote the constant multiple applied to the damping term, the authors analyze the small-data global existence threshold in the regime where a is sufficiently small.

Result: It is shown that the corresponding critical power is p_c(3+a), provided the damping multiple a is sufficiently small.

Conclusion: The Strauss conjecture generalizes to critically damped wave equations, with the effective critical power shifting from p_c(3) to p_c(3+a) for small a, revealing how critical scaling damping effectively increases the spatial dimension.

Abstract: The Strauss conjecture concerns wave equations with power-type nonlinearities and seeks the optimal range of powers that admit global solutions for all sufficiently nice and sufficiently small initial data. In three spatial dimensions, it is well known that small data global existence is available provided that the power $p>p_c(3)=1+\sqrt{2}$, where $p_c(n)$ denotes the critical power in $n$-spatial dimensions. In the current article, the analogous problem is considered for damped wave equations where the damping coefficient is at the critical scaling. When $a$ denotes the constant multiple applied to the damping term, it is shown that the corresponding critical power is $p_c(3+a)$ provided that $a$ is sufficiently small.

</details>


### [79] [The Strauss conjecture for the wave equation with scattering damping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14614)
*Elliot Adamson,Jason Metcalfe,Chengbo Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes the existence part of the Strauss conjecture for wave equations with spatially dependent scattering damping, where the damping is too weak to change the critical Strauss exponent.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Recent studies of the Strauss conjecture rely on weighted Strichartz estimates; the authors aim to develop an alternative approach for the damped setting where the damping does not alter the critical Strauss exponent.

Method: An approach based only on integrated local energy estimates, specifically those with both $r^p$ and ghost weights, combined with space-time Klainerman-Sobolev estimates to provide the necessary decay.

Result: The existence portion of the Strauss conjecture is established for wave equations with spatially dependent scattering damping; the technique also applies to the free equation.

Conclusion: The Strauss conjecture's existence part holds in the subcritical (non-shifted) damping setting, with a self-contained local-energy-based method; a follow-up will treat critically decaying damping coefficients where the Strauss exponent shifts.

Abstract: The focus of this article is to establish the existence portion of the Strauss conjecture for wave equations with spatially dependent scattering damping. In the current setting, the damping is not sufficiently strong to alter the critical Strauss exponent. In a subsequent study, the authors will consider the case of damping coefficients with critical decay where a shift of the Strauss exponent is expected. While several recent studies of the Strauss conjecture have relied upon weighted Strichartz estimates, here, instead, an approach that is based only on integrated local energy estimates, in particular those with both $r^p$ and ghost weights, is developed. This technique also applies to the problem for the free equation. Space-time Klainerman-Sobolev estimates provide the necessary decay to close the argument.

</details>


### [80] [Liouville theorems for stable solutions to the mean curvature equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14652)
*Fanheng Xu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes Liouville-type results for stable solutions of the mean curvature equation, extending known theorems from the semilinear Lane-Emden equation to this quasilinear setting in three settings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Liouville-type theorems for stable solutions are well-established for the semilinear Lane-Emden equation, but analogous results for the mean curvature equation were lacking. This paper aims to fill that gap.

Method: Proofs rely on a gradient estimate for stable solutions, an integrability result, conservation laws and Pohozaev-type identities, and a unique continuation argument.

Result: (1) Globally stable solutions in R^n: u≡0 if 1<p<p_JL (Joseph-Lundgren exponent). (2) Solutions stable outside a compact set in R^n: u≡0 if n>2 and p=p_S (Sobolev critical exponent). (3) Dirichlet problem on connected unbounded domains with smooth boundary satisfying an epigraph-type geometric condition: u≡0 if n>2, 1<p≤p_S, and stable outside a compact set.

Conclusion: The results extend Liouville-type theorems for stable solutions from the semilinear Lane-Emden equation to the mean curvature equation.

Abstract: We study stable solutions of the mean curvature equation $$ -\operatorname{div}\left(\frac{\nabla u}{\sqrt{1+|\nabla u|^2}}\right) =|u|^{p-1}u $$ in domains of $\mathbb R^n$. We establish Liouville-type results in three settings. First, for globally stable solutions in $\mathbb R^n$, we prove that $u\equiv0$ if $1<p<p_{JL}$, where $p_{JL}$ is the Joseph--Lundgren exponent. Second, for solutions in $\mathbb R^n$ that are stable outside a compact set, we show that if $n>2$ and $p=p_S$, where $p_S$ is the Sobolev critical exponent, then $u\equiv0$. Third, for solutions of the Dirichlet problem on connected unbounded domains with smooth boundary satisfying an epigraph-type geometric condition, we prove that if $n>2$, $1<p\le p_S$, and the solution is stable outside a compact set, then $u\equiv0$. The proofs are based on a gradient estimate for stable solutions, an integrability result, conservation laws and Pohozaev-type identities, and a unique continuation argument. These results extend Liouville-type theorems for stable solutions from the semilinear Lane--Emden equation to the mean curvature equation.

</details>


### [81] [Sharp decay estimates for subsolutions of fully nonlinear equations in thin domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14667)
*John Fabricius,Niklas L. P. Lundström*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper reduces the derivation of pointwise upper decay estimates for viscosity subsolutions of fully nonlinear PDEs in thin domains to solving a one-dimensional differential inequality, yielding sharp estimates that explicitly link transverse domain geometry with equation ellipticity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Establishing pointwise upper decay estimates for viscosity subsolutions of fully nonlinear PDEs in thin domains is difficult, and the interplay between the transverse geometry of the domain and the ellipticity of the equation is not well understood.

Method: The authors reduce the decay estimate problem to solving a one-dimensional differential inequality. The framework allows ellipticity to degenerate at infinity and handles nonhomogeneous equations with lower-order terms.

Result: The resulting estimates explicitly capture the interaction between transverse geometry and ellipticity and are shown to be sharp. As an application, decay estimates for p-subharmonic functions are obtained when 1 < p ≤ 2.

Conclusion: The paper provides a general, sharp framework for pointwise decay estimates in thin domains for fully nonlinear PDEs, with applications to p-subharmonic functions.

Abstract: We reduce the problem of establishing pointwise upper decay estimates in thin domains for viscosity subsolutions of fully nonlinear partial differential equations to solving a one-dimensional differential inequality. The resulting estimates make explicit the interaction between the transverse geometry of the domain and the ellipticity of the equation, and are shown to be sharp. Our framework allows the ellipticity to degenerate at infinity and accommodates nonhomogeneous equations with lower-order terms. As an application, we obtain decay estimates for $p$-subharmonic functions when $1<p\leq 2$

</details>


### [82] [Unique Determination of the Lamé Parameters in Three Dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14741)
*Huaian Diao,Umidjon Durdiev*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The full Dirichlet-to-Neumann map for static isotropic elasticity uniquely determines both smooth Lamé parameters in a bounded smooth strongly convex domain of R^3, with no smallness, symmetry, separability, or analyticity assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Establish uniqueness (injectivity) for the elastic Dirichlet-to-Neumann map, recovering both Lamé parameters from boundary measurements under minimal geometric hypotheses.

Method: Exact four-component Laplace-type reduction; use physical elastic solutions to obtain decisive identities. The reduced system's characteristic quotient has rank three, while the leading physical adjoint lift supplies only two directions; the missing polarization is built from the first nonvanishing correction of a reduced amplitude in the kernel of that lift. With fixed primal and adjoint quotient frames, construct complex geometrical optics solutions yielding a complete family of holomorphically weighted 3x3 matrix moments via the physical Betti identity. Cauchy-Plemelj factorization turns these moments into an invertible relative transport normalized in the common exterior; holomorphic dependence on the projective null direction plus transport rigidity then pin down a material ratio and the shear modulus.

Result: Uniqueness holds: the DN map determines both Lamé parameters in bounded smooth strongly convex 3D domains, without smallness, symmetry, separability, or analyticity.

Conclusion: The full elastic Dirichlet-to-Neumann map uniquely recovers the two smooth Lamé parameters via a rank-sensitive CGO construction and transport rigidity argument.

Abstract: We prove that the full Dirichlet-to-Neumann map for static isotropic elasticity uniquely determines both smooth Lamé parameters in a bounded smooth domain of \(\mathbb R^3\) under strong convexity. The proof uses an exact four-component Laplace-type reduction, but its decisive identities are obtained from physical elastic solutions. The characteristic quotient of the reduced system has rank three, whereas the leading physical adjoint lift supplies only two directions. We construct the missing polarization from the first nonvanishing correction of a reduced amplitude in the kernel of that lift. Paired with fixed primal and adjoint quotient frames, the resulting complex geometrical optics solutions yield a complete family of holomorphically weighted \(3\times3\) matrix moments through the physical Betti identity. Cauchy--Plemelj factorization converts these moments into an invertible relative transport normalized in the common exterior. Holomorphic dependence on the projective null direction and transport rigidity then determine a material ratio and the shear modulus, hence both Lamé parameters. No smallness, symmetry, separability, or analyticity hypothesis is imposed.

</details>


### [83] [Nontrivial domains for the Bernoulli's overdetermined problem with small constant vorticity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14742)
*Yong Zhang,Jialong Zhu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper constructs nontrivial solutions to Bernoulli's overdetermined problem in bounded ring-shaped domains via the implicit function theorem, and in higher dimensions constructs periodic-radial unbounded domains via bifurcation analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Bernoulli's overdetermined problem with a fixed outer boundary and a free inner boundary is challenging; the paper aims to establish existence of nontrivial solutions and extend the study to unbounded domains.

Method: The implicit function theorem is applied to find nontrivial solutions for a bounded ring-shaped domain Ω\\D with small constant γ, where the Bernoulli value Q is determined by construction. In dimension N=n+1 (n≥2), bifurcation analysis is used to construct an unbounded domain that is periodic in one variable and radial in the others.

Result: Nontrivial solutions exist for the Bernoulli overdetermined problem in bounded ring-shaped domains with the Bernoulli value Q determined by the construction. In N=n+1 (n≥2), a nontrivial unbounded domain admitting a solution is constructed, periodic in one variable and radial in the remaining variables.

Conclusion: The implicit function theorem yields nontrivial solutions for the bounded case, while bifurcation analysis provides periodic-radial unbounded domains, extending existence results for Bernoulli's overdetermined problem.

Abstract: In this paper, we first apply the implicit function theorem to find nontrivial solutions to the following Bernoulli's overdetermined problem \begin{equation} -Δu=γ\,\, \text{in}\,\,Ω\setminus\overline{D}, \,\, u=1,\,\,\partial_\mathbf{n} u=Q\,\,\text{on}\,\,\partial D, \,\,u=0\,\,\text{on}\,\,\partialΩ\nonumber \end{equation} for a fixed boundary $\partialΩ$ and a free boundary $\partial D$ in $\mathbb{R}^{N}$ with $N\geq2$, where $γ$ is a sufficiently small constant, the Bernoulli value $Q$ is determined by the construction, and $Ω\setminus\overline{D}$ is a bounded ring-shaped domain. In dimension $N=n+1$ with $n\geq2$, we further study the existence of a nontrivial unbounded domain $Ω\setminus\overline{D}$ in $\mathbb{R}^N$ such that the Bernoulli's overdetermined problem admits a solution. This unbounded domain we construct is periodic in one variable and radial in the other variables by bifurcation analysis.

</details>


### [84] [Generic nondegeneracy of constant mean curvature radial graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14755)
*Alessandro Iacopetti,Filomena Pacella*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies radial constant-mean-curvature graphs over a domain on the sphere with orthogonal (Neumann) boundary conditions, showing that generically (in a residual set of domains) all solutions are nondegenerate, hence the bounded-norm solution set is finite.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding the structure of the solution set for constant mean curvature radial graphs with free/orthogonal boundary on the cone spanned by a spherical domain, and identifying when solutions are nondegenerate.

Method: The problem is reduced to a quasilinear elliptic equation on D with homogeneous Neumann boundary conditions. After pulling the equation back to a fixed reference domain via perturbations parametrized by C^{3,α}-diffeomorphisms, the authors apply a classical transversality (Sard-Smale type) theorem.

Result: In the class of domains where N-1 is not in the Neumann spectrum of the Laplace-Beltrami operator, there is a residual set of domains for which every solution is nondegenerate. Consequently, for every such domain and every R>0, the set of solutions with C^{2,α}-norm bounded by R is finite.

Conclusion: Generic nondegeneracy of solutions holds for a residual set of domains, implying finiteness of bounded solutions and revealing the solution-set structure.

Abstract: Given a smooth domain $D\subset\mathbb S^{N-1}$, $N\geq 3$, and a positive constant $H$, we study radial graphs over $D$ with constant mean curvature $H$ which meet orthogonally the boundary of the cone spanned by $D$. The corresponding problem is given by a quasilinear elliptic equation on $D$ with homogeneous Neumann boundary condition.
  We investigate the structure of the solution set under smooth perturbations of the domain which are parametrized by an open set of $C^{3,α}$-diffeomorphisms of the sphere. After pulling the equation back to a fixed reference domain, we apply a classical transversality theorem and prove that, in the class of domains for which $N-1$ does not belong to the Neumann spectrum of the Laplace-Beltrami operator, there is a residual set for which every solution is nondegenerate. A consequence of our result is that, for every such domain and every $R>0$, the set of solutions whose $C^{2,α}$-norm is bounded by $R$ is finite.

</details>


### [85] [Global Well-Posedness and Uniform Long-Time Control for a Class of Periodic NLS-Dysthe Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14786)
*Andrea Belloni,Filippo Giuliani*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes local and global well-posedness for periodic NLS-Dysthe (higher-order NLS) equations, including a long-time uniform control result without smallness assumptions via conservation laws and mKdV flow invariance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The NLS-Dysthe (HNLS) equations arise as higher-order modulation models for dispersive PDEs like the Klein-Gordon and Serre-Green-Naghdi equations modeling shallow water waves.
