The goal is to develop a well-posedness theory for these periodic equations, avoiding restrictive non-resonance conditions from prior work (Takaoka) and handling the long-time modulation regime.

Method: The authors study $i\partial_t u+a\partial_x^2u+b|u|^2u+i\varepsilon(c\partial_x^3u+d|u|^2\partial_xu+eu^2\partial_x\overline{u})=0$ on $\mathbb T$ with $abc\neq0$, $0<\varepsilon\ll1$. They prove local well-posedness in $H^s(\mathbb T)$ for $s>3/2$ for arbitrary admissible coefficients, and an $H^1$ continuation criterion for $s\ge2$. A lower-order conservation law (when $e\neq0$) gives uniform $H^1$ control. For long-time control, they exploit cubic NLS conservation laws and their invariance under the commuting complex mKdV flow.

Result: Local well-posedness in $H^s(\mathbb T)$ for $s>3/2$ (arbitrary admissible coefficients); an $H^1$ continuation criterion for $s\ge2$; global well-posedness for $s\ge2$ when $e\neq0$ (no non-resonance condition needed). For long-time control: uniform $H^s$ bounds on intervals of order $\varepsilon^{-1}$ for integer $s\ge2$; for non-integer real $s\ge2$, maximal $H^s$ solution exists on the same time scale with uniform control at lower regularity, losing less than one derivative.

Conclusion: The paper provides a robust well-posedness theory for periodic NLS-Dysthe equations, removing the non-resonance condition and establishing global well-posedness under the natural condition $e\neq0$. It further achieves uniform long-time control without smallness assumptions, relevant to the modulation setting, via conservation laws and mKdV flow invariance.

Abstract: We study a class of periodic NLS-Dysthe (NLS-Dy) equations, also referred to as higher-order nonlinear Schrödinger equations (HNLS), of the form $i\partial_t u+a\partial_x^2u+b|u|^2u+i\varepsilon(c\partial_x^3u+d|u|^2\partial_xu+eu^2\partial_x\overline{u})=0$ with $ x\in\mathbb T$, $a,b,c,d,e\in\mathbb R$, $abc\neq0$, and $\varepsilon>0$. For $0<\varepsilon\ll1$, equations of this type arise as higher-order modulation models for some nonlinear dispersive PDEs, including the Klein-Gordon equation and the Serre-Green-Naghdi equation modeling the evolution of nonlinear shallow water waves.
  We prove local well-posedness in $H^s(\mathbb T)$ for $s>3/2$ for arbitrary admissible coefficients, and establish an $H^1$ continuation criterion for $s\ge2$. In the parameter regime $e\neq0$, a lower-order conservation law provides uniform $H^1$ control and, together with the continuation criterion, yields global well-posedness for $s\ge2$. This result does not require the non-resonance condition appearing in Takaoka's periodic theory, while the condition $e\neq0$ naturally arises in the modulation setting considered here.
  Finally, motivated by the long-time regime relevant for modulation, we establish uniform long-time control without imposing a smallness assumption on the initial datum. Using the conservation laws of cubic NLS and their invariance under the commuting complex mKdV flow, we obtain uniform $H^s$ bounds on time intervals of order $\varepsilon^{-1}$ for integer $s\ge2$. For non-integer real $s\ge2$, the maximal $H^s$ solution exists on the same time scale, while uniform control is obtained at a lower regularity, with a loss of less than one derivative.

</details>


### [86] [The CR Nirenberg Problem, Part I: Blow-up Analysis and Compactness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14797)
*Jiechen Qiang,Zhongwei Tang,Heming Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves a priori estimates and compactness for positive solutions of the CR Nirenberg problem under weighted flatness conditions, using blow-up analysis, Pohozaev identities, and recent classification of entire solutions to the critical CR Yamabe equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The CR Nirenberg problem concerns prescribing the Webster curvature on CR manifolds. A priori estimates and compactness are essential for understanding solution spaces and are prerequisites for existence and degree-theoretic results.

Method: The proofs combine refined blow-up analysis, Pohozaev identities, and the complete classification of positive entire solutions to the critical CR Yamabe equation obtained by Li--Liu--Ma and Li.

Result: A priori estimates and compactness results are established for positive solutions of the CR Nirenberg problem under natural weighted flatness conditions.

Conclusion: The compactness framework provides the foundation for existence and degree results, which are established in the companion paper (Part II).

Abstract: We establish a priori estimates and compactness results for positive solutions of the CR Nirenberg problem under natural weighted flatness conditions. The proofs combine refined blow-up analysis, Pohozaev identities, and the complete classification of positive entire solutions to the critical CR Yamabe equation recently obtained by Li--Liu--Ma \cite{LLM2026} and Li \cite{L2026}. Existence and degree results are established in our subsequent paper \cite{QTWPartII}.

</details>


### [87] [Uniqueness and stability for inverse potential scattering with multiple wavenumbers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14978)
*Dong Qiu,Xiang Xu,Yue Zhao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies inverse scattering for the 3D Schrödinger equation, determining a real-valued potential from multi-wavenumber near-field and far-field data, establishing local and global increasing stability estimates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The inverse scattering problem of recovering a potential for the 3D Schrödinger equation from multi-wavenumber data is challenging; the goal is to establish stronger (increasing) stability where the reconstruction becomes more stable as the measured wavenumber increases.

Method: The analysis combines analytic continuation, weighted resolvent estimates, and half-ray identities whose differences at chord endpoints cancel the unknown exterior contribution.

Result: Under suitable a priori bounds, local increasing stability is obtained from near-field Dirichlet data on a fixed sphere for polynomially decaying potentials, and global increasing stability from full far-field amplitudes for exponentially decaying potentials. Stability estimates consist of a noise term plus a remainder that decreases as the upper measured wavenumber grows. Global uniqueness from exact near-field data is also obtained under weaker regularity assumptions.

Conclusion: The paper provides local and global increasing stability estimates and global uniqueness for the 3D Schrödinger inverse scattering problem, showing that reconstruction improves with increasing wavenumber.

Abstract: This paper is concerned with the inverse scattering problem of determining a real-valued potential for the three-dimensional Schrödinger equation from multi-wavenumber data. Under suitable a priori bounds, we establish local increasing stability from near-field Dirichlet data on a fixed sphere for polynomially decaying potentials, and global increasing stability from full far-field amplitudes for exponentially decaying potentials. The stability estimates consist of a noise term and a remainder term, with the latter decreasing as the upper measured wavenumber increases. We also obtain global uniqueness from exact near-field data under weaker regularity assumptions. The analysis combines analytic continuation, weighted resolvent estimates, and half-ray identities whose differences at chord endpoints cancel the unknown exterior contribution.

</details>


### [88] [Stability in the determination of the coefficients for coupled relativistic Schrödinger system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15013)
*Nitesh Kumar,Parveen Kumar,Tanmay Sarkar*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes stability estimates for identifying coefficients in a coupled relativistic Schrödinger system on a bounded domain of R^{1+n} (n ≥ 3), using partial Neumann boundary data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Coefficient identification in coupled relativistic Schrödinger systems is a challenging inverse problem, and stability results under limited (partial) boundary measurements have been lacking.

Method: The authors use an input-output operator framework with Neumann boundary data available only on a subset of the boundary, and construct a suitable geometric optics (WKB-type) solution to derive the estimates.

Result: A log-log type stability estimate is obtained for the matrix-valued first-order coefficients, which then yields a log-log-log type stability estimate for the zeroth-order matrix-valued potential.

Conclusion: Stability for the coupled relativistic Schrödinger system can be guaranteed even with partial boundary data, though at the cost of iterated logarithmic (weaker) stability rates.

Abstract: In this article, we prove a stability result for the identification of the coefficients appearing in a coupled relativistic Schrödinger system posed on a bounded domain of $\mathbb{R}^{1+n}$, where $n \geq 3$. More precisely, by utilizing input-output operator, where Neumann boundary data are available only on a subset of the boundary, and constructing an appropriate geometric optics solution, we derive a stability estimate of $\log$-$\log$ type for the matrix-valued first-order coefficients. This result is subsequently used to obtain a $\log$-$\log$-$\log$ type stability estimate for the zeroth-order matrix-valued potential.

</details>


### [89] [A Half-Space Theorem for the Allen--Cahn Equation in All Dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15014)
*Sanghoon Lee*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: A bounded, non-constant entire solution of the Allen–Cahn equation whose zero set lies in a half-space must be a one-dimensional heteroclinic solution, in every dimension.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Allen–Cahn equation models phase separation, and classifying its entire solutions is central to understanding their rigidity. A classical conjecture (related to De Giorgi's) concerns whether solutions with special geometric structure — here, a zero set confined to a half-space — must be one-dimensional.

Method: The proof establishes a rigidity result for bounded entire solutions via a geometric/analytic argument, exploiting the half-space confinement of the zero set to force symmetry and reduce the solution to a one-dimensional profile.

Result: A bounded, non-constant entire solution of the Allen–Cahn equation whose zero set is contained in a half-space is necessarily a one-dimensional heteroclinic solution, in every dimension.

Conclusion: The result confirms a strong rigidity phenomenon for the Allen–Cahn equation, extending one-dimensional classification to arbitrary dimensions under the half-space condition on the zero set.

Abstract: We prove that, in every dimension, a bounded, non-constant entire solution of the Allen--Cahn equation whose zero set is contained in a half-space is a one-dimensional heteroclinic solution.

</details>


### [90] [Single-Measurement Hölder Stability for a Spatial Coefficient in a Wave Equation with Principal Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15045)
*Huaian Diao,Umidjon Durdiev*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Global uniqueness and conditional L2-Hölder stability are established for recovering a spatial coefficient in the principal memory flux of a 3D wave equation with exponential relaxation kernel, from a single lateral Cauchy measurement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Recovering a spatial coefficient in the principal memory flux of a 3D wave equation with a prescribed exponential relaxation kernel is challenging. Two difficulties arise: the affine initial displacement makes initial acceleration vanish in the unknown region, and the coefficient-difference equation contains a divergence-form source whose direct L2 estimate loses a spatial derivative.

Method: The authors use a single finite-time lateral Cauchy measurement from one fixed, coefficient-independent affine initial displacement with zero initial velocity. They establish an acoustic Carleman estimate controlling the vector source and its time derivative without spatially differentiating the source, then use a transport estimate.

Result: Global uniqueness and conditional L2-Hölder stability are proven. Stability holds uniformly over a bounded, uniformly coercive W^{5,∞} coefficient class, without an ordering condition or smallness assumption near a reference coefficient.

Conclusion: The affine condition identifies the relevant initial trace with a directional derivative of the coefficient difference, and the quadratic temporal vanishing of the source permits absorption in the weighted estimate, enabling the stability result.

Abstract: We study the recovery of a spatial coefficient in the principal memory flux of a three-dimensional wave equation with the prescribed exponential relaxation kernel $βe^{-βt}$, where $β>0$ is known. From a single finite-time lateral Cauchy measurement generated by one fixed, coefficient-independent initial displacement that is affine near the unknown region, with zero initial velocity, we establish global uniqueness and conditional $L^2$-Hölder stability. Two difficulties arise in this setting. The affine initial displacement makes the initial acceleration vanish in the unknown region, while the coefficient-difference equation contains a divergence-form source whose direct $L^2$ estimate loses a spatial derivative. We establish an acoustic Carleman estimate that controls the vector source and its time derivative without spatially differentiating the source. The affine condition identifies the relevant initial trace with a directional derivative of the coefficient difference, and the quadratic temporal vanishing of the source permits absorption in the weighted estimate. A transport estimate then yields stability uniformly over a bounded, uniformly coercive $W^{5,\infty}$ coefficient class, without an ordering condition or a smallness assumption near a reference coefficient.

</details>


### [91] [Exact $L^2$-growth profiles for the free wave equation: Sharp constants, critical regularity, and local energy decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15086)
*Vishvesh Kumar,Mohamed Majdoub*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper characterizes the large-time behavior of $L^2$ and negative-order homogeneous Sobolev norms of free wave equation solutions, giving exact first-order asymptotics with a low-frequency mechanism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: R. Ikehata (2023) identified and quantified infinite-time $L^2$-growth of free waves in dimensions $N=1,2$. This work refines and extends those results, removing weighted moment assumptions and providing a unified explanation.

Method: For finite-energy data with $u_1\in L^1(\mathbb{R}^N)$, the authors derive exact first-order asymptotics of squared $L^2$-norms and negative-order homogeneous Sobolev norms, using a low-frequency analysis. They also obtain second-order expansions in dimension one and at the critical Sobolev index for $N\ge2$.

Result: When $P=\int u_1\neq0$, squared $L^2$-norm grows linearly for $N=1$ and logarithmically for $N=2$, with leading coefficient depending only on $P$; $u_1\in L^1\cap L^2$ suffices. The $L^2$-norm is bounded (hence convergent) iff $u_1\in\dot H^{-1}$. First-order asymptotic profiles cover polynomial growth, logarithmic growth, and convergence. In dimension two, exact asymptotics yield a sharper local energy decay estimate with explicit upper asymptotic constant independent of localization radius.

Conclusion: The results reveal a unified low-frequency mechanism underlying low-dimensional $L^2$-growth, extend Ikehata's findings, and provide sharper local energy decay estimates.

Abstract: We study the large-time behavior of the $L^2$-norm of solutions to the free wave equation on $\mathbb{R}^N$ and, more generally, their homogeneous Sobolev norms of negative order. We refine and extend the results of R. Ikehata [J. Hyperbolic Differ. Equ. 20 (2023)] who identified and quantified the infinite-time $L^2$-growth of free waves in dimensions $N=1,2$. For finite-energy data with $u_1\in L^1(\mathbb{R}^N)$, we derive exact first-order asymptotics: when $P=\int_{\mathbb{R}^N}u_1(x)\,dx\neq0$, the squared $L^2$-norm grows linearly for $N=1$ and logarithmically for $N=2$, with a leading coefficient depending only on $P$. In particular, no weighted moment assumption on $u_1$ is required; $u_1\in L^1\cap L^2$ suffices.
  More generally, we prove that the $L^2$-norm is bounded, and hence convergent, if and only if $u_1\in\dot H^{-1}(\mathbb{R}^N)$. We further establish first-order asymptotic profiles in negative-order homogeneous Sobolev spaces, covering polynomial growth, logarithmic growth, and convergence to a finite limit according to the Sobolev index. These results reveal a unified low-frequency mechanism underlying the low-dimensional $L^2$-growth.
  We also derive second-order asymptotic expansions in dimension one and at the critical Sobolev index in dimensions $N\ge2$. Finally, in dimension two, the exact $L^2$-asymptotics yield a sharper local energy decay estimate with an explicit upper asymptotic constant independent of the localization radius.

</details>


### [92] [Plane wave representation for wave scattering on a sphere](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15121)
*Valentin D. Kunz,Andrey V. Shanin,Raphael C. Assier*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies two-dimensional wave scattering on the sphere S^2 from point-source incidence, deriving three plane-wave representations of the scattered field and analytically continuing it to the complexified sphere.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivated by three-dimensional wave diffraction by conical shapes, the authors consider the analogous two-dimensional problem of wave scattering on the real sphere S^2.

Method: Using the recently introduced notion of plane waves on S^2 and results on the Laplace-Beltrami Green's function G, they derive three plane-wave representations for the scattered field u, analytically continue u to the complexified sphere S^2_c, formulate a complexified Green's third identity, and characterize complex sources.

Result: Three plane-wave representations for the scattered field are obtained, along with an analytic continuation to the complexified sphere, a complexified Green's third identity, and a characterization of complex sources.

Conclusion: The results establish strong analogies between planar and spherical scattering problems, notably through the introduction of directivities.

Abstract: Motivated by three-dimensional wave diffraction by conical shapes, we consider the two-dimensional problem of wave scattering on the real sphere $\mathcal{S}^2$ resulting from a point-source incidence on a scatterer $\mathbb{S}$. Using the recently introduced notion of plane waves on $\mathcal{S}^2$ as well as recent results on the Laplace--Beltrami Green's function $G$ on $\mathcal{S}^2$, we derive three plane-wave representations for the scattered field $u$. We then explain how $u$ can be analytically continued to the complexified sphere $\mathcal{S}^2_{\mathbb{c}}$. This allows us to formulate a complexified version of Green's third identity, and to provide a characterisation of complex sources. All our results are then used to draw strong analogies between planar and spherical scattering problems, notably through the introduction of directivities.

</details>


### [93] [Double-arc rearrangement and the symmetric isoperimetric problem in Gauss space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15133)
*Marco Barchiesi,Filippo Cagnetti,Vesa Julin*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: A new rearrangement in Gauss space reduces the Gaussian perimeter of symmetric sets and is used to study minimizers of the Gaussian perimeter among symmetric sets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding minimizers of the Gaussian perimeter among symmetric sets requires a suitable rearrangement operation in Gauss space.

Method: Introduce a new rearrangement in Gauss space that transforms symmetric sets while decreasing their Gaussian perimeter.

Result: The rearrangement decreases the Gaussian perimeter of symmetric sets.

Conclusion: This rearrangement provides a tool to analyze minimizers of the Gaussian perimeter among symmetric sets.

Abstract: We provide a new rearrangement in Gauss space transforming symmetric sets while decreasing their Gaussian perimeter. We use this rearrangement to analyze the minimizers of the Gaussian perimeter among symmetric sets.

</details>


### [94] [Extremals for singular Onofri type inequality in weighted Sobolev spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15195)
*Natalino Borgia,Silvia Cingolani,Rubén Fiñana Aranega,Gabriele Mancini*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper extends the singular N-dimensional Euclidean Onofri inequality to a weighted Sobolev space, proves a sharp singular Carleson-Chang type inequality, and establishes existence of nontrivial optimizers, answering open questions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Del Pino and Dolbeault proved the N-dimensional Euclidean Onofri inequality for smooth compactly supported functions. Lam and Lu introduced a singular version. Open questions remained about optimizers in both the non-singular and singular cases.

Method: The authors extend the inequality to a natural weighted Sobolev space, establish a sharp singular Carleson-Chang type inequality and prove its equivalence with the singular Onofri inequality. They exploit a classification result for a singular Liouville-type equation involving the N-Laplacian.

Result: Existence of nontrivial, non-constant optimizers is proved. The functional's conformal invariance is established, sharpness in terms of optimal constants is shown, and consequences including a sharp Poincaré inequality are stated.

Conclusion: The work answers open questions of Del Pino-Dolbeault (non-singular) and Lam-Lu (singular), providing a sharp singular Onofri inequality with optimizers in a weighted Sobolev setting.

Abstract: The $N$-dimensional Euclidean Onofri inequality was proved by Del Pino and Dolbeault for smooth compactly supported functions in $\mathbb{R}^N$ with $N \ge 2$. In this paper, we consider the singular version introduced by Lam and Lu, and extend it to a natural weighted Sobolev space. Next, we establish a sharp singular Carleson-Chang type inequality and prove its equivalence with the singular $N$-dimensional Euclidean Onofri inequality. Furthermore, we prove the existence of nontrivial, non-constant optimizers for the functional associated with the singular Euclidean Onofri inequality, answering open questions left by Del Pino and Dolbeault in the non-singular case, and by Lam and Lu in the case with singularities. To this end, we exploit the classification result obtained for a singular Liouville-type equation involving the $N$-Laplacian. We also prove the conformal invariance of the functional, establish the sharpness of the singular $N$-dimensional Euclidean Onofri inequality in terms of optimal constants, and state several consequences, including a sharp Poincaré inequality.

</details>


### [95] [Polynomial mixing for infinite-energy solutions of the white-forced Navier--Stokes equation in an infinite pipe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15216)
*Meng Zhao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves uniqueness of the invariant measure and polynomial mixing for white-forced Navier-Stokes equations in an infinite pipe, answering an open problem of Kuksin and Shirikyan.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The study is motivated by Open Problem 1 posed by Kuksin and Shirikyan concerning the long-time behavior of infinite-energy solutions to the white-forced Navier-Stokes equation in an infinite pipe with no-slip boundary conditions, specifically in the zero cross-sectional flux case.

Method: The authors analyze the long-time behavior of infinite-energy solutions in uniformly local spaces satisfying the divergence-free condition, under suitable degeneracy and spatial decay assumptions on the noise.

Result: Under the given assumptions, they establish the uniqueness of the invariant measure and prove polynomial mixing in the dual-Lipschitz metric.

Conclusion: The work affirmatively answers Open Problem 1 of Kuksin and Shirikyan for the case of zero cross-sectional flux.

Abstract: We study the long-time behavior of the infinite-energy solutions to the white-forced Navier--Stokes equation in an infinite pipe $\mathbb{R}\times (-1,1)$ with no-slip boundary conditions. The equation is globally well-posed in uniformly local spaces satisfying the divergence-free condition. Under suitable degeneracy and spatial decay assumptions on the noise, we prove the uniqueness of the invariant measure and establish polynomial mixing in the dual-Lipschitz metric. This provides an affirmative answer to Open Problem 1 posed by Kuksin and Shirikyan \cite{KS17} in the case of zero cross-sectional flux.

</details>


### [96] [Uniqueness of Bound States for Double-Power Scalar Field Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15232)
*Yujin Guo,Wei Ji,Yong Luo*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves uniqueness (up to translation and reflection) of bound states with exactly k nodes for a scalar field equation in R^N, in a small-epsilon coupling regime, resolving a conjecture of Berestycki-Lions and Tang.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The uniqueness of fixed-node bound states for the scalar field equation (*) was conjectured by Berestycki and Lions (ARMA, 1983) and Tang (Invent. Math., 2026), motivating this study.

Method: Analysis of the elliptic PDE -Δu+u = -ε|u|^{q-1}u + |u|^{p-1}u in R^N, studying solutions with prescribed node counts in a natural coupling regime with ε>0 sufficiently small.

Result: For each integer k ≥ 1, the equation (*) has, up to translation and reflection, a unique bound state with precisely k nodes when ε>0 is sufficiently small.

Conclusion: The results confirm the conjectured uniqueness of fixed-node bound states for equation (*), addressing the conjecture of Berestycki-Lions and Tang.

Abstract: We study the uniqueness of bound states for the following scalar field equation $$ -Δu+u=-\varepsilon |u|^{q-1}u+|u|^{p-1}u \,\ \ \mbox{in}\,\ \mathbb R^N, \,\ \varepsilon>0,\tag*{$(*)$} $$ where $ N\ge3,\ 1<p<(N+2)/(N-2)$ and $q>1$ satisfies $q \neq p$. For each integer $k \geq 1$, we prove that the equation $(*)$ has, up to translation and reflection, a unique bound state with precisely $k$ nodes in a natural coupling regime, where $\varepsilon>0$ is sufficiently small. Our results address a conjecture of [H. Berestycki and P. L. Lions, ARMA, 1983][M. X. Tang, Invent. Math., 2026] on the uniqueness of fixed-node bound states for the equation $(*)$.

</details>


### [97] [Nonexistence of Stable Solutions to Lane--Emden Equations on Graphs with Polynomial Volume Growth](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15275)
*Weiqi Guan*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies stable solutions of the discrete Lane-Emden equation on graphs with polynomial volume growth, proving nonexistence analogously to the Joseph-Lundgren range.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The discrete setting lacks a chain rule, so standard differential identities used in the continuous Lane-Emden theory cannot be directly applied, motivating the need for new algebraic tools.

Method: They establish algebraic inequalities substituting for differential identities, and combine them with the stability condition and cutoff arguments to obtain nonexistence results.

Result: Nonexistence of stable solutions is proved for a range of exponents analogous to the Joseph-Lundgren range on graphs with polynomial volume growth.

Conclusion: As an application, a lower bound for the Sobolev best constant in the discrete setting is derived.

Abstract: We study stable solutions of the Lane--Emden equation $$-Δu=|u|^{p-1}u$$ on graphs with polynomial volume growth. Due to the lack of a chain rule in the discrete setting, we establish suitable algebraic inequalities which serve as substitutes for the usual differential identities. Combining these inequalities with the stability condition and cutoff arguments, we obtain nonexistence results for stable solutions in a range of exponents analogous to the Joseph--Lundgren range. As an application, we derive a lower bound for the Sobolev best constant in the discrete setting.

</details>


### [98] [Global solutions to the fully nonlinear obstacle problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15313)
*Yi Cao,Dongsheng Li*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper classifies global solutions to the fully nonlinear obstacle problem by showing their coincidence sets, after factoring out invariant directions, fall into four types, and builds a bijection linking global solutions with compact coincidence sets to their asymptotic functions at infinity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding the structure of global solutions to obstacle-type problems is fundamental for regularity and free-boundary analysis, but such solutions can be complex. The paper aims to classify their coincidence sets and characterize them via asymptotic behavior.

Method: The authors study the fully nonlinear obstacle problem, factoring out invariant directions of the solutions, and analyze the geometry of the coincidence set and behavior at infinity to derive a classification and a bijection.

Result: The coincidence set of any global solution, modulo invariant directions, is shown to be one of four types: a single point, a compact convex set with nonempty interior, a ray (n=1), or a slender body (n≥2). A bijection is established between global solutions with compact coincidence sets and functions describing their asymptotic behavior at infinity.

Conclusion: Global solutions to the fully nonlinear obstacle problem admit a rigid classification of coincidence-set geometry and a one-to-one correspondence with their asymptotic profiles, providing a complete structural picture.

Abstract: In this paper, we investigate global solutions to the fully nonlinear obstacle problem. We prove that, after factoring out invariant directions, the coincidence set of any global solution belongs to one of the following four types: a single point, a compact convex set with nonempty interior, a ray for $n=1$, or a slender body for $n\geq 2$, where $n$ denotes the spatial dimension. Furthermore, we establish a bijection between the collection of all global solutions with compact coincidence sets and a family of functions describing the asymptotic behavior of such solutions at infinity.

</details>


### [99] [pper and Lower Bounds for Blow-up Times in Coupled Reaction--Diffusion Systems with Nonlocal Sources](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15367)
*Xin Zhao,Aynuer Ailatusi,Xianfa Song,Zeen Song*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies a coupled reaction-diffusion system with nonlocal integral terms in the nonlinearities, under Dirichlet or Neumann boundary conditions, and derives upper and lower bounds for the blow-up time of its solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Reaction-diffusion systems with nonlocal (integral) nonlinearities arise in many applications, and understanding when and how fast solutions blow up is important. The paper aims to characterize blow-up time bounds for such a coupled system.

Method: The authors consider a two-component reaction-diffusion system where the nonlinear terms F and G depend on the spatial integrals U(t) and V(t) of the unknowns, together with Dirichlet or Neumann boundary conditions. Under suitable conditions on the data, they establish upper and lower bounds for the blow-up time using analytical techniques for parabolic systems.

Result: Under certain conditions, the authors obtain both upper and lower bounds for the blow-up time of the solution to the system.

Conclusion: The work provides blow-up time estimates for a class of nonlocal reaction-diffusion systems, contributing to the understanding of their blow-up behavior.

Abstract: In this paper, we consider the following reaction-diffusion system \begin{equation*} \begin{cases}
  u_t=Δu+F\bigl(x,t,u,v,V(t)\bigr), & (x,t)\in\Om\times(0,T),
  v_t=Δv+G\bigl(x,t,u,v,U(t)\bigr), & (x,t)\in\Om\times(0,T),
  \mathcal B u=\mathcal B v=0, & (x,t)\in\partial\Om\times(0,T),
  u(x,0)=u_0(x),\quad v(x,0)=v_0(x), & x\in\Om, \end{cases} \end{equation*} where \begin{equation*}
  U(t)=\int_{\Om}u(x,t)\dd x,\qquad
  V(t)=\int_{\Om}v(x,t)\dd x, \end{equation*} and the boundary operator $\mathcal B$ is either \[
  \mathcal B_D w=w
  \qquad\text{or}\qquad
  \mathcal B_N w=\partial_νw, \] $\Om\subset\R^N$ be a bounded domain with sufficiently smooth boundary, $N\ge 1$, and $ν$ is the outward unit normal on $\partial\Om$.
  Under certain conditions, we obtain the upper and lower bounds for the blowup time of the solution to the system.

</details>


### [100] [Uniqueness of prescribed-node radial bound states for a zero-mass double-power equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15405)
*Wenjing Chen,Zexi Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves, for the zero-mass Sobolev-subcritical double-power equation, that for each k≥1 there is exactly one radial bound state with k simple zeros and u(0)>0, with maximum at the origin, confirming Tang's double-power uniqueness conjecture in this case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Tang's double-power uniqueness conjecture remains open, particularly in the zero-mass, Sobolev-subcritical regime; the paper aims to resolve this case.

Method: Adapts Tang's phase-transition analysis combined with a finite-level separation argument (Cortázar–García-Huidobro–Yarur). Introduces bridge functionals to propagate sign information through low amplitudes, a weighted integral positivity estimate for n=3 and p+q<3, and a radial-flux tail estimate with finite-level inverse-branch comparison.

Result: For every integer k≥1, there is exactly one radial bound state with k simple zeros and u(0)>0; its absolute maximum is at the origin. The shooting heights are strictly ordered, and the shooting variation along the k-node state has exactly k+1 simple zeros.

Conclusion: The zero-mass, Sobolev-subcritical case of Tang's double-power uniqueness conjecture is confirmed.

Abstract: We study radial bound states of \[
  Δu-\abs{u}^{q-1}u+\abs{u}^{p-1}u=0
  \quad\text{in }\R^n,
  \qquad n\ge3,\qquad 1<q<p<\frac{n+2}{n-2}. \] For every integer $k\ge1$, we prove that there is exactly one radial bound state with $k$ simple zeros and $u(0)>0$. Its absolute maximum is attained at the origin. This confirms the zero-mass, Sobolev-subcritical case of Tang's double-power uniqueness conjecture. The proof adapts Tang's phase-transition analysis and combines it with a finite-level separation argument inspired by Cortázar--García-Huidobro--Yarur. Three main difficulties arise in the zero-mass double-power setting: the sign changes of key comparison quantities in the low-amplitude region, the possible failure of pointwise positivity of an auxiliary quantity when $n=3$ and $p+q<3$, and the absence of exponential decay at infinity. We overcome these difficulties by introducing bridge functionals to propagate the required sign information through low amplitudes, establishing a weighted integral positivity estimate in the exceptional three-dimensional range, and combining a radial-flux tail estimate with finite-level inverse-branch comparison. We also establish the strict ordering of the bound-state shooting heights and show that the shooting variation along the \(k\)-node state has exactly \(k+1\) simple zeros.

</details>


### [101] [Spectral Gårding Elliptic Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15434)
*Hao Fang,Biao Ma*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies spectral Gårding elliptic equations on compact Kähler manifolds, proving smooth solvability extends Lin's top-degree theorem to all degrees and yields a Nakai-Moishezon type criterion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior solvability results for Gårding elliptic equations were limited to the top degree; extending them to all degrees and connecting to numerical criteria was the goal.

Method: Uses a concavity theorem for ideal Gårding polynomials to give a fixed concave formulation, applies Székelyhidi's second-order argument in every degree 2≤d≤n, and combines with numerical criteria of Fu-Li-Zhang-Zhang and Chen-Nie-Xu.

Result: Smooth solvability is equivalent to existence of a smooth representative in the admissible domain, yielding a Nakai-Moishezon type criterion. A solution induces a global Lorentzian structure, a strict nonlinear Hodge-Riemann hierarchy, explicit Hodge-Riemann classes, Lefschetz isomorphisms in bidegree (1,1), and weighted Hodge-index inequalities.

Conclusion: The work extends Lin's top-degree solvability theorem to all degrees and establishes deep links between Gårding elliptic equations, Lorentzian geometry, and Hodge-Riemann theory on compact Kähler manifolds.

Abstract: We study spectral Gårding elliptic equations on compact Kähler manifolds. Smooth solvability is equivalent to the existence of a smooth representative in the admissible domain. The concavity theorem for ideal Gårding polynomials gives a fixed concave formulation to which Székelyhidi's second-order argument applies in every degree $2\le d\le n$, extending Lin's top-degree solvability theorem. Combined with the numerical criteria of Fu--Li--Zhang--Zhang and Chen--Nie--Xu, this yields a Nakai--Moishezon type criterion for solvability on compact Kähler manifolds.
  A solution converts the local ideal Gårding structure into a global Lorentzian structure. Its mixed derivatives give a strict nonlinear Hodge--Riemann hierarchy from the degree of the equation down to degree two, and the homogenization of the intersection polynomial is Lorentzian in nef class directions. Numerical criteria then produce explicit Hodge--Riemann classes, Lefschetz isomorphisms in bidegree $(1,1)$, and weighted Hodge-index inequalities.

</details>


### [102] [A new approach to interior estimates for fully nonlinear elliptic equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15457)
*Jui-Yun Hung,Jiakun Liu,Neil Trudinger*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: New elementary proofs of interior second derivative estimates for the Monge-Ampère equation and the constant quadratic Hessian equation, based on the two-point maximum principle and requiring only one differentiation of the equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing proofs of interior second derivative (C^2) estimates for these fully nonlinear equations typically rely on heavy machinery and multiple differentiations of the equation. The authors seek simpler, more elementary arguments.

Method: The two-point maximum principle is applied to derive the estimates, using only one differentiation of the equation. For Monge-Ampère, the inhomogeneous term is assumed positive and continuously differentiable; for the quadratic Hessian equation, the method works in every dimension n ≥ 2.

Result: Elementary proofs of interior second derivative estimates are obtained for both the Monge-Ampère equation and the constant quadratic Hessian equation.

Conclusion: The two-point maximum principle yields simpler proofs requiring only one differentiation of the equation, and for the quadratic Hessian equation the estimate holds in all dimensions n ≥ 2.

Abstract: We give new, elementary proofs of interior second derivative estimates for the Monge-Ampère equation and the constant quadratic Hessian equation, based on the two-point maximum principle. The arguments require only one differentiation of the equation. For the Monge-Ampère equation, the inhomogeneous term is assumed to be positive and continuously differentiable. For the quadratic Hessian equation, the estimate holds in every dimension $n\ge2$.

</details>


### [103] [Classification of minimal-mass blowup solutions to the mass-critical Hartree equation at the $L^2$-critical level](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15460)
*Zuyu Ma,Changxing Miao,Jiqiang Zheng*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper classifies minimal-mass dynamics for the focusing mass-critical Hartree equation at ground-state mass M(Q), showing global non-scattering minimal-mass solutions are solitary waves and finite-time minimal-mass blowup solutions are pseudo-conformal images of solitary waves (up to symmetries), extending prior work to L^2 and dropping the radial assumption in the global case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The focusing mass-critical Hartree equation at ground-state mass M(Q) is a natural setting for studying critical dynamics, but prior classifications of minimal-mass solutions (LZ28, Miao-Xu-Zhao3, Wang-Gao) were restricted to scaling-subcritical settings or required radial symmetry, leaving the full L^2 scaling-critical case and non-radial global solutions unresolved.

Method: Rigidity/classification analysis of minimal-mass solutions: the authors show that, modulo the equation's symmetries, every global minimal-mass non-scattering solution is a solitary wave, and every finite-time minimal-mass blowup solution is a pseudo-conformal image of a solitary wave.

Result: Up to symmetries, all global minimal-mass non-scattering solutions are solitary waves, and all finite-time minimal-mass blowup solutions are pseudo-conformal images of solitary waves.

Conclusion: The classification extends earlier results to the scaling-critical space L^2 and removes the radial assumption in the global case, providing a complete minimal-mass description for the focusing mass-critical Hartree equation.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the focusing mass-critical Hartree equation \[ iu_t+Δu=-\bigl(|x|^{-2}*|u|^2\bigr)u \] at the ground-state mass $M(Q)$. We prove that, up to the symmetries of the equation, every global minimal-mass non-scattering solution is a solitary wave, while every finite-time minimal-mass blowup solution is a pseudo-conformal image of a solitary wave. This extends the results of \cite{LZ28,Miao-Xu-Zhao3,Wang-Gao} to the scaling-critical space $L^2$ and removes the radial assumption in the global case.

</details>


### [104] [Boundary Vanishing Orders and Asymptotic Expansions for Planar Harmonic Functions with a Sublinear Robin Condition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15465)
*Hairong Liu,Long Tian,Xiaoping Yang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies boundary vanishing orders and asymptotic expansions of planar harmonic functions satisfying a sublinear Robin boundary condition, bounding nodal domains via Steklov eigenvalues and classifying vanishing orders by critical exponent.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the boundary behavior, vanishing orders, and nodal structure of planar harmonic functions under a sublinear Robin condition, extending classical results to the sublinear regime.

Method: Analysis of global solutions on bounded connected Lipschitz domains; boundary scaling arguments, conformal coordinates, normalized graph coordinates, and comparison with Steklov eigenvalues.

Result: Nodal domains are bounded by the number of Steklov eigenvalues below λ; finite vanishing order holds at every boundary point. Critical exponent is 1/(1-p). On C^1 boundaries, positive subcritical orders are integers with a unique homogeneous Neumann profile; noninteger critical orders give convergence to an explicit profile determined by p; supercritical bulk orders m are integers with a unique homogeneous Dirichlet profile, and the boundary trace has vanishing order (m-1)/p > m.

Conclusion: Integer critical vanishing orders are excluded when the boundary is C^1 with Dini mean oscillation, giving a sharp classification of allowed vanishing orders for the sublinear Robin problem.

Abstract: We study boundary vanishing orders and asymptotic expansions of planar harmonic functions satisfying the sublinear Robin condition $\partial_νu=λu-|u|^{p-1}u,$
  with $λ\geq0$ and $0<p<1$. For nontrivial global solutions on bounded connected Lipschitz domains, we bound the number of nodal domains by the number of Steklov eigenvalues strictly below $λ$ and establish finite vanishing order at every boundary point. The boundary scaling gives the critical exponent $\frac{1}{1-p}$. On $C^1$ boundaries, every positive subcritical vanishing order is an integer, and the leading term is a unique homogeneous Neumann profile in conformal coordinates. At a noninteger critical order, the rescaled solution converges in normalized graph coordinates to an explicit homogeneous profile determined by $p$ up to sign. Every supercritical bulk vanishing order $m$ is an integer, with a unique homogeneous Dirichlet profile in conformal coordinates. The boundary trace has vanishing order $\frac{m-1}{p}>m$. Integer critical vanishing orders are excluded if the boundary is $C^1$ with Dini mean oscillation.

</details>


### [105] [Orbital stability of solitary wave solutions of the derivative nonlinear Schrödinger equation in the endpoint case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15507)
*Jia Shen,Yifei Wu,Changping Yang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves orbital stability in H^1(R) of endpoint solitary waves for the derivative nonlinear Schrödinger equation (DNLS), without extra assumptions, using mass and second Hamiltonian conservation plus an inverse Bäcklund compactness argument.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Endpoint solitary waves (c>0, c^2=4ω) for the DNLS equation are borderline cases where prior stability results required additional assumptions. The authors aim to remove those assumptions and establish unconditional orbital stability.

Method: The proof exploits conservation of mass and the second Hamiltonian H_2 to keep the Bäcklund-transformed solution small in H^1(R) uniformly in time. An inverse Bäcklund compactness result is established to reconstruct the algebraic solitary wave as the spectral zero approaches the negative real axis.

Result: Orbital stability in H^1(R) is proved for endpoint solitary waves satisfying c>0 and c^2=4ω, with no additional assumptions.

Conclusion: The endpoint solitary waves of the DNLS equation are orbitally stable in H^1(R), confirming stability in the borderline regime via Bäcklund transform and inverse compactness techniques.

Abstract: In this paper, we consider the orbital stability of solitary waves for the derivative nonlinear Schrödinger equation $$iq_t+q_{xx}+i\partial_x\bigl(|q|^2q\bigr)=0, \quad (t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}.$$ For the endpoint solitary waves satisfying $c>0$ and $c^2=4ω$, we prove the orbital stability in $H^1(\mathbb{R})$ without any additional assumptions. The proof uses conservation of mass and the second Hamiltonian $\mathcal{H}_2$ to keep the Bäcklund-transformed solution small in $H^1(\mathbb{R})$ uniformly in time. We establish an inverse Bäcklund compactness result to reconstruct the algebraic solitary wave as the spectral zero approaches the negative real axis.

</details>


### [106] [The Brakke-BV structure of flat flows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15511)
*Daniele De Gennaro,Salvatore Stuvard*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Varifold limits of the Almgren--Taylor--Wang / Luckhaus--Sturzenhecker minimizing movements scheme in ambient dimension at most 7 are integral Brakke flows, hence flat flows are generalized BV flows.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Minimizing movements schemes converge to limits called flat flows, but their regularity structure (varifold/BV nature) was unclear, especially without energy convergence or when fattening occurs.

Method: Analyze the minimizing movements scheme of Almgren--Taylor--Wang and Luckhaus--Sturzenhecker, studying varifold limits and their evolution properties in ambient dimension at most seven.

Result: Varifold limits are integral Brakke flows; consequently, L^1-limit points (flat flows) are generalized BV flows.

Conclusion: The result is unconditional: it holds even without energy convergence and in the presence of fattening.

Abstract: We show that, in ambient dimension at most seven, varifold limits of the minimizing movements scheme introduced by Almgren--Taylor--Wang and Luckhaus--Sturzenhecker are integral Brakke flows. As a consequence, we show that $L^1$-limit points of the scheme, usually known as flat flows, are generalized BV flows. The result is unconditional and, in particular, remains valid even in the absence of energy convergence and in the presence of fattening.

</details>


### [107] [A Viscoelastic Wave Equation with Nonlinear Damping and Supercritical Variable-Exponent Logarithmic Sources](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15528)
*Menglan Liao,Mingxue Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Study of a viscoelastic wave equation with variable-exponent damping and logarithmic source (supercritical regime), covering local existence, blow-up, lifespan bounds, global existence, and energy decay.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Key difficulties: the source may not map the energy space into L²(Ω); the logarithmic potential is sign-changing; and Sobolev control of displacement fails under supercritical damping.

Method: Analysis under an exponent condition linking source and damping exponents, using energy identity, continuation criterion, threshold based on positive part of shifted initial energy, time-weighted displacement estimate, and splitting of the memory integral.

Result: Proves local existence, energy identity, continuation criterion, finite-time blow-up for nonpositive shifted energy, blow-up criterion from large initial displacement-velocity pairing with explicit lifespan bound, blow-up data at every energy level, global existence in a stable region, and exponential/polynomial/logarithmic decay rates.

Conclusion: Provides a comprehensive theory for viscoelastic wave equations with variable-exponent damping and logarithmic source, linking exponents to existence, blow-up, and decay behavior.

Abstract: We investigate a viscoelastic wave equation with nonlinear variable-exponent damping and a variable-exponent logarithmic source, allowing supercritical growth. The main difficulties arise from the failure of the source to map the energy space into $L^2(Ω)$, the sign-changing logarithmic potential, and the lack of Sobolev control of the displacement for supercritical damping. Under an exponent condition linking the source and damping exponents, we prove local existence, an energy identity, and a continuation criterion. We prove finite-time blow-up for nontrivial solutions with nonpositive shifted initial energy. We also establish a blow-up criterion based on a sufficiently large initial displacement--velocity pairing, with a threshold depending on the positive part of the shifted initial energy, and derive an explicit upper bound for the lifespan. This criterion allows us to construct blow-up data at every prescribed level of the original energy. For initial data in a suitable stable region, solutions exist globally. For damping within the Sobolev range, a general relaxation condition yields energy decay estimates that include exponential and polynomial rates. Beyond this range, a time-weighted displacement estimate and a splitting of the memory integral give logarithmic decay.

</details>


### [108] [Homogenization of an optimal control problem for a Navier-Stokes-Cahn-Hilliard system in a 3D perforated domain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15584)
*Amartya Chakrabortty,Haradhan Dutta,Hari Shankar Mahato*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper analyzes homogenization of an optimal control problem for a Navier-Stokes-Cahn-Hilliard system in a periodically perforated 3D domain with small obstacles, proving $\Gamma$-convergence of the cost functionals to a homogenized Stokes-Cahn-Hilliard control problem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The study addresses homogenization of optimal control problems governed by complex fluid-phase-field systems in perforated domains. Since weak solutions of the state system may lack uniqueness for fixed $\varepsilon$, the control-to-state relation is multivalued, requiring a careful formulation over control-state pairs.

Method: The domain is periodically perforated by Lipschitz obstacles of size $\varepsilon^\alpha$ with $\alpha>3$. The fluid satisfies no-slip on the outer boundary and free-slip on obstacles; the phase-field equation has a source term; and the convective term carries a pore-scale Reynolds number tending to zero. The microscopic problems are posed over control-state pairs, with a distributed body-force control and a cost functional tracking velocity in $L^2$ and order parameter in $H^1$. The authors prove existence of microscopic optimal pairs and sequential $\Gamma$-convergence of the cost functionals.

Result: The cost functionals $\Gamma$-converge to the cost functional of a control problem governed by the unsteady Stokes-Cahn-Hilliard system on the full domain. Consequently, optimal values converge, every cluster point of microscopic optimal pairs is optimal for the homogenized problem, and optimal controls converge strongly along subsequences. The $\liminf$ inequality follows from homogenization of the state system, including strong convergence of order-parameter gradients. The recovery sequence requires uniqueness of weak solutions of the homogenized system, proven when the outer boundary is of class $C^{2,1}$.

Conclusion: The paper establishes a rigorous homogenization framework for optimal control of Navier-Stokes-Cahn-Hilliard systems in perforated domains, showing that microscopic optimal controls converge to optimal controls of a homogenized Stokes-Cahn-Hilliard problem under suitable regularity assumptions.

Abstract: We study the homogenization of an optimal control problem governed by a Navier-Stokes-Cahn-Hilliard system in a periodically perforated three-dimensional domain. The obstacles are Lipschitz sets of size $\varepsilon^α$, $α>3$, where $\varepsilon$ is the period. The fluid satisfies the no-slip condition on the outer boundary and the free-slip condition on the obstacles, the phase-field equation contains a source term, and the convective term carries a pore-scale Reynolds number that tends to zero as $\varepsilon\to0$. The control is a distributed body force, and the cost functional tracks the velocity in $L^2$ and the order parameter in $H^1$. Since uniqueness of weak solutions of the state system is not known for fixed $\varepsilon$, the control-to-state relation is multivalued, and we pose the microscopic problems over control-state pairs. We prove the existence of microscopic optimal pairs and the sequential $Γ$-convergence of the cost functionals to the cost functional of a control problem governed by the unsteady Stokes-Cahn-Hilliard system on the full domain. As a consequence, the optimal values converge, every cluster point of microscopic optimal pairs is optimal for the homogenized problem, and the optimal controls converge strongly along the corresponding subsequences. The $\liminf$ inequality follows from the homogenization of the state system, including the strong convergence of the gradients of the order parameter. Only the recovery sequence requires uniqueness of weak solutions of the homogenized system, which we prove when the outer boundary is of class $C^{2,1}$.

</details>


### [109] [Global solutions to the compressible Navier-Stokes-Poisson equations in the half-space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15588)
*Hairong Liu,Jinlong Wu,Hua Zhong*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Global strong solutions and algebraic decay for 3D compressible Navier-Stokes-Poisson in a half-space with no-slip and Neumann boundary conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Establishes global well-posedness and long-time decay for the compressible Navier-Stokes-Poisson system in a half-space, a domain setting complicated by boundary effects where the electric potential couples with the fluid through the boundary.

Method: Combines density dissipation, tangential and normal derivative estimates, and a modified energy using the Helmholtz projection to handle electric boundary contributions. Decay proof uses a formulation in terms of momentum ρu, uniform negative-order estimates, and interpolation with dissipation.

Result: For small H^3 perturbations of a constant state with small L^2 initial electric field, global existence and uniqueness of strong solutions hold. Under finite tangential negative Sobolev norms of order −s (1/2 ≤ s < 1) for density perturbation, velocity, and electric field, solutions decay at rate (1+t)^{−s/2} in the full energy norm.

Conclusion: The no-slip/homogeneous Neumann half-space problem for compressible Navier-Stokes-Poisson admits global unique strong solutions with algebraic decay governed by tangential low-frequency assumptions, handled via a modified energy accounting for electric boundary effects.

Abstract: We study the initial boundary value problem for the compressible Navier-Stokes-Poisson equations without damping in the three-dimensional half-space. The velocity satisfies the no-slip boundary condition, and the electric potential satisfies the homogeneous Neumann boundary condition. For small perturbations of a constant state in $H^3$, with a small initial electric field in $L^2$, we establish global existence and uniqueness of strong solutions. The uniform estimates combine density dissipation, tangential and normal derivative estimates, and a modified energy that accounts for the electric boundary contribution through the Helmholtz projection. We also prove algebraic decay under an additional low-frequency assumption on the initial data. If the density perturbation, velocity and electric field have finite tangential negative Sobolev norms of order $-s$, where $1/2\leq s<1$, then the solution decays at the rate $(1+t)^{-s/2}$ in the full energy norm. The decay estimate follows from the formulation in terms of the momentum $ρu$, uniform negative-order estimates and interpolation with the dissipation.

</details>


### [110] [Negative Sobolev regularity and optimal decay for the fractional Navier--Stokes--Poisson system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15594)
*Yuan Xu,Junhao Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves global existence, uniqueness, time-decay estimates, and a global-in-time inviscid limit for the 3D repulsive compressible Navier-Stokes-Riesz system (Riesz exponent 0<s<1, viscosity 0<ε≤1) for small perturbations of a constant equilibrium.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Riesz interaction ∇(−Δ)^{−s}(ρ−ρ̄) generalizes the Coulomb interaction for electrons. The goal is to understand how dispersion (from the Riesz potential) and viscosity interact, and to establish a rigorous inviscid limit to the compressible Euler-Riesz system, which is non-trivial due to the coexistence of dispersive and dissipative effects.

Method: The proof combines viscosity-adapted dispersive estimates, normal-form analysis, and nonlinear energy estimates with control of both negative and positive Sobolev norms, applied to the Cauchy problem with solenoidal initial velocity of order O(ε).

Result: Global existence and uniqueness of smooth solutions; L² and L∞ time-decay estimates capturing uniform-in-ε dispersive behavior and viscosity-dependent dissipation; and a global-in-time inviscid limit to the irrotational global solution of the compressible Euler-Riesz system with an explicit convergence rate in W^{k,p} norms.

Conclusion: The compressible Navier-Stokes-Riesz system admits global smooth solutions with dual dispersion-dissipation decay, and converges globally in time to the irrotational Euler-Riesz solution as viscosity vanishes, with explicit rates.

Abstract: We study the Cauchy problem in $\R^{3}$ for the repulsive compressible Navier-Stokes-Riesz system with Riesz exponent $0<s<1$ and viscosity $0<\varepsilon\leq1$, where the Riesz interaction $\nabla(-Δ)^{-s}(ρ-\barρ)$ is a generalization of the Coulomb interaction for electrons. For small perturbations of a constant equilibrium, with the solenoidal component of the initial velocity of order $\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)$, we prove the global existence and uniqueness of smooth solutions. We derive time-decay estimates in $L^{2}$ norms and $L^{\infty}$ norms that capture both uniform-in-$\varepsilon$ dispersive behavior and viscosity-dependent dissipation. We further establish a global-in-time inviscid limit to the irrotational global solution of the compressible Euler-Riesz system whose initial data consist of the same density and the curl-free component of the velocity, with an explicit convergence rate in $W^{k,p}$ norms. The proof combines viscosity-adapted dispersive estimates, normal-form analysis and nonlinear energy estimates with control of both negative and positive Sobolev norms.

</details>


### [111] [The CR Nirenberg Problem, Part II: Existence and Topological Degree](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15597)
*Jiechen Qiang,Zhongwei Tang,Heming Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Existence and degree results for the CR Nirenberg problem on the standard sphere, following Part I's blow-up analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extends the blow-up and compactness framework from Part I to establish existence and degree-theoretic results for the CR Nirenberg problem, addressing both symmetric and general curvature settings.

Method: Proves existence under antipodal and finite-group symmetries; without symmetry, derives a Leray–Schauder degree formula under flatness conditions; obtains Aubin-type inequalities under a vanishing first-moment condition.

Result: Existence results for positive prescribed curvatures with homogeneous expansions at critical points; Aubin-type inequalities uniform near the critical exponent.

Conclusion: The CR Nirenberg problem admits solutions under symmetry assumptions and, via degree formulas, for certain prescribed curvatures with flatness/homogeneous expansion conditions.

Abstract: This paper is a continuation of Part~I \cite{QTWPartI}, where blow-up analysis and compactness results for the CR Nirenberg problem were established. We prove some existence and degree results for the CR Nirenberg problem on the standard sphere. More precisely, we first prove existence results under antipodal and, more generally, finite-group symmetries. Without symmetry, we derive a Leray--Schauder degree formula under natural flatness conditions, yielding existence results for positive prescribed curvatures with certain homogeneous expansions at critical points. We also obtain Aubin-type inequalities under a vanishing first-moment condition, uniformly near the critical exponent.

</details>


### [112] [Blow-down and immediate onset of positivity in the Neumann problem for (super-)fast diffusion equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15673)
*Youshan Tao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the homogeneous Neumann problem for the degenerate parabolic equation $u_t=\nabla\cdot(u^{m-1}\nabla u)$ in the super-fast diffusion range $m\in(-\infty,1)$, deriving optimal upper/lower solution bounds and applying them to obtain global solvability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The super-fast diffusion regime $m\in(-\infty,1)$ is comparatively less understood, and sharp estimates on solutions together with solvability results for broad initial data are needed.

Method: Two Moser-type iterations are used to infer upper and lower estimates for solutions on smoothly bounded domains $\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^n$; optimality of these bounds is established via comparison arguments.

Result: Upper and lower estimates for solutions are obtained with optimality properties, yielding information on regularization into spaces of bounded and uniformly positive functions, and leading to a global solvability result.

Conclusion: Global solvability in the associated initial-boundary value problem holds for all nonnegative, nontrivial initial data in $L^1(\Omega)\cap\bigcup_{s>n(1-m)/2}L^s(\Omega)$.

Abstract: For the degenerate parabolic equation $u_t=\nabla\cdot (u^{m-1}\nabla u)$, the homogeneous Neumann problem in smoothly bounded domains $Ω\subset {\mathbb{R}}^n$ is considered in the (super-fast) diffusion range when $m\in (-\infty,1)$. Upper and lower estimates for solutions are inferred by means of two Moser-type iterations, and optimality properties of these bounds are derived using comparison arguments. On the basis of the accordingly obtained information on regularization into spaces of bounded and uniformly positive functions, a result on global solvability in an associated initial-boundary value problem is achieved for all nonnegative and nontrivial initial data belonging to $L^1(Ω) \cap \bigcup_{s>n(1-m)/2} L^s(Ω)$.

</details>


### [113] [Sharp conditions for fully nonlinear equations with $(n-1)$-Hessian matrix: Part I. Monge--Ampère case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15693)
*Jingwen Ji,Feida Jiang,Mengni Li*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies entire subsolutions of the normalized real (n-1) Monge-Ampère equation with gradient terms, establishing necessary and sufficient existence conditions for q<2-1/n and analyzing the critical case q=2-1/n.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existence theory for the normalized real (n-1) Monge-Ampère equation with gradient dependence is largely unexplored, especially for entire subsolutions and degenerate/singular gradient cases.

Method: Analyze positive (n-1)-convex entire subsolutions under two sets of structural assumptions on continuous non-decreasing coefficients f and g, deriving sharp existence criteria.

Result: Necessary and sufficient existence conditions for q<2-1/n, covering f≡0 with 0<q<1, q<0 singular case, and sign-changing g. For q=2-1/n: recovers Keller-Osserman condition, proves nonexistence for eventually positive g, and gives an exponentially weighted condition when g has nonpositive limit.

Conclusion: The determinant case is fully treated, serving as the first of two companion papers.

Abstract: We study positive $(n-1)$-convex entire subsolutions of the normalized real $(n-1)$ Monge--Ampère equation $\operatorname{det}^{\frac1n}\bigl(ΔuI-D^2u\bigr)=f(u)+g(u)|Du|^q$ in $\mathbb R^n$. Under two sets of structural assumptions on the continuous, non-decreasing coefficients $f$ and $g$, we establish necessary and sufficient conditions for the existence of entire subsolutions when $q<2-\frac1n$. Our results cover the pure-gradient degenerate case $f\equiv0$ with $0<q<1$, the gradient-singular case $q<0$, and the case where $g$ changes sign. For $q=2-\frac1n$, we recover the Keller--Osserman condition for the gradient-independent equation, prove nonexistence when $g$ is eventually positive, and obtain an exponentially weighted condition when $g$ has a nonpositive limit at infinity. This is the first of two companion papers and treats the determinant case.

</details>


### [114] [Sharp conditions for fully nonlinear equations with $(n-1)$-Hessian matrix: Part II. $k$-Hessian case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15697)
*Jingwen Ji,Feida Jiang,Mengni Li*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Extends Part I (determinant case k=n) to the k-Hessian operator S_k^{1/k}[ΔuI−D²u]=f(u)+g(u)|Du|^q for 1≤k<n, characterizing entire subsolutions and radial boundary blow-up solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Continuing earlier work on the determinant case k=n, the authors aim to understand existence and blow-up behavior for the more general k-Hessian operator (1≤k<n), especially in regimes where standard Lipschitz/regularity assumptions fail.

Method: Under structural assumptions on continuous, non-decreasing coefficients f and g, they derive necessary and sufficient conditions for existence of admissible entire subsolutions when q<2.

Result: Conditions cover non-Lipschitz case 0<q<1 with possibly vanishing zero-order term, gradient-singular case q<0, and sign-changing gradient coefficients. They also characterize finite-radius blow-up of radial solutions, with two-sided estimates for blow-up radius and pointwise upper bounds for profiles via inverse Keller–Osserman functions.

Conclusion: The work provides a unified framework extending the determinant case to general k-Hessian operators, giving sharp existence and blow-up characterizations across various degeneracies.

Abstract: We study admissible entire subsolutions and radial boundary blow-up solutions of $S_k^{\frac1k}[ΔuI-D^2u]=f(u)+g(u)|Du|^q$ for $1\leq k<n$, continuing our study of the determinant case $k=n$ in Part I. Under structural assumptions on the continuous, non-decreasing coefficients $f$ and $g$, we establish necessary and sufficient conditions for the existence of entire subsolutions when $q<2$. Our results cover the non-Lipschitz case $0<q<1$ with a possibly vanishing zero-order term, the gradient-singular case $q<0$, and sign-changing gradient coefficients. We also characterize finite-radius blow-up of radial solutions and derive two-sided estimates for the blow-up radius, as well as pointwise upper bounds for their profiles in terms of inverse Keller--Osserman functions.

</details>


### [115] [Small-speed solitary-wave stability and global well-posedness for a Bessel-regularized Airy equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15751)
*Germán Preciado López*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Proves orbital stability of small-speed solitary waves for a Bessel-filtered Airy equation with quadratic interaction, via constrained minimization and a Lyapunov functional, supported by well-posedness in Bourgain spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper studies stability of solitary waves for an Airy equation with a quadratic (KdV-type) interaction filtered by a Bessel potential of order one, addressing the small-speed regime where a KdV limit and resolvent perturbation arguments can be applied.

Method: Constrained minimization (giving one negative direction), small-speed KdV limit plus operator-norm perturbation of the profile resolvent (yielding a one-dimensional translation kernel), a differentiable rescaled branch with bounded derivative (positive slope of the quadratic invariant), and dynamical tools: a Bourgain-space bilinear estimate, local well-posedness in L², unconditional uniqueness from 1/4 derivative, and global well-posedness in the 3/2-derivative energy space.

Result: Orbital stability in the three-halves-derivative energy space at small positive speeds, with uniqueness of centered minimizers; smooth, symmetric, strictly monotone (away from center), exponentially localized positive profiles exist for every prescribed positive speed. At general positive speeds, stability remains conditional on nondegeneracy and the slope condition.

Conclusion: Small-speed solitary waves are orbitally stable and profiles are unique/well-structured. For arbitrary speeds, stability is reduced to verifiable nondegeneracy and slope conditions.

Abstract: We prove orbital stability of minimizing solitary waves at sufficiently small positive speeds for an Airy equation with a quadratic interaction filtered by a Bessel potential of order one. The stability hypotheses are verified: constrained minimization gives one negative direction, the small-speed KdV limit and operator-norm perturbation of the profile resolvent give a one-dimensional translation kernel, and a differentiable rescaled branch with uniformly bounded derivative gives a positive slope of the quadratic invariant. A conserved Lyapunov functional then proves orbital stability in the three-halves-derivative energy space and the perturbation also gives uniqueness of centered minimizers at small speed. The dynamical framework is supplied by a direct Bourgain-space bilinear estimate, local well-posedness in the square-integrable class, unconditional uniqueness from one-quarter derivative onward, and global well-posedness in the half-derivative energy space. For every prescribed positive speed, constrained minimization constructs smooth positive profiles that are symmetric, strictly monotone away from their center, and exponentially localized. At general positive speeds the stability criterion remains conditional on nondegeneracy and the slope condition.

</details>


### [116] [Failure of dimension drop for harmonic measure on Ahlfors regular boundaries of dimension close to $1$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15759)
*Yingying Cai,Cole Jeznach,Xavier Tolsa*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: For every 0<s<1, the authors construct a compact s-Ahlfors regular set K⊂ℂ whose harmonic measure (pole at infinity) is comparable to H^s|_K, showing no dimension drop for any s<1.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Harmonic measure can exhibit a "dimension drop" relative to the Hausdorff dimension of the set. This paper addresses whether such a drop is unavoidable, aiming to construct sets where no drop occurs for all dimensions s<1.

Method: An N-adic construction based on configurations near the N-th roots of unity. Children positions are adjusted via small polynomial deformations so the logarithmic potential of the natural Bernoulli measure becomes constant in the limit. A first-mode correction achieves s<2/3, and corrections of arbitrary finite order extend to 0<s<1.

Result: For every 0<s<1, a compact s-Ahlfors regular set K⊂ℂ is constructed with harmonic measure of ℂ\K (pole at infinity) comparable to H^s|_K, establishing no dimension drop for any s<1.

Conclusion: No dimension drop occurs for any dimension s<1; suitable N-adic constructions with polynomial deformations yield the desired comparability for the full range 0<s<1.

Abstract: For every $0<s<1$ we construct a compact $s$-Ahlfors regular set $K\subset\mathbb C$ for which harmonic measure of $\mathbb C\setminus K$ with pole at infinity is comparable to $\mathcal H^s|_K$. In particular, there is no dimension drop for any dimension $s<1$. The construction is $N$-adic and based on configurations close to the $N$-th roots of unity. We adjust the positions of the children by small polynomial deformations so that the logarithmic potential of the natural Bernoulli measure becomes constant in the limit. A first-mode correction gives the range $s<2/3$, while corrections of arbitrary finite order yield the full range $0<s<1$.

</details>


### [117] [Explicit counterexamples to Frank-Loss optimizer conjectures for critical Dirac and vector Sobolev inequalities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15787)
*Bartosz Bieganowski,Jarosław Mederski*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper computes closed-form quotients of two elementary competitor families for critical Sobolev inequalities of the Dirac operator and vector fields, disproving optimizer claims and answering an open question on SO(2)-invariant minimization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Frank and Loss conjectured optimality of their spinorial family and suggested the higher-dimensional Loss-Yau potential as an optimizer; Wang and Zhang recently disproved these claims. This work seeks explicit, elementary competitors yielding strictly smaller quotients and resolving remaining questions.

Method: Two explicit competitor families are constructed: (i) for d>=2, a constant spinor times a scalar Aubin-Talenti optimizer; (ii) for d>=3, the planar azimuthal field A_d(x)=d/(1+|x|^2)^2 (-x_2,x_1,0,...,0), lying in the nonlinear gauge. Quotients are computed in closed form.

Result: Both families produce strictly smaller quotients. For odd d>=3 this gives an explicit proof of nonoptimality of the higher-dimensional Loss-Yau-Dunne-Min potential. In d=3, the field is a linear combination of the Loss-Yau potential and its reflected twin, a member of the Wang-Zhang family.

Conclusion: The spinorial and vector competitors explicitly disprove the optimality conjectures. The axisymmetry of A_d provides a negative answer to the Wang-Zhang question about minimization within the SO(2)x1-invariant class.

Abstract: Frank and Loss proposed critical Sobolev inequalities for the Dirac operator and for vector fields. They conjectured optimality of their spinorial family and wrote that it is conceivable that the higher-dimensional Loss-Yau potential is an optimizer. Wang and Zhang recently disproved both optimizer claims using direct comparison and instability arguments. We compute closed-form quotients for two elementary families of explicit competitors with strictly smaller quotients. For every $d\geq2$, the spinorial competitor is a constant spinor multiplied by a scalar Aubin-Talenti optimizer. For every $d\geq3$, the vector competitor is the planar azimuthal field \[
  A_d(x)=\frac{d}{(1+\lvert x\rvert^2)^2}
  (-x_2,x_1,0,\ldots,0). \] It lies in the nonlinear gauge and its quotient is computed in closed form. For every odd $d\geq3$, it gives an explicit proof of nonoptimality of the higher-dimensional Loss-Yau-Dunne-Min potential. In dimension three, this field is a specific member of the competitor family of Wang and Zhang: it is a linear combination of the Loss-Yau potential and its reflected twin. Its axisymmetry gives a negative answer to their question about minimization within the $\mathrm{SO}(2)\times 1$-invariant class.

</details>


### [118] [Failure of dimension drop for harmonic measure on Ahlfors regular flat boundaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15805)
*Yingying Cai,Cole Jeznach,Xavier Tolsa*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The authors construct an $N$-adic (non-Archimedean / $p$-adic) analogue of the David-Jeznach-Julia construction to build compact subsets of $\mathbb{R}\subset\mathbb{C}$ whose harmonic measure has no dimension drop, achieving any dimension $s\in(0,1/2)$ and proving the threshold $1/2$ is sharp.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The David-Jeznach-Julia construction for compact subsets of $\mathbb{R}$ was limited to complementary domains whose harmonic measure has no dimension drop. The authors want to extend this construction via an $N$-adic modification to reach the full range of dimensions $s\in(0,1/2)$ and determine the sharp threshold.

Method: They develop an $N$-adic modification of the David-Jeznach-Julia construction, applying it to compact $s$-Ahlfors regular sets $K\subset\mathbb{R}\subset\mathbb{C}$.

Result: For every $s\in(0,1/2)$, there exists an $s$-Ahlfors regular compact set $K\subset\mathbb{R}\subset\mathbb{C}$ with $\dim\omega^\infty=s$, where $\omega^\infty$ is the harmonic measure of $\mathbb{C}\setminus K$ with pole at infinity. Combined with a prior result of the third author, this proves the threshold $1/2$ is sharp.

Conclusion: The $N$-adic modification generalizes the David-Jeznach-Julia construction and establishes that $1/2$ is the sharp threshold for the dimension of harmonic measure (with no dimension drop) attainable by such compact subsets of the real line.

Abstract: We develop an $N$-adic modification of the construction of David-Jeznach-Julia for compact subsets of the real line $\mathbb{R}\subset\mathbb{C}$ for which harmonic measure for the complementary domains has no dimension drop. In particular, we show that for every $s\in(0,1/2)$, there exists an $s$-Ahlfors regular compact set $K\subset\mathbb{R}\subset\mathbb{C}$ such that $\mathrm{dim}ω^\infty=s$, where $ω^\infty$ denotes harmonic measure for $\mathbb{C}\setminus K$ with pole at infinity. Combined with a result of the third author, this shows that the threshold $1/2$ is sharp.

</details>
