### [119] [Regularity for Minimizers of Mixed-Smoothness Variational Integrals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15844)
*Jan Kristensen,Adam Prosinski,Aidan Strong*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: First partial regularity results for minimizers of suitably quasiconvex variational integrals on anisotropic Sobolev spaces, related to higher-gradient continuum models of pantographic metamaterials.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Variational integrals on anisotropic Sobolev spaces arise from higher gradient continuum models for pantographic metamaterials, but no partial regularity theory existed for their minimizers. This gap motivates establishing such results.

Method: The authors analyze minimizers of a class of suitably quasiconvex variational integrals defined on anisotropic Sobolev spaces, adapting regularity techniques (e.g., quasiconvexity and partial regularity arguments) to the anisotropic setting.

Result: They establish the first partial regularity results for minimizers of these integrals, showing regularity outside a negligible (singular) set.

Conclusion: The work initiates a partial regularity theory for anisotropic, quasiconvex variational problems tied to pantographic metamaterial models, providing a foundation for further study.

Abstract: We establish the first partial regularity results for minimizers of a class of suitably quasiconvex variational integrals defined on anisotropic Sobolev spaces. These variational integrals are related to higher gradient continuum models for pantographic metamaterials.

</details>


### [120] [Lack of exponential stability for thermoelastic Timoshenko and Bresse systems with full homogeneous Dirichlet boundary conditions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15890)
*Pedro Roberto de Lima*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Thermoelastic Timoshenko and Bresse systems with thermal coupling only on the bending moment are not exponentially stable under full homogeneous Dirichlet boundary conditions when the equal wave speed condition fails, giving complete exponential stability characterizations via coefficients.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior characterizations of exponential stability for thermoelastic Timoshenko/Bresse systems were only known under mixed boundary conditions; full homogeneous Dirichlet conditions remained unresolved.

Method: Introduces a boundary correction term in the standard trigonometric ansatz so the full homogeneous Dirichlet conditions are satisfied; uses this to prove the lack of exponential stability.

Result: The systems are not exponentially stable under full homogeneous Dirichlet boundary conditions when the equal wave speed condition is not satisfied; combined with known converses, this yields complete characterizations.

Conclusion: Complete coefficient-based characterizations of exponential stability are now established for both Timoshenko and Bresse systems, extending earlier results beyond mixed boundary conditions.

Abstract: In this paper we prove that the classical thermoelastic Timoshenko and Bresse systems with thermal coupling acting only on the bending moment are not exponentially stable under full homogeneous Dirichlet boundary conditions if the equal wave speed condition is not satisfied. Combined with their well-known converses, our results provide complete characterizations of exponential stability in terms of the coefficients of each system. To the best of our knowledge, these characterizations were previously known only under mixed boundary conditions. The proof is based on the introduction of a boundary correction term in the standard trigonometric ansatz so that the full homogeneous Dirichlet conditions are satisfied.

</details>


### [121] [Sharp stability for mixed bubbles on hyperbolic space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15920)
*Juncheng Wei,Zetian Yan,Zhipeng Yang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Sharp residual stability estimates for the critical Poincaré-Sobolev equation near a sum of one hyperbolic solution and one projected Euclidean bubble, with energy/mass constraints and a characterization of Palais-Smale levels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding stability and the energy landscape of the critical Poincaré-Sobolev equation near multi-profile superpositions, especially uniformity in profile centers and the dependence of limiting energy/mass on profile numbers.

Method: Prove sharp residual stability estimates uniform in relative positions of centers in dimensions ≥4; handle nonnegative functions under an energy constraint for λ outside a finite set, add a mass constraint; analyze arbitrary finite numbers of profiles.

Result: Sharp stability rate is linear except in dimension six, where it includes a square root of a logarithm. Nonnegative functions satisfy the same estimates under energy constraint (and mass constraint removes the finite-set restriction); all nonnegative Palais-Smale levels are identified and realized.

Conclusion: Parameters where different pairs of profile numbers share the same energy are characterized, and the limiting energy and mass together determine both profile numbers.

Abstract: We prove sharp residual stability estimates for the critical Poincaré-Sobolev equation near sums of one positive hyperbolic solution and one projected Euclidean bubble in dimensions at least four. The estimates are uniform in the relative positions of the centers. The sharp rate is linear except in dimension six, where it contains a square root of a logarithm. For nonnegative functions, the same estimates hold under an energy constraint near the sum of the two profile energies for \(λ\) outside a finite set, and an additional mass constraint removes this restriction. For arbitrary finite numbers of profiles, we identify and realize all nonnegative Palais-Smale levels. We characterize the parameters at which different pairs of profile numbers have the same energy and show that the limiting energy and mass determine both profile numbers.

</details>


### [122] [Regularity theorems for Lipschitz viscosity solutions to the minimal surface system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15952)
*Bryan Dimler*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves regularity theorems for Lipschitz viscosity solutions of the minimal surface system, centered on a "Decoupling Lemma" that lets component functions satisfy homogeneous Pucci inequalities, enabling scalar regularity theory to apply componentwise.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Regularity of solutions to the minimal surface system is harder than the scalar case, and viscosity methods have not been fully exploited for the system. The goal is to extend scalar viscosity regularity tools to the system setting.

Method: A Decoupling Lemma showing component functions of Lipschitz viscosity solutions satisfy homogeneous Pucci inequalities; then applying scalar viscosity regularity theory componentwise, plus arguments for boundary regularity, Harnack inequalities, and epsilon-regularity.

Result: 2D Lipschitz viscosity solutions are analytic in the interior; quantitative partial regularity; a geometric Harnack inequality yielding a strong maximum principle and half-space Liouville theorem; a boundary regularity theorem without density assumptions or stationarity; classification of the singular set of 4D stationary solutions on a bounded domain; and an epsilon-regularity theorem.

Conclusion: Viscosity methods are a useful tool for studying regularity of minimal graphs. The paper closes by surveying open problems and including a Bernstein-type theorem for 3D homogeneous entire Lipschitz viscosity solutions.

Abstract: We prove several regularity theorems concerning Lipschitz viscosity solutions to the minimal surface system. The main lemma (i.e. ``Decoupling Lemma") says that the component functions of such solutions satisfy the homogeneous Pucci inequalities. This allows us to apply the regularity theory for scalar viscosity solutions for elliptic PDE component-wise to a viscosity solution for the system.
  As a result of the Decoupling Lemma, we deduce that 2D Lipschitz viscosity solutions to the system are analytic in the interior, and obtain a quantitative partial regularity theorem. We also prove a geometric Harnack inequality for Lipschitz solutions. The Harnack inequality yields a strong maximum principle and half-space Liouville theorem. Another significant consequence of the lemma is a boundary regularity theorem for Lipschitz viscosity solutions. Notably, our theorem does not require any density assumption at the graph boundary, and we do not assume stationarity. This is used to prove a classification theorem for the singular set of a 4D stationary solution on a bounded domain. We also prove an $\varepsilon$-regularity theorem extending a result of the author.
  To conclude, we survey open problems. As an example, we have included a Bernstein-type theorem for 3D homogeneous entire Lipschitz viscosity solutions. Taken together, our results suggest that viscosity methods are a useful tool for studying the regularity of minimal graphs.

</details>


### [123] [Global stability and sharp decay for the two-dimensional non-rotating primitive equations without thermal diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16029)
*Fengjie Luo,Quan Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: For 2D non-rotating primitive equations with horizontal and vertical viscosity but no thermal diffusion, small doubly periodic $H^s$ perturbations of a stably stratified rest state yield global strong solutions with persistent stratification, an inverse-map conservation law, a unique energy-minimizing limiting equilibrium, and sharp algebraic decay (with matching lower bounds).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand long-time behavior and stability of the primitive equations with viscosity but without thermal diffusion, where the full diagnostic vertical velocity loses derivatives and standard energy estimates do not close directly.

Method: Weighted energy methods, spectral analysis, and inverse-map identities, exploiting the monotone (label) structure of the temperature and rearrangement-type conserved quantities.

Result: Global strong solutions for small compatible doubly periodic $H^s$ ($s\ge4$) data with horizontal velocity and temperature uniformly small and strict stratification persisting; an inverse-map conservation law expressing the terminal temperature as the normalized increasing rearrangement of the initial label; uniqueness of the limiting equilibrium as quadratic-energy minimizer over compatible strictly stratified states with the same label distribution; and sharp algebraic decay in lower-order Sobolev norms for velocity, temperature, and pressure differences, with exact nonlinear solutions giving matching lower bounds.

Conclusion: The paper gives a complete small-data global well-posedness and long-time asymptotic picture for the 2D primitive equations without thermal diffusion, including a conservation law, equilibrium selection principle, and optimal decay rates.

Abstract: We study the two-dimensional non-rotating primitive equations with positive horizontal and vertical viscosity and without thermal diffusion. Our main results are fourfold. First, for every integer \(s\ge4\), we prove that sufficiently small, compatible, doubly periodic \(H^s\) perturbations of a stably stratified rest state with an affine temperature profile generate unique global strong solutions; the horizontal velocity and temperature remain uniformly small at the initial Sobolev order despite the derivative loss in the full diagnostic vertical velocity, and strict stratification persists. Second, we establish an inverse-map conservation law that determines the terminal temperature solely from the initial temperature: the terminal label is identified as the normalized increasing rearrangement of the initial label modulo the vertical period, and the terminal perturbation admits a quadratic expansion with cubic remainder. Third, the limiting equilibrium uniquely minimizes the quadratic perturbation energy among compatible, strictly stratified states with the same periodic label distribution. Finally, we establish sharp algebraic decay in suitable lower-order Sobolev norms for both velocity components and for the temperature and pressure differences from equilibrium. Exact nonlinear solutions provide matching lower bounds, establishing optimality for uniform estimates over the small-data class. The proofs combine weighted energy methods, spectral analysis, and inverse-map identities.

</details>


### [124] [Boundary regularity for fractional porous medium-type equations in bounded domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16113)
*Mengyu Huo,Tianling Jin,Jingang Xiong*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves boundary Hölder regularity for fractional porous medium-type equations and more general nonlocal operators in bounded smooth domains with zero exterior data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Boundary regularity for fractional porous medium and related nonlocal equations is not well understood in bounded domains with zero exterior data; the authors aim to establish sharp boundary Hölder estimates.

Method: They combine comparison principles and boundary barriers with a blow-up and compactness argument, relying on a Liouville theorem for ancient solutions in a half-space. Results cover weak dual solutions of \partial_t v+(-Δ)^σ(v^m)=0 and viscosity solutions of \partial_t u=u^γLu for symmetric stable operators.

Result: For nonnegative weak dual solutions v, v/d^{σ/m} lies in C^{α, αm/(σ(m+1))}_{x,t} up to the boundary away from initial time, for some α∈(0,σ). More generally, boundary Hölder estimates are obtained for u/d^σ for nonnegative viscosity solutions with initial data comparable to d^σ.

Conclusion: The paper establishes boundary Hölder regularity for fractional porous medium-type equations and general symmetric stable operators, showing the natural boundary behavior is governed by the distance to the boundary.

Abstract: We prove boundary Hölder regularity for fractional porous medium-type equations in bounded smooth domains with zero exterior data. For $0<σ<1$, $n>2σ$, and $m>1$, we show that nonnegative weak dual solutions $v$ of $\partial_t v+(-Δ)^σ(v^m)=0$ satisfy \[ \frac{v}{d^{σ/m}}\in C_{x,t}^{α,\frac{αm}{σ(m+1)}} \] up to the boundary and away from the initial time, for some $α\in(0,σ)$, where $d$ denotes the distance to the boundary. More generally, we establish boundary Hölder estimates for $u/d^σ$ for nonnegative viscosity solutions of $\partial_t u=u^γLu$, with $0<γ<1$ and initial data comparable to $d^σ$. Here $L$ is a symmetric stable operator of order $2σ$ whose measurable angular density is bounded above and below by positive constants. Our proof combines comparison principles and boundary barriers with a blow-up and compactness argument based on a Liouville theorem for ancient solutions in a half-space.

</details>


### [125] [Cubic moments of eigenfunctions on surfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16157)
*Xing Wang,Cheng Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper shows Gaussian curvature drives cubic cancellation of Laplace eigenfunctions on Riemannian surfaces, proving sharp decay rates and the cubic Gaussian moment conjecture for negatively curved closed surfaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Cubic integrals of Laplace eigenfunctions are conjectured to decay (Gaussian moment conjecture), but prior results relied on arithmetic or dynamical assumptions. The authors seek a purely local geometric mechanism—Gaussian curvature—that enforces cubic cancellation without such assumptions.

Method: The proof is local and rests on an exact identity relating the reduced phase at a Fermat point to Gaussian curvature. Finite-order nondegeneracy of the curvature yields polynomial decay rates; global decay on hyperbolic surfaces is obtained via bounded covariant derivatives of weights.

Result: Cubic integrals against smooth compactly supported weights vanish whenever curvature is nonzero a.e. where the weight is nonzero. Sharp polynomial rates follow from finite-order nondegeneracy; on closed nowhere-vanishing-curvature surfaces the rate is O(λ^{-1/6}), attained by round-sphere harmonics. On complete hyperbolic surfaces with positive systole and no finite-area assumption, global O((log λ)^{-3/2}) decay holds.

Conclusion: Gaussian curvature is identified as a local mechanism for cubic eigenfunction cancellation, proving the cubic Gaussian moment conjecture for normalized real eigenfunctions on closed negatively curved surfaces and extending decay results to non-finite-area hyperbolic surfaces.

Abstract: We identify Gaussian curvature as a local mechanism for cubic cancellation of Laplace eigenfunctions on Riemannian surfaces, without arithmetic or dynamical assumptions. For $L^2$-normalized local solutions at frequency $λ$, cubic integrals against smooth compactly supported weights tend to zero whenever the curvature is nonzero almost everywhere where the weight is nonzero. Finite-order nondegeneracy yields sharp polynomial decay rates. In particular, on closed surfaces with nowhere-vanishing curvature, we obtain the rate $O(λ^{-1/6})$, attained by spherical harmonics on the round sphere. This proves the cubic case of the Gaussian moment conjecture for all normalized real eigenfunctions on closed negatively curved surfaces. The local proof rests on an exact identity relating the reduced phase at a Fermat point to Gaussian curvature. On complete hyperbolic surfaces with positive systole, we also prove global $O((\logλ)^{-3/2})$ decay for $L^2$-normalized eigenfunctions and smooth weights with bounded covariant derivatives through order two, without assuming finite area.

</details>


### [126] [A counterexample to integral Aleksandrov-Bakelman-Pucci estimates for a nonlocal operator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16162)
*Yongsheng Jiang,Lei Xu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper constructs a counterexample showing that integral Aleksandrov-Bakelman-Pucci (ABP) estimates fail for nonlocal elliptic inequalities with merely bounded, measurable, symmetric coefficients, and hence no $L^p$ maximum estimate holds for any $1\le p<\infty$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Classical ABP estimates and their nonlocal analogues are central tools in regularity theory. For nonlocal operators with measurable coefficients, one might hope that an integral (weak) ABP estimate still holds under uniform ellipticity conditions (C). The paper tests whether such estimates survive without regularity of the coefficient.

Method: For every $n\ge1$, $0<\sigma<2$, and $0<\lambda<\Lambda$, the authors construct a single fixed measurable symmetric coefficient $a(x,y)$ satisfying $\lambda\le a\le\Lambda$. They build a sequence of smooth compactly supported functions $u_j\ge0$ and $f_j\ge0$ with $L_a u_j\ge-f_j$ in $B_1$, $0\le f_j\le1$, and $|\operatorname{supp} f_j|\to0$, yet $u_j\ge d>0$ on $B_{1/4}$. This is achieved via a careful geometric/measure-theoretic construction exploiting the lack of regularity of $a$.

Result: The construction yields a fixed operator for which the forcing terms $f_j$ have vanishing measure of support while the solutions $u_j$ remain bounded below by $d>0$ on $B_{1/4}$. Consequently, the integral ABP estimate fails, and the operator admits no $L^p$ maximum estimate for any $1\le p<\infty$.

Conclusion: Nonlocal ABP-type integral estimates do not hold for general bounded measurable symmetric kernels. Regularity assumptions on the coefficient are essential; in their absence, maximum estimates in $L^p$ fail completely.

Abstract: We construct a counterexample to integral Aleksandrov-Bakelman-Pucci estimates for nonlocal inequalities of the form \begin{equation*}
  L_au(x):=\int_{\mathbb R^n}δ_yu(x)
  \frac{a(x,y)}{|y|^{n+σ}}\,dy
  \ge-f(x)\quad\text{in }B_1, \end{equation*} with $u\le0$ in $\mathbb R^n\setminus B_1$, where $δ_yu(x)=u(x+y)+u(x-y)-2u(x)$, and the measurable coefficient satisfies \begin{equation}\tag{C}
  λ\le a(x,y)\leΛ,\qquad
  a(x,y)=a(x,-y),\qquad
  x\in\mathbb R^n,\quad y\ne0. \end{equation} More precisely, for every $n\ge1$, $0<σ<2$, and $0<λ<Λ$, there exist a fixed coefficient $a(x,y)$ satisfying \textup{(C)}, a constant $d>0$, and nonnegative functions $u_j,f_j\in C_c^\infty(B_1)$ such that $L_au_j\ge-f_j$ in $B_1$, $0\le f_j\le1$, and $|\operatorname{supp}f_j|\to0$ as $j\to\infty$, while $u_j\ge d$ on $B_{1/4}$ for every $j$. Consequently, this fixed operator admits no $L^p$ maximum estimate for any $1\le p<\infty$.

</details>


### [127] [C^(sigma+alpha) regularity for fractional Hamilton-Jacobi equations, and rates of convergence in periodic homogenization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16211)
*Adina Ciomaga,Andrei Rodriguez-Paredes,Erwin Topp*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes C^{σ+α} regularity for nonlocal Bellman equations with state-dependent index sets and applies it to prove quantitative periodic homogenization for stationary nonlocal Hamilton-Jacobi equations, achieving convergence rate ε^{σα/(2+α)}.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Regularity of solutions when the control set depends on the state variable is not covered by existing theories. Homogenization of nonlocal Bellman equations with kernels depending on slow variable, fast variable, and control lacked rigorous quantitative convergence results.

Method: Develops a C^{σ+α} regularity theory for nonlocal Bellman equations with Hölder continuous state-dependent index sets, extending Caffarelli-Silvestre and Serra Schauder estimates. Uses these estimates as compactness source to construct the effective problem's solution. Combines regularity with discounted approximate correctors to make the perturbed test function method quantitative.

Result: Effective equation is a nonlocal Bellman equation with supremum over viscosity Mather measures varying Hölder continuously with space. Constructs a classical solution of the effective problem, unique among bounded classical solutions. Proves entire family of solutions converges to the effective solution with explicit rate ε^{σα/(2+α)}.

Conclusion: The work extends Schauder-type regularity to state-dependent nonlocal Bellman equations and delivers quantitative homogenization with explicit rate, uniting regularity theory and homogenization in a general framework.

Abstract: We study two related questions for nonlocal equations of Bellman type: the regularity of solutions when the set of controls depends on the state variable, and the periodic homogenization of stationary nonlocal Hamilton-Jacobi equations driven by uniformly elliptic integro-differential operators of order $σ\in(0,2)$, whose kernels depend simultaneously on the slow variable, the fast periodic variable and the control. The effective equation is again a nonlocal Bellman equation, but its supremum runs over a set of viscosity Mather measures that varies with the space variable, in a merely Hölder continuous way. We first develop a $C^{σ+α}$ regularity theory for nonlocal Bellman equations with Hölder continuous, state-dependent index sets, extending the Schauder estimates of Caffarelli-Silvestre and Serra, in a general framework independent of homogenization. We then use these estimates as a source of compactness to construct a classical solution of the effective problem, unique among bounded classical solutions. Finally, combining this regularity with discounted approximate correctors, we make the perturbed test function method quantitative, and prove that the whole family of solutions converges to the effective solution, with the explicit rate $ε^{σα/(2+α)}$, where $α$ is the Hölder exponent of the data.

</details>


### [128] [Sharp decay characterization for the Oldroyd-B system with only velocity dissipation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16228)
*Zhi Chen,Mingwen Fei,Lvqiao Liu,Jiahong Wu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes a sharp large-time decay characterization for the incompressible Oldroyd-B system with velocity as the only dissipated quantity, identifying the active pair U=(u, Λ^{-1} P div τ) as the correct decaying observable in a critical hybrid Besov framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: In the studied regime, the stress tensor undergoes neither relaxation nor diffusion, so the operator P div on symmetric stresses has an infinite-dimensional kernel of exact stationary states. Consequently, no decay estimate can hold for the full stress tensor, and existing sharp-decay theory for dissipative systems does not apply.

Method: The authors isolate the active pair U=(u, Λ^{-1} P div τ) as the appropriate decaying observable and work within a critical hybrid Besov framework to analyze its large-time asymptotics.

Result: For -d/2<σ_1<σ≤d/2-1, they prove \|U(t)\|_{H^σ}=O(t^{-(σ-σ_1)/2}) iff the low-frequency part of U_0 lies in \dot B^{σ_1}_{2,∞}. For σ<d/2-1, they show the two-sided estimate \|U(t)\|_{H^σ}≃t^{-(σ-σ_1)/2} iff the low-frequency part of U_0 lies in a nondegenerate subclass 𝔅^{σ_1}_{2,∞} of \dot B^{σ_1}_{2,∞}.

Conclusion: The paper provides a complete characterization of the large-time behavior of the active pair for the Oldroyd-B system in the velocity-only dissipation regime, giving sharp decay rates and identifying the precise low-frequency regularity class governing them.

Abstract: We study the incompressible Oldroyd-B system on $\mathbb R^d$, $d\ge2$, in the regime in which the velocity is the only dissipated quantity: the stress tensor undergoes neither relaxation nor diffusion. In this regime the operator $\mathbb P\operatorname{div}$ acting on symmetric stresses has an infinite-dimensional kernel consisting of exact stationary states, so no decay estimate can hold for the full stress tensor and the existing sharp-decay theory for dissipative systems does not apply. We identify the active pair $U=(u,Λ^{-1}\mathbb P\operatorname{div}τ)$ as the correct decaying observable and establish, in a critical hybrid Besov framework, a complete characterization of its large-time behavior. For $-d/2<σ_1<σ\le d/2-1$ we prove that $\|U(t)\|_{\mathcal H^σ}=O(t^{-(σ-σ_1)/2})$ holds if and only if the low-frequency part of $U_0$ belongs to $\dot B^{σ_1}_{2,\infty}$, and, for $σ<d/2-1$, that the two-sided estimate $\|U(t)\|_{\mathcal H^σ}\simeq t^{-(σ-σ_1)/2}$ holds if and only if the low-frequency part of $U_0$ belongs to a nondegenerate subclass $\mathfrak B^{σ_1}_{2,\infty}$ of $\dot B^{σ_1}_{2,\infty}$.

</details>


### [129] [Weighted and Sobolev Estimates for Fractional Schrödinger Operators with Reverse Hölder Potentials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16274)
*Aparajita Dasgupta,Drishti Garg*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes weighted and Sobolev estimates for two fractional Schrödinger operators $(-\Delta)^s+V$ and $(-\Delta+V)^s$ on $\mathbb{R}^n$ ($n\geq3$, $0<s<1$), covering heat-kernel bounds, semigroup maximal and Hardy–Littlewood–Sobolev estimates with weighted strong/weak endpoints, and mapping properties of $\nabla Q^{-1/2}$ on Hardy–Sobolev spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper seeks to extend classical weighted theory and Sobolev mapping results—well developed for Schrödinger operators and the fractional Laplacian—to fractional Schrödinger settings with both additive potentials $(-\Delta)^s+V$ and spectral powers $(-\Delta+V)^s$, where sharp kernel decay and critical-radius structure are less understood.

Method: The main tool is the fractional critical radius $\rho_s$ and a decomposition at that scale. For $L=(-\Delta)^s+V$ with $V\in RH_q$ nonnegative and nontrivial, the authors prove a critical-radius form inequality and semigroup mass decay, yielding a heat-kernel bound mixing polynomial off-diagonal decay with a fixed positive exponent relative to $\rho_s$. This yields weighted strong-type bounds for the semigroup maximal operator and weighted Hardy–Littlewood–Sobolev estimates for $L^{-\alpha/2}$ ($0<\alpha s<n$) with endpoint weak-type versions, under weights with explicit growth restrictions. For $Q=(-\Delta+V)^s$ with $V\in RH_{q_1}$, $n/(1+s)\leq q_1<n$ and $\rho_1(x)\geq1$, they use Hardy–Sobolev spaces and duality to handle $\nabla Q^{-1/2}$.

Result: For $L=(-\Delta)^s+V$, the authors obtain a heat-kernel bound combining polynomial off-diagonal decay with a fixed positive decay exponent relative to $\rho_s$, plus weighted strong-type estimates for the semigroup maximal operator and weighted Hardy–Littlewood–Sobolev estimates for $L^{-\alpha/2}$ ($0<\alpha s<n$) with corresponding endpoint weak-type estimates. For $Q=(-\Delta+V)^s$, they prove $\nabla Q^{-1/2}$ maps $H^{1-s,p}(\mathbb{R}^n)\to L^p(\mathbb{R}^n)$ for $1<p\leq p_0$, $1/p_0=1/q_1-1/n$, and by duality its adjoint maps $L^{p'}(\mathbb{R}^n)\to H^{s-1,p'}(\mathbb{R}^n)$ for $p_0'\leq p'<\infty$.

Conclusion: The fractional critical-radius scale provides a unified framework for weighted and Sobolev estimates of fractional Schrödinger operators, yielding kernel decay, weighted maximal and Hardy–Littlewood–Sobolev bounds, and sharp Hardy–Sobolev mapping for $\nabla Q^{-1/2}$ under reverse Hölder assumptions on the potential.

Abstract: We study weighted and Sobolev estimates for two fractional Schrödinger operators on $\mathbb{R}^n$, where $n\geq3$ and $0<s<1$. For the additive operator $L=(-Δ)^s+V$, with a nonnegative, nontrivial potential $V$ belonging to a suitable reverse Hölder class, $RH_q$, we establish a critical-radius form inequality and decay estimates for the semigroup mass. These yield a heat-kernel bound combining polynomial off-diagonal decay with a fixed positive exponent of decay relative to the fractional critical radius $ρ_s$. A decomposition at this scale gives weighted strong-type estimates for the semigroup maximal operator and weighted Hardy--Littlewood--Sobolev estimates for $L^{-α/2}$, $0<αs<n$, together with the corresponding endpoint weak-type estimates. The admissible weights satisfy explicit growth restrictions determined by the kernel decay, and the fractional integration order is $αs$. We also consider the spectral operator $Q=(-Δ+V)^s$. Assuming $V\in RH_{q_1}$ with $n/(1+s)\leq q_1<n$ and classical critical radius $ρ_1(x)\geq1$, we prove that $\nabla Q^{-1/2}$ maps $H^{1-s,p}(\mathbb{R}^n)$ into $L^p(\mathbb{R}^n)$ for $1<p\leq p_0$, where $1/p_0=1/q_1-1/n$. By duality, its adjoint
  maps $L^{p^{'}}(\mathbb{R}^n)$ into $H^{s-1,p^{'}}(\mathbb{R}^n)$ for $p_0^{'}\leq p^{'}<\infty$.

</details>


### [130] [Some remarks on nonexistence in the Gamow liquid drop model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16306)
*Dario Mazzoleni,Aldo Pratelli,Berardo Ruffini*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies the Gamow liquid drop model variational problem, focusing on non-existence of minimizers for large masses, proving an optimal thickness property for asymptotic minimizers and showing non-existence for large masses via a cube-tiling reduction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Gamow liquid drop model is a celebrated variational problem balancing perimeter and Riesz-type nonlocal attraction. Understanding whether minimizers exist—particularly for large masses—is a central question, as existence/non-existence governs the structure of optimal sets in this model.

Method: The authors analyze the minimization of P(Ω) + V_α(Ω) under a volume constraint |Ω|=m. They first prove that any potential minimizer in the asymptotic (large mass) regime must satisfy an optimal thickness property. They then restrict to a simplified setting where sets are tilings of cubes and analyze this reduced problem across the full parameter range N≥2, α∈(0,N).

Result: Any possible minimizer in the asymptotic regime must satisfy an optimal thickness property. In the simplified cube-tiling setting, non-existence of minimizers is shown to occur for large masses for the entire range of parameters N≥2 and α∈(0,N).

Conclusion: The results provide evidence that minimizers for the Gamow liquid drop model fail to exist for sufficiently large masses, with the cube-tiling analysis establishing non-existence across all relevant dimensions and interaction exponents.

Abstract: In this paper we deal with the celebrated Gamow liquid drop model, namely, for $m>0$, $N\geq 2$ and $α\in (0,N)$, we study the problem \[ \min\Big\{P(Ω)+ V_α(Ω) : Ω\subset \mathbb{R}^N,\;|Ω|=m\Big\}\,, \] and focus mostly on the nonexistence of minimizers for large masses. First we show that any possible minimizer in the asymptotic regime must satisfy an optimal thickness property. Eventually, in the simplified setting of sets made up as a tiling of cubes, we show that nonexistence occurs for large masses for the whole range of parameters $N\geq 2$ and $α\in (0,N)$.

</details>


### [131] [One-dimensional pressureless flow with a nonlinear velocity law: Bregman variational construction and well-posedness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16341)
*Gaowei Cao,Feimin Huang,Guirong Tang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves global existence, uniqueness, and quantitative W_∞ stability for 1D pressureless flow with transport velocity f(u) (f'>0), allowing measure-valued mass and cluster collisions, via a Bregman variational scheme and Lagrangian representation under an Oleinik-type condition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Cluster collisions are subtle: mass and momentum conservation yield a mass-weighted average velocity, but transport follows the nonlinear law f applied to that average. Prior theory lacks global existence, uniqueness, and stability for this general f(u) transport in the pressureless setting with measure-valued, possibly atomic initial data.

Method: A Bregman variational time discretization whose cost reproduces the transport velocity from the mass-weighted average, coupled with conditional-expectation updates of physical velocity. Existence of global entropy solutions; Lagrangian representation for entropy solutions satisfying an Oleinik condition on transport velocity and initial weak continuity of the kinetic energy measure; uniqueness by comparing flows with possibly different cluster intervals; W_∞ comparison in mass coordinates.

Result: Global entropy solutions exist. Uniqueness holds within the described class. For two solutions with same velocity law and common mass, a W_∞ estimate for normalized mass measures is obtained in terms of initial position and physical-velocity discrepancies in mass coordinates, provided the initial W_∞ distance is finite. The Lagrangian evolution is a semigroup.

Conclusion: The framework gives a complete well-posedness and stability theory for 1D pressureless flow with nonlinear transport velocity, handling atomic and unbounded mass distributions. The Lagrangian-semigroup structure and W_∞ stability provide a foundation for further analysis of clustering dynamics.

Abstract: We establish global existence, uniqueness and quantitative stability for one-dimensional pressureless flow governed by \[ \partial_tρ+\partial_x\bigl(ρf(u)\bigr)=0, \qquad \partial_t(ρu)+\partial_x\bigl(ρu f(u)\bigr)=0, \] where $ρ$ is the mass density, possibly measure-valued, $u$ is the physical velocity, and $f\in C^1(\mathbb R)$, with $f'>0$, determines the transport velocity $f(u)$. The initial mass has finite positive mass and finite second moment, allowing atoms and unbounded support, and the initial physical velocity is bounded. The difficulty at a cluster collision is that mass and momentum conservation determine a mass-weighted average of physical velocity, whereas transport is governed by the nonlinear law $f$ applied to that average. We construct global entropy solutions by a Bregman variational time discretization whose cost is chosen to reproduce the transport velocity determined by this mass-weighted average, coupled with conditional-expectation updates of physical velocity. For every entropy solution satisfying the Oleinik condition on the transport velocity and initial weak continuity of the kinetic energy measure, we establish a Lagrangian representation and prove uniqueness within this class by comparing flows with possibly different cluster intervals. For two such solutions with the same velocity law and common mass, we obtain a $W_\infty$ estimate for the normalized mass measures in terms of the initial position and physical-velocity discrepancies in mass coordinates, whenever the initial $W_\infty$ distance is finite. The Lagrangian evolution has the semigroup property.

</details>


### [132] [Spectral stability of resonant Y-solitons for the KP-II equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16378)
*Tetsu Mizumachi*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Using Darboux transformations, this paper proves spectral stability of Y-shaped line solitons in the KP-II equation within an exponentially weighted space capturing waves receding behind the solitons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: KP-II was introduced to explain transverse stability of line solitary waves in shallow water; the stability of more complex Y-shaped line solitons needed rigorous treatment.

Method: Darboux transformations; spectral analysis of the linearized operator; exponentially weighted spaces; analytic dependence of pairings with adjoint eigenfunctions on spectral parameters to reduce orthogonality conditions.

Result: The linearized operator's spectrum comprises the continuous spectrum of the zero background plus three residual spectral domains whose boundaries contain continuous spectra tied to constituent line solitons. Orthogonality conditions reduce to conditions against three families of adjoint continuous eigenfunctions.

Conclusion: Spectral stability of Y-shaped line solitons is established, and linear stability is further examined in a subspace satisfying orthogonality conditions against adjoint continuous eigenfunctions associated with outgoing modulations along each constituent line soliton.

Abstract: The KP-II equation was introduced by Kadomtsev and Petviashvili to explain the transverse stability of line solitary waves in shallow water. Using Darboux transformations, we prove the spectral stability of Y-shaped line solitons in an exponentially weighted space that captures the amount of waves receding behind the line solitons. We show that the spectrum of the linearized operator consists of the continuous spectrum of the zero background together with three residual spectral domains whose boundaries contain continuous spectra associated with the constituent line solitons. Exploiting the analytic dependence of the pairings with adjoint eigenfunctions on the spectral parameters, we prove that the orthogonality conditions associated with all adjoint eigenfunctions can be reduced to orthogonality conditions against three families of adjoint continuous eigenfunctions. Moreover, we investigate linear stability of Y-solitons in a subspace satisfying orthogonality conditions against adjoint continuous eigenfunctions associated with outgoing modulations along each line soliton constituting the Y-soliton.

</details>


### [133] [Boundedness and exponential stabilization in a degenerate migration-consumption system with logistic damping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16399)
*Ai Huang,Jing Li,Yifu Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves uniform L∞×W1,∞ boundedness and exponential convergence to a homogeneous state for a degenerate taxis-type migration-consumption system with logistic damping, without smallness assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Previous results only established L2-boundedness for such degenerate taxis systems, and uniform elliptic regularization is lost due to the motility function vanishing at zero. The authors aim to show that logistic damping can drive the system toward homogeneity despite this degeneracy.

Method: A weighted Gagliardo–Nirenberg type inequality adapted to the algebraic degeneracy of order α≥1, combined with the dissipative structure of the signal equation.

Result: Uniform boundedness in L∞(Ω)×W1,∞(Ω) for every fixed damping coefficient b>0, with no smallness assumption on initial data, improving prior L2-boundedness. For α>1, exponential convergence to (a/b,0) with an explicit admissible rate.

Conclusion: Logistic damping drives the system toward a spatially homogeneous steady state even when uniform elliptic regularization is lost, extending boundedness and convergence theory for degenerate taxis-type systems.

Abstract: We study the no-flux problem for a taxis-type migration-consumption system with logistic damping in a bounded planar domain, where the motility function \(φ\) vanishes at zero and degenerates algebraically of order \(α\ge1\). We prove uniform boundedness in \(L^\infty(Ω)\times W^{1,\infty}(Ω)\) for every fixed damping coefficient \(b>0\), without any smallness assumption on the initial data, thus improving the previous \(L^2\)-boundedness results. For \(α>1\), we further prove that the solution converges exponentially to the spatially homogeneous state \((a/b,0)\), with an explicit admissible rate. The proof combines a weighted Gagliardo--Nirenberg type inequality adapted to the degeneracy with the dissipative structure of the signal equation. Therefore logistic damping drives the system toward homogeneity, despite the loss of uniform elliptic regularization.

</details>


### [134] [The Inviscid Limit of Viscous Burgers through Shock Formation and Development](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16420)
*Chao Wang,Yueyi Yang,Zhifei Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper extends the analysis of the inviscid limit of the 1D viscous Burgers equation beyond the shock-formation time, proving a pointwise error bound between the viscous solution and the inviscid entropy solution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Chaturvedi and Graham obtained sharp order ν^{1/4} estimates only up to the shock-formation time. This work extends the comparison beyond that time, where a viscous shock layer develops.

Method: Matching of an outer expansion, an inner expansion near the formation point, and a viscous shock-profile expansion.

Result: With shock-formation time normalized to t=0, a pointwise error bound on 0 ≤ t ≤ ε̄ (ε̄ independent of ν) is proven, consisting of an order ν^{1/4} term plus an exponentially localized viscous shock-layer contribution.

Conclusion: Combined with Chaturvedi–Graham, this gives a quantitative description of the inviscid limit from initial time through the development of the young shock.

Abstract: We study the inviscid limit of the one-dimensional viscous Burgers equation from smooth initial data through nondegenerate shock formation and subsequent shock development. Chaturvedi and Graham \cite{CG} obtained sharp estimates of order $ν^{1/4}$ up to the shock-formation time. We extend their analysis beyond this time, comparing the viscous solution with the inviscid entropy solution evolving from the same initial data. With the shock-formation time normalized to $t=0$, we prove a pointwise error bound on a fixed interval $0\leq t \leq \bar{\varepsilon}$, where $\bar{\varepsilon}>0$ is independent of the viscosity $ν$. The bound consists of a term of order $ν^{1/4}$ and an exponentially localized contribution originating from the viscous shock layer. The proof is based on matching an outer expansion, an inner expansion near the formation point, and a viscous shock-profile expansion. Combined with the results of Chaturvedi and Graham, this yields a quantitative description of the inviscid limit from the initial time through the development of the young shock.

</details>


### [135] [Radial pressureless Euler flow: structure and stability of absorption at the origin](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16436)
*Gaowei Cao,Feimin Huang,Guirong Tang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Global well-posedness and structure of absorbed mass at the origin for radial pressureless Euler flow in R^n are established, including a Lagrangian projection characterization, Stieltjes impulse identities, stability under data convergence, and realizability of arbitrary/ singular-continuous absorption measures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Radial pressureless Euler flow can funnel mass into the origin, producing permanent absorption (sticky dynamics) that is not well understood, especially for general nonnegative locally finite initial mass and for the structure/regularity of the absorbed mass measure; the paper aims to characterize existence, uniqueness, and the precise nature of absorption.

Method: Works in a full-space radial entropy class with bounded radial velocity vanishing at origin, one-sided Lipschitz bound, and an initial kinetic-energy trace; proves global existence/uniqueness. For finite positive mass with finite second moment, uses a Lagrangian projection representation via extreme minimizers of a free-position primitive, derives a radial scalar momentum balance with a nonnegative Stieltjes boundary impulse measure, and proves absolute continuity relations and stability results under strong L^2 convergence of quantiles/velocities and origin masses.

Result: Proves global existence and uniqueness; absorption is permanent and retains any initial origin atom. Characterizes absorbed mass and its left limits through extreme minimizers; establishes a weighted Stieltjes identity linking time-weighted impulse to initial radii over absorbed labels (valid for jumps and singular continuous absorption), with boundary impulse and absorption measures mutually absolutely continuous on (0,∞). Shows stability: strong L^2 convergence of initial quantiles/Lagrangian velocities plus origin-mass convergence yields convergence of absorbed mass at continuity times and local weak convergence of absorption measures. Shows any compactly supported probability measure on (0,∞) is realizable as an absorption measure with fixed smooth initial density by choosing bounded radial velocity, so purely singular continuous absorption occurs.

Conclusion: Radial pressureless Euler absorption at the origin admits a sharp global theory: well-posed dynamics, exact Lagrangian/Stieltjes descriptions of absorbed mass, stability under approximations, and extreme flexibility (arbitrary, including purely singular continuous) of absorption measures—highlighting fundamental non-uniqueness/irregularity at the level of absorbed mass distributions.

Abstract: We study absorption at the origin in radial pressureless Euler flow in $\mathbb{R}^n$, $n\geq2$. For rotation-invariant nonnegative locally finite initial mass and bounded radial initial velocity vanishing at the origin, we prove global existence and uniqueness in a full-space radial entropy class with a one-sided Lipschitz bound and an initial kinetic-energy trace. Absorption is permanent, including retention of any initial origin atom.
  For finite positive mass with finite second moment, a Lagrangian projection representation characterizes the absorbed mass and its left limits at positive times through extreme minimizers of a free-position primitive. We derive a radial scalar momentum balance with a nonnegative Stieltjes boundary impulse measure. The minimum value of the same primitive yields a weighted Stieltjes identity equating accumulated time-weighted impulse with the integral of initial radii over absorbed labels, valid also for jumps and singular continuous absorption; the boundary impulse measure and the absorption measure are mutually absolutely continuous on $(0,\infty)$.
  For such finite-mass data with common total mass, strong $L^2$ convergence of initial quantiles and Lagrangian velocities, together with convergence of initial origin masses, yields convergence of absorbed mass at continuity times of the limiting absorption and local weak convergence of absorption measures on $(0,\infty)$. Finally, even with a fixed smooth initial radial probability density, every probability measure compactly supported in $(0,\infty)$ can be realized as the absorption measure by choosing a bounded radial initial velocity; in particular, purely singular continuous absorption occurs.

</details>


### [136] [A geometric lower bound for the clamped plate eigenvalue problem in low dimensions and extensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16485)
*Francesca Bianchi,Francesco Bozzola,Lorenzo Brasco*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The Dirichlet-biLaplacian spectrum is quantitatively lower-bounded in terms of inradius only, for general open sets in dimensions 2 and 3, and this is extended to fractional Laplacian powers s with N/2 < s < 2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish inradius-based lower bounds for the spectrum of the Dirichlet-biLaplacian (and its fractional counterparts), generalizing classical Laplacian spectral bounds to general open sets in low dimensions.

Method: Capacitary techniques in the vein of Maz'ya.

Result: Quantitative lower bounds on the Dirichlet-biLaplacian spectrum via inradius in dimensions 2 and 3; extension to fractional powers s of the Laplacian with N/2 < s < 2; the constant has correct asymptotic behavior as s approaches N/2 and 2.

Conclusion: Inradius alone controls the lower spectrum of the Dirichlet-biLaplacian and its fractional powers in dimensions 2 and 3, with sharp constants at the endpoints.

Abstract: We show that the spectrum of the Dirichlet-biLaplacian can be quantitatively bounded from below in terms of the inradius only, for a general open set in dimensions $2$ and $3$. For the same dimensions, we also extend this result to fractional powers $s$ of the Laplacian, with $N/2<s<2$. The resulting estimate comes with a constant having the correct asymptotic behavior as $s$ approaches the two endpoints $N/2$ and $2$. The proofs exploit capacitary techniques in the vein of Maz'ya.

</details>


### [137] [Asymptotic stability and sharp decay near homogeneous solutions of a nonlinearly damped Schrödinger equation on the torus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16526)
*Boris Shakarov*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Global existence and asymptotic convergence to an explicit homogeneous solution for the 1D periodic NLS with power-type nonlinear damping, in both focusing and defocusing regimes, with explicit decay rates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the long-time dynamics (adiabatic decay) of the 1D periodic nonlinear Schrödinger equation subject to power-type nonlinear damping, and to characterize convergence to homogeneous states for small damping and near-homogeneous initial data.

Method: Perturbative analysis around a nonzero spatially homogeneous state with a sufficiently small damping parameter; study initial data close in relative H^1 norm; derive adiabatic decay laws and establish convergence after a constant phase adjustment.

Result: For small damping and near-homogeneous data, prove global existence and asymptotic convergence to an explicit homogeneous solution. L^2 and H^1 norms follow the explicit adiabatic decay law with the same leading coefficient as the homogeneous solution. Explicit convergence rates are obtained, including a t^{-1/2} bound for the relative spatial perturbation. For every nonconstant datum nearby, the kinetic energy has a strictly positive leading coefficient and decays one power of time faster than the squared L^2 norm.

Conclusion: The nonlinear damping drives solutions toward a homogeneous state with quantifiable adiabatic decay, and nonconstant perturbations enhance decay (faster kinetic energy decay versus the squared L^2 norm), for both focusing and defocusing nonlinearities.

Abstract: We study the one-dimensional periodic nonlinear Schrödinger equation with a power-type nonlinear damping, both in the focusing and the defocusing regime. For a sufficiently small damping parameter and initial data close in relative $H^1$ norm to a nonzero spatially homogeneous state, we prove global existence and asymptotic convergence, after a constant phase adjustment, to an explicit homogeneous solution. The \(L^2\) and \(H^1\) norms follow the explicit adiabatic decay law, with the same leading coefficient as the homogeneous solution. We obtain explicit convergence rates, including a \(t^{-1/2}\) bound for the relative spatial perturbation. For every nonconstant initial datum in a small neighborhood of the homogeneous solution, the kinetic energy has a strictly positive leading coefficient and decays one power of time faster than the squared \(L^2\) norm.

</details>


### [138] [Minimal time for heat observability on almost-Riemannian surfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16568)
*Vincent Boulard,Amaury Hayat,Emmanuel Trélat*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper determines the minimal observation time for the heat equation on a closed almost-Riemannian surface, deriving a complex-phase formula for concentration near singular (Baouendi-Grushin) circles and extending it to the Schrödinger observation time.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Observation/observability on almost-Riemannian surfaces is complicated by singularities along simple Baouendi-Grushin circles, where the structure and density are analytic but coefficients may depend on both variables. Existing thresholds were only known for separated models, so a general minimal-observation-time characterization near such singular sets was needed.

Method: The authors analyze observation sets that leave sufficiently thin collars around simple Baouendi-Grushin circles, exploiting analyticity of the structure and density near these circles. They derive the minimal observation time via a complex phase describing concentration near the singular set, construct quasimodes concentrated near simple singular circles with exponentially small errors, and use their superpositions to obtain lower bounds.

Result: The minimal observation time is expressed through a complex phase reflecting concentration near the singular set, extending known thresholds for separated models. Regular unobserved regions away from the circles do not alter this time provided all other singularities are observed. For singular circles with constant bracket step greater than two, local analyticity implies failure of observability at every time for observation sets at positive distance. Quasimode superpositions yield a lower bound for the Schrödinger observation time.

Conclusion: Observability near Baouendi-Grushin singular circles is governed by an explicit complex-phase time threshold; higher bracket-step circles with positive-distance observation sets lose observability entirely, and quasimode constructions transfer these results to the Schrödinger setting.

Abstract: We determine the minimal observation time for the heat equation on a closed almost-Riemannian surface, for a class of observation sets leaving sufficiently thin collars around simple Baouendi-Grushin circles. The structure and density are analytic near these circles, and the coefficients may depend on both variables. The formula is expressed through a complex phase describing concentration near the singular set and extends the thresholds known for separated models. Regular unobserved regions away from the circles do not change this time, provided all other singularities are observed. For singular circles of constant bracket step greater than two, local analyticity implies failure of observability at every time for observation sets at positive distance. We also construct quasimodes concentrated near simple singular circles, with exponentially small errors. Their superpositions give a lower bound for the Schrödinger observation time.

</details>


### [139] [Rigidity results for overdetermined problems of p-Laplace equation with spatially dependent data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16686)
*Xi Dai*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves that an overdetermined problem for the p-Laplacian with position-dependent data forces the domain to be a ball, the source radial, and the Neumann datum constant, using a new boundary-local moving-plane argument that avoids analyzing the p-Laplacian's degenerate set.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Overdetermined problems with two boundary conditions typically force symmetry of the domain. Classic moving-plane arguments struggle with the p-Laplace operator due to its possible degeneracy or singularity, so a technique is needed that sidesteps analyzing the degenerate set.

Method: A boundary-local version of the moving-plane (reflection) argument is developed for the possibly degenerate p-Laplace operator. This approach avoids analyzing the degenerate set. Under suitable structural conditions on the data, rigidity is derived.

Result: Under reasonable structural conditions, solvability forces Ω to be a ball, f to be radial, and g to be a suitable constant; an explicit formula for the solution is given. Further consequences include one-directional domain symmetry, full rigidity from reflection information in directions whose linear reflections generate a dense subgroup of O(n), and a constant mean curvature characterization.

Conclusion: The overdetermined p-Laplacian problem exhibits strong rigidity: the domain must be a ball with radial source and constant Neumann datum. The boundary-local moving-plane technique provides a general tool for degenerate quasilinear operators.

Abstract: Let $p>1$ and $Ω\subset\mathbb{R}^n$ be a bounded smooth domain. We study the following overdetermined problem for the p-Laplacian: \begin{equation*} \left\{ \begin{array}{ll} -Δ_p u=f(x)&x\inΩ,\\ u=0&x\in\partialΩ,\\ \frac{\partial u}{\partialν}=g(x)&x\in\partialΩ, \end{array} \right. \end{equation*} where $ν$ is the inward unit normal to $\partialΩ$ and both data may depend on position. A boundary-local version of the moving-plane argument is developed for the possibly degenerate p-Laplace operator. The advantage of this argument is that it avoids the analysis of the degenerate set of the p-Laplacian. Under some reasonable structural conditions, we prove that the solvability of the above mentioned problem forces the domain to be a ball, the source to be radial, and the Neumann datum to be a suitable constant. We also give an explicit formula for the solution. Further consequences include one directional domain symmetry, the conclusion that full rigidity follows from reflection information in any set of directions whose linear reflections generate a dense subgroup of the orthogonal group $O(n)$, and a constant mean curvature characterization.

</details>


### [140] [Surface-to-Volume Homogenization of NonLinear Steklov Eigencouples with NonHomogeneous Orlicz Growth in Perforated Domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16693)
*Hermann Douanla,Hubert Nnang,Joel Fotso Tachago,Jean Louis Woukeng*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies normalized principal Steklov eigencouples on periodically perforated domains with Orlicz-type bulk and boundary growth; it establishes convergence of constrained minima and (sub)convergence of minimizers, fluxes and multipliers to a homogenized problem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Eigenvalue problems on perforated domains are relevant for homogenization, and Steklov-type boundary conditions with critical surface-to-volume scaling are delicate. Generalizing to possibly nonhomogeneous Orlicz growth extends classical power-growth results.

Method: Analysis of constrained minimization problems for a family of test functions, using a strong approximation of microscopic traces by cell averages measured in the scaled surface modular; passage to the limit identifies the effective bulk operator via a convex cell problem and converts the boundary term into a volume term weighted by the total surface density.

Result: For each fixed mass level, constrained minima converge along the whole family; selected minimizers, fluxes and Lagrange multipliers converge along subsequences to the corresponding objects of the homogenized problem. The limit bulk operator is a convex cell problem, and the boundary term becomes a volume term weighted by the total surface density of the reference hole.

Conclusion: The homogenization of normalized principal Steklov eigencouples extends to general Orlicz growth with critical surface-to-volume scaling, with the limit problem explicitly characterized.

Abstract: We study normalized principal Steklov eigencouples on periodically perforated domains when both the bulk energy and the boundary normalization have general, possibly nonhomogeneous, Orlicz growth. The surface term on the hole boundaries carries the critical surface-to-volume scaling. For each fixed mass level, the constrained minima converge along the whole family, while selected minimizers, fluxes and Lagrange multipliers converge along subsequences to the corresponding objects of a homogenized problem. In the limit, the bulk operator is given by a convex cell problem, and the boundary term becomes a volume term weighted by the total surface density of the reference hole. The key tool is a strong approximation of the microscopic traces by cell averages, measured in the scaled surface modular.

</details>


### [141] [Well-posedness for the spatially inhomogeneous Fermi-Dirac-Fokker-Planck equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16700)
*Gayrat Toshpulatov*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes global well-posedness (existence and uniqueness) of entropy solutions to the space-inhomogeneous Fermi-Dirac-Fokker-Planck equation without smallness or close-to-equilibrium assumptions, via a vanishing viscosity approximation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Previous results on this equation were limited to smallness or near-equilibrium regimes due to the nonlinear, degenerate parabolic structure and the nonlinear transport term f(1-f). This work aims to remove those restrictive assumptions.

Method: A vanishing viscosity approach adds a spatial Laplacian to the equation. Uniform estimates for the regularized problem yield compactness in BV, enabling passage to the limit. A notion of entropy solutions respecting the kinetic structure is formulated for the degenerate limit equation.

Result: Existence and uniqueness of global solutions are proven. Every vanishing-viscosity limit satisfies the entropy formulation, and uniqueness holds within this entropy class.

Conclusion: The paper provides a global well-posedness theory for the Fermi-Dirac-Fokker-Planck equation beyond the perturbative regime.

Abstract: This paper is concerned with space inhomogeneous Fermi-Dirac-Fokker-Planck equation posed on a classical kinetic phase space. The nonlinear factor $f(1-f)$ appears both in the transport term and in the collison part of the Fokker-Planck operator, accounting for the exclusion principle of fermions. Solutions are subject to the physical constraint $0\leq f\leq 1$.
  Previous existing results have mainly been obtained under suitable smallness or close-to-equilibrium assumptions. A main difficulty stems from the nonlinear and degenerate parabolic structure of the equation. We prove the existence and uniqueness of global solutions without any smallness or close-to-equilibrium assumptions. To construct solutions, we use a vanishing viscosity approximation by adding a Laplacian in the spatial variable. We derive uniform estimates for the regularized problem and obtain compactness in the space of functions of bounded variation $(BV),$ allowing us to pass to the limit as the spatial viscosity vanishes. Since the limiting equation is degenerate with a nonlinear transport, we formulate an appropriate notion of entropy solutions incorporating the kinetic structure of the equation. We prove uniqueness within this class and show that every vanishing-viscosity limit satisfies the entropy formulation. Our result provides a global well-posedness theory beyond the perturbative regime.

</details>


### [142] [Sharp Thresholds for Linear Determinacy and Zero Wave Speed in Lotka-Volterra Competition-Diffusion Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16748)
*Shizhao Ma,Dongyuan Xiao,Maolin Zhou*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper derives explicit Gaussian integral representations for two sharp competition thresholds in the Lotka-Volterra competition-diffusion system: the zero-speed threshold for bistable fronts and the linear-determinacy threshold for monostable fronts, without needing unknown traveling-wave profiles or speeds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Sharp thresholds separating propagation regimes in the Lotka-Volterra competition-diffusion system are important but typically not explicitly computable, since they involve unknown traveling-wave profiles or speeds.

Method: Building on prior results on propagation direction and traveling-wave classification, the authors derive Gaussian integral representations of both critical parameters analytically. ChatGPT (OpenAI) Pro assisted the derivation.

Result: Explicit Gaussian integral formulas for both critical thresholds are obtained, providing exact characterizations of the optimal competition parameters separating the propagation regimes.

Conclusion: The new representation formulas yield exact, computable characterizations of the two sharp competition thresholds, with theoretical foundations from the authors' earlier work.

Abstract: We study two sharp competition thresholds in the Lotka-Volterra competition-diffusion system: the zero-speed threshold for bistable traveling fronts and the linear-determinacy threshold for monostable traveling fronts. Building on our previous results on propagation direction and the classification of traveling waves, we derive explicit Gaussian integral representations of both critical parameters, without involving unknown traveling-wave profiles or speeds. These formulas provide exact characterizations of the optimal competition parameters separating the corresponding propagation regimes. The derivation of these representations was assisted by ChatGPT (OpenAI) Pro, while the underlying theoretical foundations were established in our earlier works.

</details>


### [143] [Regularity theory for lattice based harmonic maps and low temperature Gibbs measures of the spin-$O(N)$ model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16833)
*Christophe Garban,Nathan de Montgolfier*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper develops a discrete regularity theory for harmonic maps from lattices Z^d to spheres S^{N-1}, proving uniform Hölder regularity in the bulk for d=2 without boundary regularity assumptions, and extends these results to positive-temperature O(N) Gibbs measures as T→0.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Classical harmonic map regularity theory is set on continuous domains (R^d or manifolds) and typically requires boundary regularity or Sobolev extension assumptions. The authors want to understand the analogous theory on lattices, where such boundary assumptions can be dropped, and to connect it to the zero-temperature limit of Spin O(N) Gibbs measures.

Method: Define lattice harmonic maps σ: Z^d → S^{N-1} as minimizers of the internal Dirichlet energy over finite subgraphs Λ given boundary data. Revisit classical regularity arguments in this discrete setting, establishing Hölder regularity in the bulk of large domains Λ ⊂ Z^2 uniformly over all boundary configurations; then extend to positive temperatures by analyzing infinite-volume Spin O(N) Gibbs measures as T→0.

Result: For d=2, the bulk Hölder regularity holds uniformly over all boundary configurations σ ∈ (S^{N-1})^{∂Λ}, with no regularity assumption on boundary values—contrasting with the continuum, which requires Sobolev-space extensions. Uniformity in boundary conditions enables a local regularity theory for all infinite-volume Spin O(N) Gibbs measures as T→0, at least for d=2 and N≥3. Implications for d≥3 are also discussed.

Conclusion: Discrete harmonic map regularity on lattices is stronger in a key sense than the continuum theory (no boundary regularity needed), and this gain transfers to a zero-temperature local regularity theory for O(N) Gibbs measures, with further consequences explored in higher dimensions.

Abstract: The purpose of this note is twofold. First, we revisit the classical regularity theory of harmonic maps in the setting of maps defined on a lattice $\mathbb{Z}^d$ instead of $\mathbb{R}^d$ or some other manifold. The target space of our harmonic maps will be spheres $\mathbb{S}^{N-1}, N\geq 2$. A harmonic map $σ: \mathbb{Z}^d \to \mathbb{S}^{N-1}$ is a map which in any finite subgraph $Λ$ minimizes the internal Dirichlet energy $\frac 1 2 \sum_{i \sim j \in Λ} \|σ_i -σ_j\|^2$ given the boundary condition $σ_{|\partial Λ}$.
  One key difference between our setting and the continuum is that our main Hölder regularity statement in a large domain $Λ\subset \mathbb{Z}^{d=2}$ does not require any regularity assumption on the map $σ$ restricted to the boundary of the domain. For example, when $d=2$ and $N=3$, the Hölder regularity induced in the bulk of the domain holds uniformly in all boundary configurations $σ\in (\mathbb{S}^{2})^{\partial Λ}$ (this is in contrast with the continuum which requires extensions in suitable Sobolev spaces).
  The fact we obtain results uniformly in the boundary conditions allows us to extend our results for positive temperatures. Namely, as $T \searrow 0$, we obtain a local regularity theory for all infinite volume Spin $O(N)$ Gibbs measures, at least when $d=2$ and $N\geq 3$.
  We also discuss implications of our analysis in dimensions $d\geq 3$.

</details>


### [144] [Gauge Transforms and Strichartz Estimates for Benjamin--Ono-Type Dirac Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16842)
*Nelson Faustino*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper constructs a unit-modulus gauge map from solutions of a Benjamin-Ono-type Dirac equation, showing the Plemelj components solve a coupled Schrödinger system, and derives a priori Sobolev and Strichartz-type estimates for n≥3.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand and control solutions of a Benjamin-Ono-type Dirac equation via gauge transformation and Strichartz estimates.

Method: Under a pointwise anti-commutation condition and dyadic regularity on the Fourier profile, construct u=exp(-iw) with Dw=ψ. Show Plemelj components u±=P±u satisfy a coupled Schrödinger system with gauge nonlinearities. Use a priori estimates and homogeneous/inhomogeneous Strichartz estimates.

Result: A priori estimates for homogeneous Sobolev seminorms Ḣ^σ of ψ with at most exponential growth, estimates for P±N, and for n≥3 and every sharp Schrödinger admissible pair (q,p), an a priori Strichartz-type estimate for ψ in L_T^q Ẇ_x^{σ,p} in terms of initial datum and a priori controls in L_T^∞ H_x^{σ+1} and L_T^∞ L_x^∞.

Conclusion: The gauge transformation reduces the Benjamin-Ono-type Dirac equation to a coupled Schrödinger system, enabling sharp Strichartz-type a priori estimates for n≥3.

Abstract: For $T>0$, we consider the Benjamin--Ono-type Dirac equation $$ i\partial_tψ(x,t) + D(-Δ)^{\frac{1}{2}}ψ(x,t) = D\,G(ψ(x,t)), \qquad (x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^n\times[0,T], $$ where $(-Δ)^{\frac{1}{2}}$ is the fractional Riesz operator, $D$ is the Dirac operator associated with the Clifford algebra of signature $(0,n)$, and $G(\cdot)$ a scalar-valued quadratic nonlinearity. Under a pointwise anti-commutation condition on $ψ$ and a uniform dyadic regularity assumption on its spatial Fourier profile, we construct a unit-modulus gauge map $u$ by $ u=\exp\bigl(-iw\bigr),$ where $w$ is a solution of $Dw=ψ$. Assuming, in addition, the stated regularity and commutation conditions on $w$, we show that the Plemelj components $u_{\pm}=P_{\pm}u$ solve a coupled Schrödinger system with gauge nonlinearities $P_{\pm}N(ψ,u)$, where $$ N(ψ(x,t),u(x,t)) = -2P_{-}\!\left[ G(ψ(x,t)) \right]u(x,t). $$ We also obtain a priori estimates for the homogeneous Sobolev seminorms $\dot H^σ$ of $ψ(\cdot,t)$, with at most exponential growth on $[0,T]$, together with estimates for $P_{\pm}N(ψ(\cdot,t),u(\cdot,t))$. Combining these bounds with the homogeneous and inhomogeneous Strichartz estimates for the Schrödinger system, we obtain, for $n\geq 3$ and every sharp Schrödinger admissible pair $(q,p)$, an a priori Strichartz-type estimate for $ψ$ in $L_T^q\dot W_x^{σ,p}$, expressed in terms of the initial datum $ψ(\cdot,0)$ and the a priori controls of $ψ$ in $L_T^\infty H_x^{σ+1}$ and $L_T^\infty L_x^\infty$.

</details>


### [145] [A cell problem for the boundary density and approximation of boundary correctors in homogenization theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17026)
*Shari Moskow*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper shows the Dirichlet-to-Neumann operator N_n of the half-space boundary layer problem satisfies a nonlinear Riccati equation on the period cell, enabling computation of the boundary layer tail d(n) at irrational normals without rational approximation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The boundary corrector θ_ε in two-scale expansions for periodic elliptic problems converges to θ*, whose boundary data is the boundary layer tail d(n). Computing d(n) (via the Dirichlet-to-Neumann operator N_n) is essential but previously depended on the half-space problem; the authors seek a cell-local characterization enabling practical computation.

Method: Using the lifted formulation of Gérard-Varet and Masmoudi, where d(n) is given by cell correctors and the Dirichlet-to-Neumann operator N_n of the half-space problem, the authors derive a nonlinear Riccati equation for N_n on the period cell alone, with the depth derivative replaced by a commutator with the directional derivative along n. This cell problem is solved by a Riccati doubling algorithm.

Result: N_n satisfies a nonlinear equation on the period cell alone, so d(n) and its boundary density can be computed on the cell. The Riccati doubling algorithm computes d(n) at irrational normals without rational approximation. Dropping the commutator term yields an operator square root approximation for N_n capturing the leading term of d(n) for small contrast (truncated problem). θ_ε at finite ε is approximated by two constant-coefficient solutions, one with oscillatory boundary data and one θ*, tested on the disk.

Conclusion: The boundary layer tail can be computed cell-locally via a Riccati cell problem, removing the need for rational approximation at irrational normals; the method extends to an approximation of the finite-ε corrector θ_ε.

Abstract: We study the boundary corrector $θ_ε$ in the two-scale expansion for periodic elliptic problems with Dirichlet conditions. By results of Gérard-Varet--Masmoudi and Prange, on smooth uniformly convex domains $θ_ε$ converges to the solution $θ^*$ of a homogenized problem with boundary data given by the boundary layer tail $d(n)$, which is determined by a half-space problem for each normal $n$. In the lifted form of Gérard-Varet and Masmoudi, $d(n)$ is given explicitly by the cell correctors and the Dirichlet-to-Neumann operator $N_n$ of this half-space problem. Our main result is that $N_n$ satisfies a nonlinear equation on the period cell alone, so that $d(n)$ and the boundary density that represents it can be computed on the cell. This equation is a Riccati equation in which the derivative in depth is replaced by a commutator with the derivative along $n$. This cell problem can therefore be solved by a Riccati doubling algorithm, and we can compute $d(n)$ at irrational normals without rational approximation. Dropping the commutator term in the cell equation gives an operator square root approximation for $N_n$ that captures the leading term of $d(n)$ for small contrast, for the truncated problem. Finally, we approximate $θ_ε$ at finite $ε$ by the sum of two solutions of constant coefficient problems, one with oscillatory boundary data and the other $θ^*$, and test it on the disk.

</details>


### [146] [Fundamental solution expansions in stochastic homogenization of parabolic systems with time-dependent coefficients](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16846)
*Junxin Pan,Li Wang,Qiang Xu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes quenched and annealed Gaussian bounds and quantitative two-scale expansions for fundamental solutions of uniformly parabolic systems with stationary ergodic random coefficients.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Fundamental solutions of parabolic systems with random coefficients are central to stochastic homogenization, but sharp kernel-level Gaussian bounds and quantitative two-scale expansions (especially for spatial and mixed derivatives) under ergodicity and local regularity were not fully available.

Method: Combines large-scale regularity theory, extended correctors, weighted estimates, and a new parabolic weak-norm estimate to pass from deterministic pairings to local annealed estimates without interchanging expectation and a supremum over test functions. Gradient and mixed-derivative expansions are built iteratively from the kernel expansion.

Result: Quenched Gaussian bounds for the kernel and its spatial derivatives with stationary random prefactors; a spectral gap assumption yields all finite moments and annealed Gaussian bounds. Quantitative two-scale expansions for the kernel, its gradient, and mixed spatial derivative hold in every finite probability moment with Gaussian decay, with dimension-dependent growth governed by a corrector weight and only one extra logarithmic factor in derivative estimates (none for the mixed derivative).

Conclusion: The work provides a unified framework giving sharp annealed and quenched parabolic kernel estimates and quantitative two-scale expansions, with the corrector weight capturing dimension-dependent growth and minimal logarithmic losses.

Abstract: This paper studies fundamental solutions of uniformly parabolic systems with stationary random coefficients depending on space and time. Under ergodicity and local regularity, we establish quenched Gaussian bounds for the kernel and its spatial derivatives, with stationary random prefactors. A spectral gap assumption gives moments of every finite order for these prefactors and hence annealed Gaussian bounds. We also prove quantitative two-scale expansions for the kernel, its spatial gradient, and its mixed spatial derivative. The error estimates hold in every finite probability moment and retain Gaussian decay. Their dimension-dependent growth is described by a corrector weight, with a single additional logarithmic factor in the derivative estimates. The proofs combine large-scale regularity, extended correctors, weighted estimates, and a parabolic weak-norm estimate. The latter supplies the passage from deterministic pairings to local annealed estimates without interchanging expectation and a supremum over test functions. The mixed-derivative estimate builds on the gradient expansion and does not introduce a further logarithmic loss.

</details>


### [147] [Limit cycles govern wave energy accumulation in space-time-varying materials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16847)
*Bryn Davies,Erik Orvehed Hiltunen*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies wave energy amplification and concentration in smooth, periodic space-time media via characteristic curves of the wave equation, showing stable limit cycles attract neighboring characteristics, focus energy, and persist under perturbations, with analytical rate estimates and a Floquet-Fourier numerical method.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
