Motivation: Understanding how wave energy can be amplified and concentrated in space-time modulated media is of interest, particularly the mechanism behind energy focusing and its robustness. The paper aims to explain this through the dynamics of characteristic curves and to quantify the attraction rate.

Method: Characteristic-curve analysis of the wave equation in impedance-matched settings; analytical estimation of the exponential attraction rate via the system's Lyapunov exponent; study of nonperiodic perturbations; a Floquet-Fourier numerical method for computing stable/unstable limit cycles and attraction rates; examples using modulation from superposed travelling waves; full-wave simulations.

Result: Stable limit cycles attract neighboring characteristics and focus wave energy density; exponential attraction rate estimated from the Lyapunov exponent; attraction and energy accumulation persist under nonperiodic perturbations; saddle-node bifurcations where limit cycles appear depending on relative wave speeds; full-wave simulations confirm the characteristic mechanism describes energy accumulation beyond the impedance-matched case.

Conclusion: Characteristic curve dynamics explain and predict wave energy amplification and concentration in periodic space-time media, with quantifiable attraction rates and robustness, and remain descriptive even beyond the impedance-matched regime.

Abstract: We study the amplification and concentration of wave energy in smooth, periodic space-time media through the dynamics of the characteristic curves of the associated wave equation. In an impedance-matched setting, we show that stable limit cycles attract neighbouring characteristics, focusing the wave energy density. The exponential attraction rate is estimated analytically from the system's Lyapunov exponent. We further show that the attraction of characteristics and energy accumulation persist under a range of nonperiodic perturbations. Finally, we present a Floquet-Fourier numerical method for computing stable and unstable limit cycles and their attraction rates. Throughout, we exemplify the results using the example of modulation given by a superposition of travelling waves, revealing saddle--node bifurcations where limit cycles may appear depending on the relative wave speeds. Full-wave simulations confirm that the characteristic mechanism continues to describe energy accumulation beyond the impedance-matched case.

</details>


### [148] [Existence and uniqueness of viscous-dispersive shock profiles for the Navier-Stokes-Fourier-Korteweg system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16874)
*Sungho Han*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves existence and uniqueness (up to translation) of monotone viscous-dispersive shock profiles for the 1D Navier-Stokes-Fourier-Korteweg system under Rankine-Hugoniot and Lax entropy conditions for small shock strength, constructing profiles via geometric singular perturbation theory and providing pointwise estimates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Shock profiles for the NSFK system are needed as building blocks for nonlinear stability analysis of generic Riemann solutions, but their existence and uniqueness required rigorous justification in the small-amplitude regime.

Method: Treat the small shock strength as a singular perturbation parameter and construct the profiles using Fenichel's geometric singular perturbation theory; derive pointwise estimates for the profiles.

Result: Existence and uniqueness (up to translation) of monotone viscous-dispersive shock profiles is established when the end states satisfy Rankine-Hugoniot and Lax entropy conditions with small shock strength, along with pointwise estimates.

Conclusion: Monotone viscous-dispersive shock profiles for the NSFK system exist and are unique up to translation, and the pointwise estimates serve as a foundation for nonlinear stability analysis of generic Riemann solutions in a companion work.

Abstract: We establish the existence and uniqueness, up to translation, of monotone viscous-dispersive shock profiles for the one-dimensional Navier-Stokes-Fourier-Korteweg (NSFK) system, provided the two constant end states satisfy the Rankine-Hugoniot and Lax entropy conditions and the shock strength is small. Treating the small shock strength as a singular perturbation parameter, we construct the profiles via Fenichel's geometric singular perturbation theory. We further provide pointwise estimates for the profiles, which are used in the nonlinear stability analysis of generic Riemann solutions to the NSFK system in a companion work.

</details>


### [149] [Fresnel scattering for open waveguide networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17398)
*Solomon Quinn,Charles L. Epstein*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies Helmholtz scattering for open waveguide networks with plane-wave incident data, reducing it to a Fredholm second-kind integral equation on an infinite interface to derive asymptotic far-field behavior, uniqueness, and waveguide-mode contributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extends prior analysis (arXiv:2302.04353, arXiv:2310.05816, arXiv:2401.04674) of Helmholtz scattering in open waveguide networks from previous data settings to plane-wave incident data, addressing the need to understand far-field asymptotics and uniquely solvable formulations for this configuration.

Method: The scattering problem is reduced to a Fredholm second-kind integral equation on an infinite interface, yielding an integral representation of the solution. This representation is then used to derive detailed asymptotic estimates of the solution at infinity, examine the behavior of amplitude and phase shift across a critical polar angle, and compute waveguide-mode contributions in a perturbative regime.

Result: To leading order and away from the waveguides, the scattered field is itself a plane wave; its amplitude and phase shift exhibit a jump across a critical polar angle dependent on the incident plane-wave direction. The estimates imply the scattered field is outgoing in a weak sense, enabling a uniqueness result. Waveguide-mode contributions to the scattered field are calculated in a perturbative regime.

Conclusion: The paper establishes a rigorous integral-equation framework for plane-wave scattering in open waveguide networks, characterizes the far-field asymptotics (plane-wave leading behavior with angle-dependent jump), proves uniqueness via weak outgoingness, and quantifies perturbative waveguide-mode contributions.

Abstract: This paper concerns the Helmholtz scattering problem for open waveguide networks, extending the analysis from [arXiv:2302.04353, arXiv:2310.05816, arXiv:2401.04674] to plane-wave incident data. We first reduce the problem to a Fredholm second-kind integral equation on an infinite interface. This produces an integral representation of the solution to the original scattering problem, which is then used to obtain detailed asymptotic estimates of the solution at infinity. To leading order and away from the waveguides, the scattered field is itself a plane wave. Its amplitude and phase shift exhibit a jump across a critical polar angle depending on the direction of the incident plane-wave. Our estimates also imply that the scattered field is outgoing in a weak sense, allowing us to establish a uniqueness result. We conclude by calculating the waveguide-mode contributions to the scattered field in a perturbative regime.

</details>


### [150] [Borderline double phase functionals with powers of the logarithm: regularity in the vectorial case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16900)
*Youlin Choi,Jehan Oh*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Study of regularity for vector-valued local minimizers of a borderline double phase functional with logarithmic perturbation |Dw|^p + a(x)|Dw|^p log^γ(e+|Dw|).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extends regularity results for vector-valued minimizers of this double phase functional beyond the previously known case γ=1.

Method: Comparison with frozen functionals whose regularity theory holds uniformly in the frozen coefficient, combined with higher integrability of minimizers.

Result: If the modulus of continuity ω satisfies ω(r)log^γ(1/r)→0, minimizers are locally Hölder continuous for all exponents <1; if a(·) is Hölder continuous, gradients are locally Hölder continuous.

Conclusion: Hölder continuity of minimizers and their gradients is established for all γ>0, generalizing earlier vector-valued results limited to γ=1.

Abstract: We study vector-valued local minimizers of the borderline double phase functional whose integrand is $|D\mathbf{w}|^p+a(x)|D\mathbf{w}|^p\log^γ(e+|D\mathbf{w}|)$, where $p>1$, $γ>0$ and the coefficient $a(\cdot)$ is nonnegative and bounded. If the modulus of continuity $ω(\cdot)$ of $a(\cdot)$ satisfies $ω(r)\log^γ(1/r)\to0$ as $r\to0$, then minimizers are locally Hölder continuous for every exponent less than one. If $a(\cdot)$ is Hölder continuous, then the gradients of minimizers are locally Hölder continuous. In the vector-valued setting these results were previously available only for $γ=1$. The proof combines a comparison with frozen functionals, whose regularity theory applies uniformly in the frozen coefficient, with the higher integrability of minimizers.

</details>


### [151] [Transformed Convexity for Lane--Emden Equations and Torsion on Spherical Domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17012)
*Xiuda Liang,Wenjie Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies positive Dirichlet solutions of Lane--Emden and torsion equations on geodesically convex domains of the sphere, proving convexity of transformed solutions via spherical Schur-complement estimates and continuation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding the geometry of positive solutions to semilinear elliptic equations on curved domains, particularly convexity properties such as positive-definite Hessians and geodesically convex superlevel sets, is a central problem. Previous results often required extra assumptions like diameter or circumradius bounds, especially for the torsion equation.

Method: The authors combine a spherical Schur-complement estimate with a priori bounds and continuation methods. They analyze the Lane--Emden equation with critical Sobolev-type exponent p_n^*=2n/(2n-3), the torsion equation, and a non-power reaction.

Result: For Lane--Emden, the solution set has a connected component containing the near-linear curve, projecting onto (1,p_n^*], with ∇^2(u^{-(p-1)/3})>0. For torsion, ∇^2√u<0 holds in all dimensions without diameter assumptions. Solutions have unique nondegenerate maxima and strictly geodesically convex superlevel sets. For the non-power reaction, a positive solution exists with positive definite Hessian of the transformed quantity.

Conclusion: The paper establishes geometric convexity properties for positive Dirichlet solutions on uniformly geodesically convex spherical domains, removing prior diameter/circumradius assumptions for the torsion equation and providing a unified framework via Schur-complement estimates and continuation.

Abstract: We study the geometry of positive Dirichlet solutions on smooth, uniformly geodesically convex domains $Ω\subsetneq\mathbb S^n$, $n\ge2$. Set $p_n^*=2n/(2n-3)$. For the Lane--Emden equation $-Δ_g u=u^p$, $1<p\le p_n^*$, we prove that the set of positive solution pairs $(p,u)$ has a connected component containing the near-linear solution curve and projecting onto $(1,p_n^*]$. Every pair on this component satisfies $\nabla_g^2(u^{-(p-1)/3})>0$ in $Ω$. For the torsion equation $-Δ_g u=1$, we establish $\nabla_g^2\sqrt{u}<0$ for its unique Dirichlet solution in all dimensions $n\ge2$, without an additional diameter or circumradius assumption. In both settings, the solutions specified above have a unique nondegenerate maximum and strictly geodesically convex positive superlevel sets. For the non-power reaction $f(s)=s^p/(1+λs^δ)$, with $1<p\le p_n^*$, $λ>0$, and $0<δ\le(p-1)/3$, we also prove the existence of at least one positive solution $u$ for which the transform $\left(\int_u^\infty ds/f(s)\right)^{1/3}$ has positive definite Hessian. The proofs combine a spherical Schur-complement estimate with a priori bounds and continuation.

</details>


### [152] [Local Turnpike Stability for Two-Population Second-Order Mean-Field Game Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17103)
*Mohamed Ghattassi,Fanze Kong,Nader Masmoudi*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Establishes a local turnpike property for two-population second-order mean-field games with quadratic Hamiltonians and cross-interacting running costs, showing that scalar stability conditions are insufficient and that a matrix-valued coercivity condition is the correct requirement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: For two (or more) populations, imposing a scalar stability condition separately on each species neglects cross-interaction effects, so it cannot correctly characterize stability. A proper framework must capture the coupling between populations.

Method: Analyzes vector perturbations (μ_1, μ_2) using a matrix-valued coercivity condition built from the symmetric part of the linearized interaction matrix and weighted Poincaré inequalities w.r.t. the two stationary measures. Proves equivalence between matrix coercivity and strict positivity of the coupled second variation, with a spectral characterization via the principal spectral value of the coupled stationary linearized operator.

Result: Matrix coercivity is exactly equivalent to strict positivity of the second variation. For linear cross-interacting models, explicit algebraic conditions separate stable from unstable parameter ranges. The skew-symmetric part of the interaction matrix does not change the coercivity threshold but can cause transient oscillatory dynamics.

Conclusion: Scalar turnpike theory extends—nontrivially—to multi-population systems, where stability is governed by positivity of a coupled matrix-valued second variation encoding both self- and cross-interactions. Diffusion acts independently per population, while interactions are controlled by the full coupling matrix.

Abstract: This paper establishes a local turnpike property for a two-population, second-order mean-field game system with a quadratic Hamiltonian, in which the running cost for each population may depend on both population densities. In the case of two populations, imposing a scalar stability condition separately on each species is not sufficient because it neglects cross-interaction effects. The appropriate requirement is instead a matrix-valued coercivity condition on vector perturbations $(μ_1,μ_2)$, which involves the symmetric part of the linearized interaction matrix together with weighted Poincaré inequalities associated with the two stationary measures. Consequently, in the multi-population framework, diffusion still acts independently on each population, but the interactions are controlled by the full coupling matrix. A naive scalar energy functional fails to capture mixed second-variation terms arising from costs that depend on both densities. We prove that matrix coercivity is precisely equivalent to the strict positivity of this second variation, and we provide a spectral characterization in terms of the principal spectral value of the coupled stationary linearized operator. For linear models with cross-interactions, this yields explicit algebraic conditions that separate stable parameter ranges from unstable ones. In the non-potential framework, the skew-symmetric part of the interaction matrix leaves the coercivity threshold unchanged, but it can induce transient oscillatory dynamics. Moreover, the scalar turnpike theory extends in a natural, though nontrivial, manner to multi-population systems, where stability is governed by the positivity of a coupled matrix-valued second variation that encodes both self-interactions and cross-interactions.

</details>


### [153] [Sharp dimension threshold for unique FKPP steady states and BBM recurrence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17121)
*Luca Rossi,Oliver Tough*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Stationary solutions of the Fisher-KPP equation with Dirichlet conditions are unique and exist iff the generalized principal eigenvalue is positive—up to dimension 6. In dimension 7+, both non-uniqueness and existence at criticality occur, proving the Berestycki–Graham conjecture affirmatively (≤6) and negatively (≥7), with implications for branching Brownian motion and long-time convergence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper addresses a conjecture of Berestycki and Graham concerning uniqueness and existence of positive bounded stationary solutions of the Fisher-KPP equation with Dirichlet boundary conditions, and seeks to identify the sharp dimension threshold governing these properties, analogous to the recurrence/transience transition of Brownian motion.

Method: The authors analyze stationary solutions via the generalized principal eigenvalue, establishing equivalence between existence/uniqueness and supercriticality. They construct explicit counterexamples in dimension 7 and higher (including for the classical reaction f(u)=u−u^2), and extend the analysis beyond the strong-KPP condition to obtain a general sharp dimension threshold. Results require no domain boundary regularity.

Result: Uniqueness holds and solutions exist iff the generalized principal eigenvalue is strictly positive, for dimensions ≤6. This is sharp: dimension 7+ admits non-uniqueness and existence at criticality. Consequences include that branching Brownian motion cannot survive globally without surviving locally in dimension ≤6 but can in dimension ≥7, and evolution reaction-diffusion solutions always converge in the long run for dimension ≤6, with non-convergent solutions in dimension >6.

Conclusion: The Berestycki–Graham conjecture is proven true for dimension ≤6 and false for dimension ≥7, establishing a sharp dimension threshold (with a more general threshold upon relaxing strong-KPP). This yields new results on branching Brownian motion survival and long-time convergence of reaction-diffusion equations, all without boundary regularity assumptions.

Abstract: We consider the existence and uniqueness of positive, bounded stationary solutions of the Fisher-KPP equation with Dirichlet boundary conditions. We establish that stationary solutions are unique, and exist if and only if the generalised principal eigenvalue is strictly positive (supercriticality), in dimension at most $6$. This is sharp: we exhibit examples both of non-uniqueness and of existence at criticality in dimension $7$ or higher, including for the classical reaction $f(u)=u-u^2$. This proves a conjecture of Berestycki and Graham in the affirmative for dimension at most $6$, and in the negative for dimension at least $7$. We thereby show that branching Brownian motion cannot survive globally without surviving locally in dimension $6$ or lower, but can do so in dimension $7$ or higher, mirroring the classical transition of Brownian motion from recurrence to transience from dimension $2$ to $3$. We further show that relaxing the strong-KPP condition results in a more general sharp dimension threshold for the uniqueness/non-existence result. These results are valid without any regularity assumptions on the domain boundary. As a corollary, we show that solutions of the evolution reaction-diffusion equation are always convergent in the long run in dimension at most $6$, whilst we exhibit non-convergent solutions in dimension larger than $6$.

</details>


### [154] [Non-branching properties of Regular Lagrangian Flows and well posedness of the continuity equation in the class of nonnegative solutions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17129)
*Luigi Ambrosio,Stefano Bianchini,Federico Renzi,Federico Vitillaro*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies Regular Lagrangian Flows, showing that non-branching characteristics are necessary (not just sufficient) for well-posedness, and revisits well-posedness results for special vector fields of bounded deformation and BV vector fields.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The uniqueness statements in DiPerna-Lions theory typically assert that the path X(·,x) is essentially unique for a given initial position, but whether the paths can fail to branch (as in classical theory) had not been established as a necessary condition for well-posedness.

Method: The authors work within the axiomatized theory of Regular Lagrangian Flows, using the decomposition of L^{1+d}-almost all space-time into a family of essentially disjoint characteristics. They prove a theorem (Theorem 4.3, Corollary 4.5) on necessity of this decomposition, and revisit constructions from prior work ([8,9] and Section 9 of [13]).

Result: They prove that the disjoint characteristic decomposition is both sufficient and necessary for well-posedness, strengthening uniqueness to non-branching of paths. Example 1.1 shows that with singular divergence, well-posedness may hold only for sign-preserving solutions. They obtain well-posedness for special vector fields of bounded deformation and nonnegative solutions under a mild time regularity assumption (5.3), and provide existence and uniqueness for BV vector fields in the class of nonnegative bounded solutions.

Conclusion: Non-branching characteristics characterize well-posedness in Regular Lagrangian Flow theory, and well-posedness results extend to bounded deformation and BV vector fields within suitable (nonnegative) solution classes under weaker regularity assumptions.

Abstract: In this paper we deal with the theory of Regular Lagrangian Flows, as initiated in [29] and axiomatized in [2]. In the first part of the paper we show in Theorem 4.3 (see also Corollary 4.5) that the decomposition of $\mathcal{L}^{1+d}$-almost all the space time in a family of (essentially) disjoint characteristics, the technique at the basis of the well posedness in [1] (for planar, divergence-free and autonomous vector fields), and in [13] (for vector fields having a suitable regularity in space, including BV vector fields) is not only sufficient for well posedness, but also necessary. This shows that in many cases the typical uniqueness statement of the DiPerna-Lions theory, namely that the path $\mathbb{X}(\cdot,x)$ is (essentially) unique given the initial position $x$, can, as in the classical theory, be strengthened by saying that the paths $\mathbb{X}(\cdot,x)$ (essentially) do not branch. As the simple Example 1.1 shows, when we consider vector fields with singular divergence well posedness results may hold only for solutions that do not change sign. In the second part of the paper we revisit [8,9] obtaining a well posedness result for special vector fields of bounded deformation and nonnegative solutions, under a mild regularity assumption (5.3) with respect to the time variable, weaker than the space-time $SBD$ regularity assumption made in [9] for nearly incompressible vector fields. Finally, we revisit carefully the constructions of Section 9 of [13], to see how they provide existence and uniqueness for (1.2) assuming $BV$ regularity in space, in the class of nonnegative bounded solutions.

</details>


### [155] [Local well-posedness for the exponentially modified Benjamin-Ono equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17182)
*Thyago S. R. Santos*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves local well-posedness for the modified Benjamin-Ono equation in $H^s(\mathbb{R})$ for $s>3/2$, using a Cole-Hopf transform to remove the quadratic derivative loss. In the degenerate case $\gamma=0$, the transform fully linearizes the equation giving well-posedness for all $s>1/2$, down to the scaling-critical threshold.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The modified Benjamin-Ono equation, related to Benjamin-Ono via a Miura-type transformation (analogous to mKdV/KdV), suffers from an apparent derivative loss in its quadratic part, which obstructs standard well-posedness arguments.

Method: A Cole-Hopf transform adapted to the equation's structure removes the quadratic derivative term, converting the equation into a Schrödinger equation with transport and commutator terms controllable without derivative loss. Solutions are built via parabolic regularization, and frequency envelopes establish continuity of the data-to-solution map.

Result: Local well-posedness is obtained in $H^s(\mathbb{R})$ for $s>3/2$. In the degenerate case $\gamma=0$, the transform completely linearizes the equation, yielding local well-posedness for every $s>1/2$, reaching the scaling-critical threshold.

Conclusion: The Cole-Hopf transform effectively eliminates the derivative-loss obstacle, enabling well-posedness results; in the degenerate case it fully linearizes the equation and achieves sharp well-posedness down to the scaling-critical regularity.

Abstract: We prove local well-posedness for the modified Benjamin-Ono equation in $H^s(\mathbb{R})$ for $s>3/2$. This equation arises as a modified counterpart of the Benjamin-Ono equation through a Miura-type transformation, in close analogy with the classical relation between mKdV and KdV equations. One of the main obstacles is the apparent derivative loss in the quadratic part of the equation. To overcome this difficulty, we introduce a Cole-Hopf transform adapted to the structure of the equation. The map removes the quadratic derivative term and converts the equation into a Schrödinger equation with a transport and a commutator terms that can be controlled without derivative loss. Solutions are then constructed by parabolic regularization, while frequency envelopes are used to obtain the continuity of the data-to-solution map. In the degenerate case $γ=0$, the transform completely linearizes the equation and yields local well-posedness for every $s>1/2$, down to the scaling-critical threshold.

</details>


### [156] [Multi-peak solutions for a magnetic nonlinear Schrödinger equation with exponential critical growth in $\mathbb{R}^{2}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17250)
*Pietro d'Avenia,Chao Ji,Qingyue Li*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves existence, multiplicity, and concentration of solutions for a magnetic nonlinear Schrödinger equation in R^2 with exponential critical growth and a multi-well potential, for small epsilon.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Magnetic NLS equations with exponential critical growth in 2D and multi-well potentials are physically relevant (e.g., Bose-Einstein condensates, quantum mechanics with magnetic fields), but existence and concentration results in this critical regime remain underexplored.

Method: Variational methods combined with a penalization technique.

Result: Existence and multiplicity of nontrivial solutions for sufficiently small epsilon > 0. When A is C^1, solutions exhibit multiple peaks with established concentration behavior and exponential decay.

Conclusion: The study establishes nontrivial solution multiplicity and concentration/localization properties for the magnetic NLS equation with exponential critical growth, confirming multi-peak structures for small epsilon.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the following magnetic nonlinear Schrödinger equation \[ \left(\frac{\varepsilon}{i}\nabla-A(x)\right)^{2}u+V(x)u=f(|u|^{2})u\,\ \text{in}\,\ \mathbb{R}^{2}, \] where $\varepsilon>0$ is a parameter, $A\in C(\mathbb{R}^{2}, \mathbb{R}^{2})$ is a magnetic potential, $V\in C(\mathbb{R}^{2}, \mathbb{R})$ is a multi-well potential, and $f\in C^1(\mathbb{R}, \mathbb{R})$ exhibits exponential critical growth. By variational methods and a penalization technique, we prove the existence and multiplicity of nontrivial solutions for sufficiently small $\varepsilon>0$. If, in addition, $A\in C^1(\mathbb{R}^2,\mathbb{R}^2)$, we show that these solutions have multiple peaks and we establish their concentration behavior and exponential decay.

</details>


### [157] [Norm-resolvent asymptotics for the Neumann Laplacian on periodic resonant thin structures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17294)
*Kirill Cherednichenko,Chuanjie Wu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper develops a finite-$h$ periodic graph approximation for the Neumann Laplacian on a 3D periodic network of thin rods (thickness $h$, vertex diameter $h^{2/3}$), achieving uniform generalized norm-resolvent error $O(h)$ and quantifying Bloch band/gap stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Thin-tube networks model wave propagation in periodic structures, but rigorous linking of the true Neumann Laplacian spectrum to graph models requires uniform, quantitative error control in $h$ across Floquet quasi-momenta. Prior resonant reductions were mostly fixed-graph; extending them to finite-$h$ periodic approximations with explicit rates and gauge-consistent fibres is needed to justify band and gap approximations.

Method: Neumann Laplacian on a periodic network of thin rods (edge thickness $\sim h$, vertex diameter $\sim h^{2/3}$). Extension of fixed-graph resonant reduction to finite-$h$ periodic graph approximation. Exact gauge transformation matching phase-corrected graph fibres to canonical lattice Floquet fibres. Collapsing contact points while retaining ambient edge tangents. Proof via Ryzhov boundary triple and Schur–Feshbach reduction.

Result: Generalized norm-resolvent error $O(h)$, uniform in Floquet quasi-momentum; same rate for global operators via gauge transformation. Collapsed-contact approximation gives error $O(h^{2/3})$. Quantitative Bloch band approximation and stability of open gaps on bounded energy intervals. For simple-cubic lattice: complete band-gap decomposition and $O(h)$ control of persistence of each fixed finite collection of gaps.

Conclusion: The work provides a rigorous, quantitative finite-$h$ periodic graph approximation of thin-rod Neumann Laplacians, with explicit uniform error rates and gap-stability guarantees, and offers refined spectral/gap analysis for the simple-cubic lattice.

Abstract: We consider the Neumann Laplacian on a three-dimensional periodic network of thin rods with edge thickness of order $h$ and vertex diameter of order $h^{2/3}$. We extend the fixed-graph resonant reduction to obtain a finite-$h$ periodic graph approximation with generalised norm-resolvent error $O(h)$, uniformly in the Floquet quasi-momentum. An exact gauge transformation identifies the phase-corrected graph fibres with the canonical lattice Floquet fibres, giving the same rate for the global operators. Collapsing the contact points while retaining the ambient edge tangents gives an approximation with error $O(h^{2/3})$. The proof uses a Ryzhov boundary triple and a Schur--Feshbach reduction. The estimates yield quantitative approximation of Bloch bands and stability of open gaps on bounded energy intervals. For the simple-cubic lattice, we refine the known resonant dispersion calculation to obtain a complete band-gap decomposition and $O(h)$ control of the persistence of each fixed finite collection of gaps in the thin structure.

</details>


### [158] [Modeling soliton-potential interactions for the 1-D cubic NLS via an invariant-preserving finite-difference method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17486)
*Anthony D. Stone,Christopher C. Hogan*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: A finite-difference scheme for the 1-D focusing cubic NLS with a smooth, rapidly-decaying potential is adapted to preserve mass and energy, then used to model soliton-potential interactions across varying soliton velocities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The interaction between a spatially-dependent potential and boosted solitons of the free 1-D focusing cubic NLS is of active interest, especially how this soliton-potential interaction depends on the incident soliton's velocity.

Method: Adapt a finite-difference scheme to the setting of a smooth, rapidly-decaying spatially-dependent potential and demonstrate that the adaptation preserves the appropriate mass and energy invariants.

Result: The adapted finite-difference scheme preserves the mass and energy invariants, and is used to model soliton-potential interactions at a variety of incident soliton velocities.

Conclusion: The scheme provides a reliable numerical tool for studying how soliton-potential interactions depend on incident soliton velocity in the 1-D focusing cubic NLS with a decaying potential.

Abstract: We consider the 1-D focusing cubic NLS with a smooth, spatially-dependent potential which decays rapidly as $x \rightarrow \pm \infty$. As is well known, under sufficient regularity conditions, solutions of the cubic NLS with such a spatially-dependent potential conserve both mass and energy. Additionally, in the absence of a potential, the 1-D focusing cubic NLS admits boosted soliton solutions. Furthermore, in the presence of a potential, the interaction of the potential with boosted soliton solutions to the free case is of active interest in the literature, particularly with respect to the dependence of this soliton-potential interaction on the velocity of the incident soliton. We adapt a finite-difference scheme to the setting of a smooth, rapidly-decaying spatially-dependent potential and show that this adaptation preserves the appropriate mass and energy invariants. Additionally, we use this algorithm to model soliton-potential interactions at a variety of incident soliton velocities.

</details>


### [159] [What remains when integrability fails? Mathematical structure in the K(m,m) compacton equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17489)
*James M. Hyman*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: For non-integrable dispersive equations like K(m,m) compactons, the authors propose algebraic compacton formulas and a "constraint-deficit" mechanism where finite conservation laws constrain some modes but not radiation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Integrable systems (Lax pairs) are well understood, but the structure governing provably non-integrable nonlinear dispersive equations—those with no Lax pair and finitely many conservation laws—remains unclear.

Method: Study K(m,m) compacton equations as a test case; derive universal algebraic formulas for compacton shape, width, and speed; develop a constraint-deficit mechanism from five conservation laws; use phase-plane analysis of smooth solitary waves.

Result: Exact algebraic formulas for all K(m,m) compactons; conservation laws constrain emergent amplitudes and suppress the fundamental Fourier mode of collision debris while higher radiation modes stay unconstrained; compactons emerge as a singular limit of smooth solitary waves via regularity loss and support compactification.

Conclusion: The results define an intermediate regime between integrable order and generic nonlinear dynamics, organized by scaling universality and the constraint deficit of a finite conservation structure.

Abstract: Peter Lax's 1968 discovery that certain nonlinear wave equations possess operator pairs (Lax pairs) opened the theory of completely integrable systems, yielding exact solutions, infinite conservation hierarchies, and perfectly elastic soliton collisions. When a nonlinear dispersive equation is provably non-integrable (admitting no Lax pair and only finitely many conservation laws), what mathematical structure governs its dynamics?
  The K(m,m) compacton equations, u_t + (u^m)_x + (u^m)_{xxx} = 0 for m >= 2, provide a testing ground: they support traveling waves with compact support, yet fail every known integrability test. We prove universal algebraic formulas determining the shape, width, and speed of every K(m,m) compacton. We develop a constraint-deficit mechanism explaining how the five independent conservation laws constrain emergent amplitudes and suppress the fundamental Fourier mode of the collision debris, while higher radiation modes remain unconstrained. The mechanism's conclusions rest on explicit hypotheses supported by numerical evidence. A phase-plane analysis reveals the compacton as a singular limit of smooth solitary waves through simultaneous regularity loss and support compactification. These results delineate intermediate territory between integrable order and generic nonlinear dynamics, organized by scaling universality and by the constraint deficit of a finite conservation structure.

</details>


### [160] [Recovery of Rough Sources in the Inverse Monge--Ampère Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17519)
*Gunther Uhlmann,Philipp Zimmermann*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves that a nonnegative L¹ source in the Monge–Ampère equation on a bounded uniformly convex C^{1,1} domain is uniquely determined by generalized boundary measurements (extremal boundary slopes) for cylindrical Dirichlet data, without requiring a positive lower bound on the source.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: This addresses the problem of unique determination of the source f in the Monge–Ampère equation from boundary measurements, extending the Dirichlet-to-Neumann type inverse problem. Prior results typically require a positive lower bound on the source; here uniqueness is sought without such a constraint, including cases where boundary slopes may be infinite.

Method: The measurement is the extremal boundary slope (extending the outward normal derivative, possibly +∞). For each direction ω on the sphere, one uses cylindrical Dirichlet data of the form (t_k^ω/2)|P_ω x|² with amplitudes t_k^ω → ∞. The boundary value and extremal slope determine the interior subgradient image up to a null set. An exact change-of-variables formula and a large-amplitude limit recover the Euclidean X-ray transform of f, yielding uniqueness. The argument extends to weighted equations ϑ(∇u)det D²u=f with locally integrable, bounded-below weights.

Result: Every nonnegative L¹ source is uniquely determined by the generalized boundary measurements. For nonnegative L^∞ sources, the extremal slopes are finite and coincide with interior normal derivatives, giving a Dirichlet-to-interior-Neumann formulation. The result also extends to the weighted Monge–Ampère setting for nonnegative L¹ sources, conditional on existence of the corresponding Alexandrov solutions.

Conclusion: The boundary value and extremal slope data for cylindrical Dirichlet data uniquely recover the source via the Euclidean X-ray transform, requiring no positive lower bound. This uncovers a Dirichlet-to-interior-Neumann formulation and extends to weighted equations under existence assumptions.

Abstract: We prove that every nonnegative source \(f\in L^1(Ω)\) in the Monge--Ampère equation \[ \det D^2u=f\quad\text{in }Ω, \] on a bounded uniformly convex \(C^{1,1}\) domain \(Ω\subset\mathbb R^n\), \(n\geq2\), is uniquely determined by generalized boundary measurements of Alexandrov solutions. The measurement is the extremal boundary slope, which extends the outward normal derivative and may equal \(+\infty\). For each \(ω\in\mathbb S^{n-1}\), it suffices to know these slopes for the cylindrical Dirichlet data \[ \frac{t_k^ω}{2}|P_ωx|^2,\qquad t_k^ω\to\infty, \] along any sequence of amplitudes, where \(P_ω\) projects onto \(ω^\perp\). The boundary value and extremal slope determine the interior subgradient image up to a Lebesgue null set. An exact change-of-variables formula and a large-amplitude limit then recover the Euclidean X-ray transform of \(f\), yielding uniqueness without a positive lower bound on the source. For nonnegative \(L^\infty\) sources, the extremal slopes are bounded and coincide with the interior normal derivatives, giving a Dirichlet-to-interior-Neumann formulation. The argument also extends to \[ \vartheta(\nabla u)\det D^2u=f, \] where the known weight \(\vartheta\) is locally integrable, Borel measurable, and bounded below by a positive constant. Conditional on existence of the corresponding Alexandrov solutions, the same cylindrical boundary-slope data determine every nonnegative \(L^1\) source.

</details>


### [161] [Schauder estimate in large scale for compressible planar cavity flows with fast-decaying vorticity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17522)
*Chilin Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies planar compressible cavity flows with fast-decaying vorticity, proves a large-scale Schauder estimate, and derives a sharp asymptotic growth rate bound for the cavity, s^{1/2−δ} ≲ h(s) ≲ s^{1/2+δ}.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Asymptotic analysis of compressible cavity flows had been limited; vorticity effects had largely been ignored, leaving the growth rate estimate imprecise.

Method: Establish a Schauder estimate in large scale for planar compressible cavity flows with fast-decaying vorticity.

Result: Obtains an a priori asymptotic growth rate estimate of the cavity: s^{1/2−δ} ≲ h(s) ≲ s^{1/2+δ}.

Conclusion: Provides the most accurate asymptotic estimate for compressible cavity flows to date and is likely the first work to incorporate vorticity into the asymptotic analysis.

Abstract: In this paper, we study planar compressible cavity flows fast-decaying vorticity and establish a Schauder estimate in large scale. As an application, we obtain an a priori asymptotic growth rate estimate of the cavity, that $s^{\frac{1}{2}-δ}\lesssim h(s)\lesssim s^{\frac{1}{2}+δ}$. This is by far the most accurate asymptotic estimate for compressible cavity flows and is probably the first paper that takes vorticity into account in the asymptotic analysis.

</details>


### [162] [Fractional Calderón problem with $L^{\infty}$-conductivities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17524)
*Gunther Uhlmann,Philipp Zimmermann*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: For the fractional conductivity equation with uniformly elliptic $L^\infty$ conductivities, the local exterior Dirichlet-to-Neumann map on any nonempty open subset of the exterior of a bounded domain uniquely determines the conductivity a.e. in $\mathbb R^n$, for all $n\geq1$ and $0<s<1$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper addresses the inverse problem for the fractional conductivity equation under minimal assumptions. Prior results typically require continuity, Sobolev regularity, or prescribed behavior at infinity on the conductivity. The goal is to prove uniqueness of the conductivity from exterior Dirichlet-to-Neumann data without such restrictive conditions.

Method: The proof works directly with the conductivity bilinear form, avoiding the fractional Liouville reduction. It combines exterior determination at Lebesgue points, equality of weighted solutions, and a new unique continuation principle for the fractional Laplacian in the weighted class $L_s$. This unique continuation principle is proven via the Caffarelli--Silvestre extension and boundary unique continuation for the associated degenerate elliptic equation.

Result: The exterior Dirichlet-to-Neumann map, measured locally on any nonempty open subset of the exterior of a bounded open domain, uniquely determines the conductivity $\gamma$ almost everywhere in $\mathbb R^n$, for every $n\geq1$ and $0<s<1$, with no continuity, Sobolev regularity, or behavior at infinity assumed. Additionally, a unique continuation result is established: if $g\in L_s$ and both $g$ and $(-\Delta)^s g$ vanish on a nonempty open set (distributionally), then $g=0$ a.e., without requiring local boundedness or Sobolev regularity of $g$.

Conclusion: The paper establishes strong uniqueness results for the fractional conductivity inverse problem and a general unique continuation principle for the fractional Laplacian under minimal regularity, using the Caffarelli--Silvestre extension rather than fractional Liouville reduction.

Abstract: We study the inverse problem for the fractional conductivity equation with uniformly elliptic conductivities $γ\in L^\infty(\mathbb R^n)$. For every $n\geq1$ and $0<s<1$, we prove that the local exterior Dirichlet-to-Neumann map on any nonempty open subset of the exterior of a bounded open domain determines $γ$ uniquely almost everywhere in $\mathbb R^n$. No continuity, Sobolev regularity, or prescribed behavior at infinity is required.
  The proof works directly with the conductivity bilinear form and avoids the fractional Liouville reduction. It combines exterior determination at Lebesgue points, equality of weighted solutions, and a unique continuation principle for the fractional Laplacian in the weighted class \[
  L_s=\left\{
  g\in L^1_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb R^n):
  \int_{\mathbb R^n}
  \frac{|g(x)|}{1+|x|^{n+2s}}\,dx<\infty
  \right\}. \] Specifically, if $g\in L_s$ and both $g$ and $(-Δ)^s g$ vanish in a nonempty open set, with the latter equality understood distributionally, then $g=0$ almost everywhere. This result requires neither local boundedness nor Sobolev regularity of $g$. Its proof uses the Caffarelli--Silvestre extension and boundary unique continuation for the associated degenerate elliptic equation.

</details>


### [163] [An $\eps$-regularity criterion at one scale for 3-D Navier-Stokes equations and improved upper bound on the box dimension of the interior singular set](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17569)
*Yi Lu,Rongrong Chi,Wei Ren*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes a one-scale ε-regularity criterion for 3D Navier-Stokes equations via a coarse-to-refined mean-fluctuation argument, using it to improve the box dimension bound of the interior singular set to 15/13.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding the size and structure of singular sets in 3D Navier-Stokes equations is a central open problem; improving dimension bounds gives finer insight into where and how singularities can form.

Method: A coarse-to-refined estimate argument based on mean-fluctuation decomposition to derive a one-scale ε-regularity criterion.

Result: The upper bound on the box dimension of the interior singular set is improved to 15/13.

Conclusion: The mean-fluctuation-based ε-regularity approach yields a sharper quantitative control on the singular set of the 3D Navier-Stokes equations.

Abstract: In this paper, we obtain an $\varepsilon$-regularity criterion at one scale for 3D Navier-Stokes equations using a coarse-to-refined estimate argument based on mean-fluctuation decomposition. Making use of this $\varepsilon$-regularity criterion, we improve the upper bound on the box dimension of the interior singular set to $15/13$.

</details>


### [164] [Global existence of large strong solutions to the 3D compressible micropolar equations with density-dependent viscosities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17585)
*Tiantian Liu,Xinying Xu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves global existence of strong solutions for 3D compressible micropolar equations with density-dependent viscosities when the initial density is large enough, and also for the case with only large initial shear and angular viscosities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend global large strong solution results for compressible Navier-Stokes equations to the more complex compressible micropolar system with density-dependent viscosities, and to improve prior work requiring both shear and bulk viscosities to be large.

Method: Cauchy problem for 3D compressible micropolar equations. Classical L^p estimates for elliptic systems, definition of suitable energy functions, and elaborate index analysis. All viscosities may depend on density.

Result: Global existence of strong solutions with all density-dependent viscosities provided the initial density is sufficiently large. Also, global large strong solutions exist when only the initial shear viscosity μ1(ρ) and angular viscosity μ2(ρ) are large.

Conclusion: The results are consistent with prior work on 3D compressible Navier-Stokes equations with constant viscosities and improve recent results that required both shear and bulk viscosities to be large.

Abstract: In this paper, we consider the Cauchy problem for the three-dimensional (3D) compressible micropolar equations with density-dependent viscosities. By classical $L^p$ estimates to elliptic system, defining suitable energy functions, after the elaborate index analysis, we establish the global existence of strong solutions to the system with all viscosities depending density as long as the initial density is large enough. By product, We also prove the existence of global large strong solutions with only large initial shear viscosity $μ_1(ρ)$ and angular viscosity $μ_2(ρ)$, which consists with the research work \cite{ZZ2014} of 3D compressible Navier-Stokes equations with constant viscosities and improve the recent result of \cite{HLZ} in some extent where they requiring both initial shear viscosity and bulk viscosity to be large.

</details>


### [165] [Existence of positive solutions to anisotropic elliptic equations in the entire Euclidean space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17601)
*Rulong Xie,Hailian Wang,Lei Yang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes existence of nontrivial positive weak solutions for an anisotropic elliptic equation on R^N with exponential or critical growth, and shows a perturbed version admits two distinct positive weak solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Anisotropic elliptic equations on the whole Euclidean space with exponential or critical nonlinear growth are of interest, motivating the study of existence and multiplicity of positive weak solutions.

Method: The mountain-pass theorem is used to prove existence of a nontrivial positive weak solution; combining the mountain-pass theorem with Ekeland's variational principle yields two distinct positive weak solutions for a perturbed equation.

Result: Under exponential or critical growth assumptions on the nonlinearity, the equation admits a nontrivial positive weak solution, and its perturbation possesses two distinct positive weak solutions.

Conclusion: The anisotropic elliptic equation admits positive weak solutions, and multiplicity is obtained via a perturbation combined with variational methods.

Abstract: In this paper, we research an anisotropic elliptic equation in the entire Euclidean space R^{N} . Under the assumption that the nonlinearity of the equation has exponential or critical growth, we prove, by means of the mountain-pass theorem, that the equation admits a nontrivial positive weak solution. Moreover, applying the mountain-pass theorem and Ekeland's variational principle, we show that a perturbation of the equation possesses two distinct positive weak solutions.

</details>


### [166] [Global existence of the $s$-Schrödinger maps in $n \geq 5$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17668)
*Ahmed Dughayshim,Armin Schikorra*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves global existence of small-data solutions to the fractional Schrödinger map equation for $s \in (1/2,1)$ and $n \geq 5$ in a critical Besov space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is to establish well-posedness (global existence) for the fractional (s-)Schrödinger map equation, generalizing the classical Schrödinger map flow to nonlocal/fractional dispersion, in the critical regime.

Method: The authors work in the critical Besov space framework, using a fixed-point/perturbative argument for small initial data to construct the solution to the $s$-Schrödinger map equation on $\mathbb{R}^n \times \mathbb{R}$.

Result: For $s \in (1/2,1)$ and $n \geq 5$, small initial data in the critical Besov space yield a global solution.

Conclusion: The $s$-Schrödinger map equation admits global solutions for sufficiently small critical Besov data in dimensions $n \geq 5$ and $s \in (1/2,1)$.

Abstract: We study the $s$-Schrödinger map equation \[ \begin{cases} \partial_t u = -u \wedge (-Δ)^s u
  \quad \text{on } \mathbb{R}^n \times \mathbb{R}\\ u: \mathbb{R}^n \times \mathbb{R} \to \mathbb{S}^2\\ u(x,0) = u_0. \end{cases} \] For $s \in (1/2,1)$ and $n \geq 5$ we prove that for small initial data $u_0$ in the critical Besov space one can obtain a global solution.

</details>


### [167] [Vanishing viscosity limit of the stationary Navier-Stokes equations in an expanding annulus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17754)
*Xinghong Pan,Chuankai Zhao,Jianfeng Zhao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves the vanishing viscosity limit for the 2D stationary Navier-Stokes equations in an expanding annulus with fixed width, constructing an approximate solution via higher-order asymptotic expansion and validating the boundary layer expansion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The different boundary conditions (large rotation perturbation at one boundary, rest at the other) create asymmetric boundary layer effects near the interior and exterior boundaries, leading to different boundary layer thicknesses and leading-order equations that need to be understood via vanishing viscosity limit.

Method: Higher-order asymptotic approximation is used to construct an approximate solution to the 2D Navier-Stokes equations. The leading-order Euler solution is the Taylor-Couette flow (Ar + B/r)e_θ with constants A and B determined by the Prandtl-Batchelor theory. Variously weighted energy estimates up to third-order derivatives of the error solution are constructed to close the energy of the nonlinear system.

Result: The validity of the boundary layer expansion is established, and the convergence of the approximate solution to the 2D Navier-Stokes equations is shown despite the growing tangential velocity perturbation as the annulus expands.

Conclusion: The vanishing viscosity limit is rigorously established for this expanding-annulus setting, with the Taylor-Couette flow as the Euler limit and boundary layer effects resolved via asymptotic expansion and weighted energy estimates.

Abstract: In this paper, we establish vanishing viscosity limit of the 2D stationary Navier-Stokes equations in an expanding annulus with a fixed width. The flow is subjected to a large perturbation of a rotation of rigid body at one boundary and stays at rest at another boundary. Due to this different boundary condition setting, the boundary layer effects are different near the interior and exterior boundaries, which result in different thickness of boundary layer and different leading order boundary layer equations. We will construct an approximate solution to this 2D Navier-Stokes equations by using higher order asymptotic approximation and show the validity of the boundary layer expansion. The leading order of the Euler solution is the Taylor-Couette flow $\left(Ar+B\frac{1}{r}\right)e_θ$ for some suitable constant $A$ and $B$, which is determined by using the principle of the Prandtl-Batchelor theory. The perturbation of the tangential velocity becomes large as the annulus expands, which causes heavy difficulties when we show the convergence of the approximate solution. Variously weighted energy estimates up to third-order derivatives of the error solution are constructed to close the energy of the nonlinear system.

</details>


### [168] [Ground states of a logarithmic Schrödinger equation with a power perturbation in $\mathbb{R}^N$: existence, uniqueness and nondegeneracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17766)
*Jinqi Yan,Bo You*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies existence, uniqueness, and asymptotic behavior of positive ground state solutions for a logarithmic Schrödinger equation with a power nonlinearity in R^N.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish existence and uniqueness of positive ground state solutions for -Δu = u log u^2 + μ|u|^{p-1}u, addressing non-smooth logarithmic nonlinearity and its qualitative properties.

Method: Minimization on the Nehari manifold combined with uniform compactness and an exterior coefficient estimate preventing vanishing of normalized difference and kernel sequences.

Result: Existence of positive ground states for all μ>0; solutions are smooth, radially symmetric about some point, strictly decreasing radially. As μ→0, they converge uniformly to U_0(x)=exp((N-|x|^2)/2) in logarithmic Orlicz-Sobolev space and C^{2,α}. For small μ, solutions are unique up to translations with kernel spanned by translation derivatives.

Conclusion: The paper fully characterizes ground state solutions including uniqueness up to translations for small μ and kernel structure of the linearized operator.

Abstract: The objective of this paper is to study the existence and uniqueness of positive ground state solutions of the following Schrödinger problem \[
  -Δu=u\log u^2+μ\abs{u}^{p-1}u\qquad\text{in }\mathbb {R}^N, \] where $N\ge2$, $1<p<2^*-1$ and $μ>0.$ We prove that there exists a positive ground state solution for every $μ>0$ and every such solution is smooth, radially symmetric with respect to some point, strictly decreasing in the radial variable. Moreover, as $μ\downarrow0,$ the positive ground state solutions centered at the origin will converge, uniformly over the ground-state set, to $U_0(x)=\exp(\frac{(N-\abs{x}^2)}{2})$ in the logarithmic Orlicz--Sobolev space and in $C^{2,α}(\R^N)$ for every $α\in(0,1),$ respectively. Furthermore, for sufficiently small $μ,$ we also prove that the positive ground state solutions are unique up to translations and the kernel space of the real scalar linearized operator can be spanned by the translation derivatives. The proof combines minimization on the Nehari manifold with uniform compactness and an exterior coefficient estimate that prevents normalized difference and kernel sequences from vanishing locally.

</details>


### [169] [Cubic Nonlinear Schrödinger Systems and Orthonormal Fermionic Ground States in $\mathbb R$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17941)
*Bin Chen,Yujin Guo,Yong Luo,Juncheng Wei*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper classifies all nontrivial solutions of a 1D nonlinear Schrödinger system in the cubic case p=2, proves non-degeneracy of the linearized operator, derives exact L^2-mass formulas, and resolves conjectures on a fermionic variational problem J_N(p).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Systems of coupled NLS equations with orthonormal constraints arise in fermionic quantum many-body problems. Prior works (Gontier–Lewin–Nazar; Frank–Gontier–Lewin) posed conjectures about existence/attainment of ground states in such systems, which this paper aims to settle.

Method: For the cubic case p=2 and arbitrary N≥2, the authors fully classify nontrivial solutions, establish non-degeneracy of the linearized operator at any nontrivial solution, and derive exact L^2-mass formulas in terms of distinct Lagrange multipliers. They then analyze the fermionic variational problem J_N(p) via this structural classification.

Result: All nontrivial solutions are classified; the linearized operator is non-degenerate at any nontrivial solution; exact L^2-mass formulas are obtained. Consequently, for p=2 the system has no orthonormal solution for any N≥2. For the variational problem J_N(p), there exists δ_N>0 such that J_N(p) is attained for p∈(2−δ_N,2) but not attained for p∈[2,2+δ_N).

Conclusion: The results settle conjectures of Gontier–Lewin–Nazar (ARMA, 2021) and Frank–Gontier–Lewin (CMP, 2021), providing a complete structural picture of the cubic system and resolving the attainment question for the fermionic variational problem.

Abstract: We study the one-dimensional nonlinear Schrödinger system \[ -u_i''-2\Big(\sum_{k=1}^N u_k^2\Big)^{p-1}u_i=μ_i u_i \quad \mbox{in } \ \mathbb R,\ \ i=1,2,\cdots,N, \tag{S} \] where $u=(u_1,\cdots,u_N)\in H^1(\mathbb{R})^N$, $μ_1\leqμ_2\leq\cdots\leqμ_N<0$, $1<p<3$ and $N\geq 2$ are arbitrary. For the cubic case where $p=2$ and $N\ge 2$, we establish the following structural results of the system $(S)$: (i) all nontrivial solutions can be classified completely; (ii) the linearized operator at any nontrivial solution is non-degenerate; (iii) exact $L^2$-mass formulas of all nontrivial solutions are derived in terms of distinct Lagrange multipliers $μ_i$. As the applications of the above structural results, we first show that the system $(S)$ with $p=2$ does not have any orthonormal solution for any $N\ge2$. We then investigate a fermionic variational problem $J_N(p)$ with $N\ge2$, whose minimizers correspond to ground states of the system $(S)$ under orthonormal constraints. More precisely, we prove that there exists a sufficiently small $δ_N>0$ such that $J_N(p)$ is attained for any $p\in(2-δ_N,2)$, whereas $J_N(p)$ is not attained for any $p\in[2,2+δ_N)$. These settle some conjectures of [Gontier--Lewin--Nazar, ARMA, 2021] and [Frank--Gontier--Lewin, CMP, 2021].

</details>


### [170] [Existence of solution for Schrödinger-Newton system with vanishing magnetic potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17945)
*Liying Shan,Wei Shuai,Ping Yang,Jianghua Ye*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies a Schrödinger-Newton system with vanishing magnetic and electric potentials in R^3, proving existence of ground state or high energy solutions using compactness and degree theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how vanishing magnetic potential A and electric potential V affect the existence of solutions in the Schrödinger-Newton system, particularly whether ground states exist or blow-up high energy solutions arise.

Method: Shows the ground state energy of the constant-coefficient system (A≡0, V≡V∞) is isolated. Combines this with a global compactness lemma, a barycenter function, and Brouwer degree theory to establish existence results.

Result: Under suitable conditions on A and V, the system admits either a ground state solution or a high energy solution. The existence of a ground state is significantly influenced by A and V.

Conclusion: Sufficient conditions are given for existence of a ground state solution, as well as conditions under which no ground state exists and a high energy solution appears instead.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the following Schrödinger-Newton system with a vanishing magnetic potential and an electric potential \begin{equation}\label{0.1} \left\{
  \renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.25}
  \begin{array}{ll}
  (-i\nabla +A(x))^2u+V(x)u=Φu, \ & x\in\mathbb{R}^3,\\ -ΔΦ=|u|^2, \ & x\in\mathbb{R}^3,
  \end{array}
  \right. \end{equation} where $i$ is the imaginary unit, $A:\mathbb{R}^3\to \mathbb{R}^3$ is a continuous magnetic potential, and $V:\mathbb{R}^3\to \mathbb{R}$ is a continuous electric potential. First, we show that the ground state energy of system with $A(x)\equiv0$ and $V(x)\equiv V_{\infty}>0$ is isolated. Using this fact together with a global compactness lemma, a barycenter function, and Brouwer degree theory, we prove that under suitable conditions on $A$ and $V$, system admits either a ground state solution or a high energy solution. Interestingly, whether a ground state solution exists is significantly affected by the magnetic potential $A$ and the electric potential $V$. Furthermore, we provide sufficient conditions that guarantee the existence of a ground state solution, as well as conditions under which no ground state solution exists and a high energy solution appears instead.

</details>


### [171] [Local Hadamard well-posedness of the 2D primitive equations without thermal diffusion under strict stratification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17995)
*Fengjie Luo,Quan Wang,Zhengdong Du*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Establishes local Hadamard well-posedness and a unique maximal strong solution for 2D non-rotating primitive equations with anisotropic viscosity and no thermal diffusion, for non-small doubly periodic perturbations of a stratified hydrostatic equilibrium.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The 2D primitive equations with anisotropic viscosity and no thermal diffusion pose a challenge: the diagnostic vertical velocity loses a horizontal derivative, threatening to close energy estimates at a higher Sobolev order and to break continuous dependence in the original topology, especially for non-small data.

Method: Uses stratification-weighted energy estimates and a horizontal compensation argument to avoid an extra temperature derivative while keeping full vertical dependence and the vertically homogeneous velocity component from periodic compatibility; a one-sided comparison keeps higher-order norms of the more regular solution only in lower-order difference terms, enabling the Bona-Smith method for continuous dependence.

Result: Obtains local Hadamard well-posedness in Sobolev spaces under strict stratification for sufficiently regular initial horizontal velocity and temperature with vanishing vertical means, without smallness; estimates close at the same Sobolev order for these variables. Also gets a unique maximal strong solution, continuous dependence of diagnostic vertical velocity and pressure in their natural Sobolev spaces, and lower semicontinuity of the maximal lifespan.

Conclusion: The paper resolves the derivative-loss obstruction in the anisotropic, non-diffusive 2D primitive equations, proving Hadamard well-posedness and maximal-lifespan regularity for non-small stratified perturbations.

Abstract: We study the two-dimensional non-rotating primitive equations with anisotropic viscosity and no thermal diffusion. For doubly periodic perturbations of a hydrostatic equilibrium with an affine temperature profile, we establish local Hadamard well-posedness in Sobolev spaces under strict stratification. The initial horizontal velocity and temperature have sufficient regularity and vanishing vertical means, but need not be small. The estimates close at the same Sobolev order for these variables despite the horizontal derivative loss in the diagnostic vertical velocity. Stratification-weighted energy estimates and a horizontal compensation argument avoid an additional temperature derivative while retaining the full vertical dependence and the vertically homogeneous velocity component determined by periodic compatibility. A one-sided comparison places higher-order norms of the more regular solution only in terms involving lower-order differences. This structure allows the Bona--Smith method to yield continuous dependence in the original Sobolev topology. We obtain a unique maximal strong solution in the strictly stratified class, together with continuous dependence of the diagnostic vertical velocity and pressure in their natural Sobolev spaces and lower semicontinuity of the maximal lifespan.

</details>


### [172] [Sobolev Minimizers for Critical Vectorial $p$-Laplacian Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18002)
*Simone Mauro*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper classifies minimizers of critical Sobolev quotients for vector-valued maps in both whole space and bounded domains, establishing existence, synchronization, and regularity results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding minimizers of optimal Sobolev quotients for critical embeddings into L^{p*} is fundamental in the calculus of variations and nonlinear analysis. Extending scalar results to the vectorial (R^m-valued) setting and characterizing minimizers, synchronization phenomena, and symmetry breaking motivates the study.

Method: For the whole space, minimizers of D^{1,p}(R^N;R^m) into L^{p*} are classified via Aubin-Talenti bubbles. On bounded domains, W^{1,p}(Ω;R^m) is equipped with the norm involving the parameter λ>0; scalar attainment results are used to prove existence and synchronization of vectorial minimizers. At resonance (λ=0), the pure Neumann problem for the p-Laplacian with critical nonlinearity is studied, proving existence and global C^1 regularity of quotient minimizers.

Result: All whole-space minimizers are exactly u = c U_{δ,x0}, where c ≠ 0 and U_{δ,x0} is an Aubin-Talenti bubble. On bounded domains, existence and synchronization of vectorial minimizers are established. At resonance, existence and global C^1(Ω̄) regularity of minimizers for the associated quotient are proven. Synchronization of radial solutions is shown, and symmetry breaking on balls is deduced.

Conclusion: The paper fully classifies critical Sobolev minimizers in the vectorial whole-space case, extends attainment/synchronization to bounded domains, and establishes regularity plus symmetry-breaking phenomena at resonance.

Abstract: We study minimizers of the optimal Sobolev quotients for the critical embeddings $D^{1,p}(\mathbb R^N;\mathbb R^m)\hookrightarrow L^{p^*}(\mathbb R^N;\mathbb R^m)$ and $W^{1,p}(Ω;\mathbb R^m)\hookrightarrow L^{p^*}(Ω;\mathbb R^m)$. In the whole space, we classify all minimizers as $\boldsymbol u=\boldsymbol c\,U_{δ,x_0}$, where $\boldsymbol c\neq\boldsymbol0$ and $U_{δ,x_0}$ is an Aubin--Talenti bubble.
  On bounded domains and for $λ>0$, we equip $W^{1,p}(Ω;\mathbb R^m)$ with $
  \|\boldsymbol u\|_λ^p
  :=\int_Ω
  \bigl(|D\boldsymbol u|^p+λ|\boldsymbol u|^p\bigr) $. Using scalar attainment results, we establish existence and synchronization of vectorial minimizers. At resonance, we consider the pure Neumann problem $
  -\boldsymbolΔ_p\boldsymbol u
  =|\boldsymbol u|^{p^*-2}\boldsymbol u\ \text{in }Ω. $ For the associated quotient, we prove existence and global $C^1(\overlineΩ)$ regularity of minimizers. Finally, we prove synchronization of radial solutions and deduce symmetry breaking on balls.

</details>


### [173] [Orthonormal Strichartz estimates over fractal time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18042)
*Guoxia Feng,Jinbong Lee,Sanghyuk Lee*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves Strichartz estimates for orthonormal families of initial data for fractional Schrödinger, wave, and Klein-Gordon equations, integrating in time against an α-dimensional Frostman measure with ℓ^β coefficients, establishing sharp orthogonality gains and necessary conditions on β.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Strichartz estimates for orthonormal families of initial data are a central tool in the study of well-posedness and dynamics of dispersive equations, allowing gains beyond those available for a single solution by exploiting orthogonality. The paper aims to extend these orthogonality estimates to a refined setting where time is integrated against a general α-dimensional Frostman measure and coefficients are measured in ℓ^β, generalizing prior results tied to Lebesgue measure and revealing the sharp role of β.

Method: The proofs combine Frostman fractional integration, a spectral near-far argument at the endpoint, and asymmetric Hilbert-Schmidt bounds together with orthogonal frequency-block summation.

Result: For the fractional Schrödinger, wave, and Klein-Gordon equations, orthogonality gains are obtained in the admissible region along with necessary conditions on β; for fractional Schrödinger the exponents are sharp up to an endpoint, with optimality of β=q/2 requiring μ to be Ahlfors regular. For Schrödinger in d≥2, a Lorentz endpoint estimate is proved, and if d>2α the critical exponent β=q/2 holds for all 2≤q,r<∞ with (d/2)(1/2−1/r)≥α/q and r>dq/(d−α), with a restricted weak-type estimate at r=∞. Further estimates are obtained for d=1 (fractional Schrödinger) and d=2 (wave and Klein-Gordon) with α>1/2.

Conclusion: The paper establishes sharp Strichartz orthogonality estimates for orthonormal families against Frostman measures, characterizing the critical exponent β=q/2 and, since constants depend on μ only through its Frostman constant, yielding global-in-time critical estimates for Lebesgue measure when d≥3, with consequences for densities of freely evolving states.

Abstract: We study Strichartz estimates for orthonormal families of initial data when time is integrated against an $α$-dimensional Frostman measure $μ$, $0<α\le1$, with coefficients measured in $\ell^β$. For the fractional Schrödinger, wave and Klein--Gordon equations, we obtain orthogonality gains in the admissible region and necessary conditions on $β$. For the fractional Schrödinger equation the exponents are sharp up to an endpoint; the optimality of the threshold $β=q/2$ requires $μ$ to be Ahlfors regular. For the Schrödinger equation in dimensions $d\ge2$, we prove a Lorentz endpoint estimate and, if $d>2α$, the critical exponent $β=q/2$ at every pair $2\le q,r<\infty$ with \[\frac d2(\frac12-\frac1r)\ge\fracαq, \quad r>\frac{dq}{d-α};\] on $r=\infty$ we obtain a restricted weak-type estimate. Since the constants depend on $μ$ only through its Frostman constant, Lebesgue measure yields global-in-time critical estimates in this range when $d\ge3$. The proofs combine Frostman fractional integration, a spectral near--far argument at the endpoint, and asymmetric Hilbert--Schmidt bounds with orthogonal frequency-block summation. We also obtain further estimates in the low-dimensional cases $d=1$ (fractional Schrödinger) and $d=2$ (wave and Klein--Gordon) with $α>1/2$, and formulate the consequences for densities of freely evolving states.

</details>


### [174] [Stability Analysis of Bounded Solutions to 2-D Incompressible Navier-Stokes Equations in L^2_uloc Spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18102)
*Haokun Tai,Xiaoxin Zheng*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the vanishing-viscosity limit of 2D incompressible fluids in $L^2_{uloc}$ spaces, proving convergence of Navier-Stokes solutions to Euler solutions under weakened assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extend the classical Yudovich-type stability framework for Euler equations to settings without decay conditions at infinity, working in $L^2_{uloc}$ spaces and relaxing assumptions to $u^\nu_0, \omega^\nu_0 \in L^\infty$.

Method: First derive a priori estimates for Navier-Stokes in $L^\infty$ spaces. Then apply harmonic analysis tools—Littlewood-Paley decomposition and Bony's paraproduct calculus—to handle the convergence.

Result: $L^\infty$-convergence of the velocity field and $L^2_{uloc}$-convergence of the vorticity as viscosity $\nu \to 0$.

Conclusion: Navier-Stokes solutions converge to Euler solutions in the vanishing-viscosity limit under weakened assumptions, without requiring decay at infinity.

Abstract: This paper examines the stability analysis of two-dimensional incompressible fluids in $L^2_{uloc}$ spaces. Extending the classical stability framework for Euler equations in Yudovich-type settings, we establish the convergence of Navier-Stokes solutions toward Euler solutions as viscosity $ν\to 0$, under the weakened assumptions $u^ν_0 \in L^\infty$ and $ω^ν_0 \in L^\infty$ without imposing decay conditions at infinity. We first derive a priori estimates for the Navier-Stokes equations in $L^\infty$ spaces. Then, using harmonic analysis tools including Littlewood-Paley decomposition and Bony's paraproduct calculus, we prove the $L^\infty$-convergence of the velocity field and $L^2_{uloc}$-convergence of the vorticity as $ν\to 0$.

</details>


### [175] [Multi-dimensional centered rarefaction waves of gas dynamics with vacuum states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18177)
*Jin Jia,Tao Luo*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves structural stability of the 2D isentropic compressible Euler R-vacuum-R configuration for polytropic gases with γ ∈ (1,1.3] ∪ {5/3,7/5}, constructing a continuous entropy solution through the vacuum edge.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Local-in-time rarefaction fronts have only been constructed away from vacuum (where sound speed is bounded away from zero). Constructing them through the vacuum edge—and thus establishing structural stability of the R-vacuum-R configuration—has remained open. This is essentially the only vacuum-forming Riemann pattern absent external forces.

Method: A geometric formulation directly on the first-order system, combined with a direct analysis of the initial slice at the vacuum edge, where the formal high-order expansion fails.

Result: For sufficiently small perturbations, the Riemann problem admits a continuous entropy solution on a fixed time interval. The domain is partitioned by four characteristic surfaces, with the solution in the three central regions belonging to W^{2,∞} in self-similar coordinates (t, x1/t, x2), though it may contain logarithmic singularities c^α(ln c)^β at the vacuum edge.

Conclusion: The structural stability of the R-vacuum-R configuration is established for γ ∈ (1,1.3] ∪ {5/3,7/5}, resolving the previously open problem of constructing rarefaction fronts through the vacuum edge.

Abstract: We consider the two-dimensional isentropic compressible Euler equations for a polytropic gas, $p(ρ)=kρ^γ$. The $R$--vacuum state--$R$ configuration---two rarefaction waves bounding a vacuum---occurs when the two constant states move apart with sufficiently large relative velocity and is essentially the only vacuum-forming Riemann pattern in the absence of external forces. Local-in-time rarefaction fronts have been constructed only away from vacuum, where the sound speed is bounded away from zero; their construction through the vacuum edge, and hence the structural stability of the $R$--vacuum state--$R$ configuration, has remained open. We establish this stability for $γ\in(1,1.3]\cup\{\frac{5}{3},\frac{7}{5}\}$: for sufficiently small perturbations, the Riemann problem admits a continuous entropy solution on a fixed time interval, with the domain partitioned by four characteristic surfaces and the solution in the three central regions belonging to $W^{2,\infty}$ in the self-similar coordinates $(t,x_{1}/t,x_{2})$, even though it may contain logarithmic singularities of the form $c^α(\ln c)^β$ at the vacuum edge. The proof develops a geometric formulation directly on the first-order system, together with a direct analysis of the initial slice at the vacuum edge, where the formal high-order expansion fails.

</details>


### [176] [Uniqueness of solutions to the 2D compressible Navier-Stokes equations for bounded densities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18178)
*Lorenzo Pescatore,Alessandro Violini*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Uniqueness of global solutions to 2D barotropic compressible Navier-Stokes equations in the Danchin–Mucha regularity class, for general C^1 pressure laws.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Danchin and Mucha [14] introduced a regularity framework for these equations; this work addresses the uniqueness question within that class, which was not established.

Method: Proof of uniqueness by imposing that both solutions have bounded divergence, locally integrable in time; this keeps densities bounded without excluding vacuum or requiring spatial regularity of the density.

Result: Uniqueness of global solutions is established in the Danchin–Mucha class for general C^1 pressure laws.

Conclusion: Uniqueness holds under the stated bounded-divergence assumption, covering general C^1 pressure laws and allowing vacuum.

Abstract: We prove the uniqueness of global solutions to the two dimensional barotropic compressible Navier--Stokes equations within the regularity class introduced by Danchin and Mucha [14], for general $C^1$ pressure laws. A crucial assumption is that both solutions have bounded divergence, locally integrable in time, which ensures that their densities remain bounded without excluding vacuum or requiring spatial regularity of the density.

</details>


### [177] [Interior Hessian estimates for $k$-convex solutions of Hessian quotient equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18252)
*Zhisu Li,Ke Wu*

Main category: math.AP
