# math.SP [[Back]](#toc)

### [278] [An optimization problem for $δ'$ leaky stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15771)
*Pavel Exner*

Main category: math.SP

TL;DR: For singular Schrödinger operators with an attractive δ' interaction on a star graph, the ground-state eigenvalue is uniquely maximized by the most symmetric star graph. Two proofs are given: one for an even number of edges via comparison with the δ interaction star, and a general one using the Birman-Schwinger technique.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding how the geometry/symmetry of a graph affects the ground-state eigenvalue of a singular Schrödinger operator with δ' interaction.

Method: Two approaches: (1) for an even number of edges, a comparison to the δ interaction star; (2) a general Birman-Schwinger technique argument.

Result: The ground-state eigenvalue is uniquely maximized by stars of maximum symmetry.

Conclusion: Maximum symmetry maximizes the ground-state eigenvalue for δ' interaction star graphs, established via two distinct proofs.

Abstract: We consider singular Schrödinger operators with an attractive $δ'$ interaction of strength $β$ supported by a star graph and prove that the ground-state eigenvalue is uniquely maximized by stars of the maximum symmetry. Two different proofs are presented, one for an even number of edges using a comparison to the $δ$ interaction star, the other general based on Birman-Schwinger technique.

</details>


### [279] [Resonance counting and spectral gaps for hyperbolic surfaces via Fourier decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17276)
*Louis Soares*

Main category: math.SP

TL;DR: Proves an improved fractal Weyl upper bound for resonances of infinite-area convex cocompact hyperbolic surfaces, in terms of limit set Hausdorff dimension and Patterson–Sullivan measure Fourier decay exponent, yielding a sharper upper bound on the essential spectral gap.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Resonances of infinite-area convex cocompact hyperbolic surfaces and the associated essential spectral gap are central objects in spectral geometry and related dynamics; existing fractal Weyl bounds can be sharpened.

Method: Analytic/spectral-geometric approach: derive a fractal Weyl upper bound for resonances expressed via the Hausdorff dimension of the limit set and the Fourier decay exponent of the Patterson–Sullivan measure.

Result: An improved fractal Weyl upper bound for resonances; as a consequence, a sharper upper bound for the essential spectral gap for these surfaces.

Conclusion: The refined bound links resonance counting to limit set dimension and Patterson–Sullivan Fourier decay, improving the essential spectral gap estimate for infinite-area convex cocompact hyperbolic surfaces.

Abstract: We prove an improved fractal Weyl upper bound for resonances of infinite-area convex cocompact hyperbolic surfaces, expressed in terms of the Hausdorff dimension of the limit set and the Fourier decay exponent of the Patterson--Sullivan measure. This bound also yields a sharper upper bound for the essential spectral gap for these surfaces.

</details>


<div id='physics.acc-ph'></div>

# physics.acc-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [280] [Multi-Objective Design of C-Shaped Targets for Energy--Divergence Control in Laser-Driven Proton Acceleration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14824)
*Mohammad Rezaei-Pandari,Mahdi Shayganmanesh,Mohammad Hossein Mahdieh*

Main category: physics.acc-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses 2D PIC simulations and constrained multi-objective Bayesian optimization to optimize the energy–divergence trade-off of laser-driven protons from a C-shaped target, identifying feasible, Pareto-optimal designs and the underlying physical regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Laser-driven proton sources face a fundamental trade-off between achieving high cutoff energy and maintaining low angular divergence, and this trade-off needs to be systematically optimized and physically understood rather than addressed through ad hoc parameter choices.

Method: Two-dimensional particle-in-cell simulations combined with constrained multi-objective Bayesian optimization; five geometry and focusing parameters are varied to maximize proton cutoff energy and minimize RMS divergence of forward protons (E_k >= 5 MeV) under a proton-only conversion-efficiency constraint (eta_H >= 9%).

Result: Of 82 designs, 60 are feasible and 15 Pareto-optimal. Max-Energy endpoint: 28.64 MeV at 52.51 degrees; Min-Divergence endpoint: 11.47 MeV at 11.13 degrees; a representative compromise: 21.44 MeV at 38.44 degrees. Numerical tests support principal trends; reflection timing does not support a monotonic trapping-duration law, and the charge-sheath relation remains non-local.

Conclusion: The results establish a design-resolved route to controlling proton energy and angular quality, linking optimized regimes to optical-field buildup, electron energization, charge separation, and outer-sheath organization.

Abstract: We optimize the energy-divergence trade-off of laser-driven protons from a C-shaped target using two-dimensional particle-in-cell simulations and constrained multi-objective Bayesian optimization. Five geometry and focusing parameters are varied to maximize proton cutoff energy and minimize the RMS divergence of forward protons with E_k >= 5 MeV under a proton-only conversion-efficiency constraint of eta_H >= 9%. Of 82 designs, 60 are feasible and 15 Pareto-optimal. The Max-Energy and Min-Divergence endpoints yield 28.64 MeV at 52.51 degrees and 11.47 MeV at 11.13 degrees, respectively; a representative compromise yields 21.44 MeV at 38.44 degrees. Matched-stage diagnostics link these regimes to optical-field buildup, electron energization, charge separation, and outer-sheath organization. Numerical tests support the principal trends. Reflection timing does not support a monotonic trapping-duration law, and the charge-sheath relation remains non-local. These results establish a design-resolved route to controlling proton energy and angular quality.

</details>


### [281] [A modern particle-in-cell code for beam-beam simulations of future colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17557)
*Arianna Formenti,Remi Lehe,Axel Huebl,Stepan S. Bulanov,Jens Osterhoff,Andrew Myers,Weiqun Zhang,Spencer Gessner,Peter Kicsiny,Anna Kinderman,Bao Nguyen,Jinseo Park,Jean-Luc Vay*

Main category: physics.acc-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces WarpX, an open-source GPU-portable particle-in-cell code, as a modern tool for modeling beam-beam interaction points in future lepton colliders, validated against GUINEA-PIG and CAIN.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Predicting beam-beam interaction effects is a key challenge in designing next-generation colliders. These nonlinear, coupled effects (strong focusing forces, radiative effects, background particle generation) are most accurately described by numerical methods rather than analytical formulas, necessitating a high-performance, extensible simulation tool.

Method: The authors introduce WarpX, an open-source particle-in-cell code, as a tool for modeling the interaction point in future lepton colliders. It provides high-performance execution on distributed GPU architectures. They perform convergence scans and benchmark WarpX (v26.09) against established beam-beam codes GUINEA-PIG (v1.2.2) and CAIN (v2.42) across a wide range of beam parameters for different collider concepts.

Result: WarpX reproduces the benchmark beam-beam observables, including luminosity spectra, photon emission, and secondary-particle production, while enabling GPU-portable, extensible simulations.

Conclusion: These results establish WarpX as a scalable platform for high-fidelity beam-beam modeling in future collider studies.

Abstract: A key challenge in the design of next-generation colliders is predicting the effects of beam-beam interactions and using these predictions to optimize machine performance. Beam-beam interactions are characterized by strong focusing forces, radiative effects, and the generation of background particles. While analytical formulas provide insight into the dynamics of beam-beam interactions, these nonlinear, coupled effects are most accurately described with numerical methods. In this paper, we introduce the open-source particle-in-cell code WarpX as a modern tool for modeling the interaction point in future lepton colliders. WarpX provides high-performance execution on distributed GPU architectures within an open-source and actively maintained code base. We present convergence scans and benchmarks against the established beam-beam codes GUINEA-PIG (v1.2.2) and CAIN (v2.42) for luminosity spectra, photon emission, and secondary-particle production under a wide range of beam parameters corresponding to different collider concepts. Our results show that WarpX (v26.09) reproduces the benchmark beam-beam observables while enabling GPU-portable, extensible simulations. These results establish WarpX as a scalable platform for high-fidelity beam-beam modeling in future collider studies.

</details>


<div id='math.CA'></div>

# math.CA [[Back]](#toc)

### [282] [Reverse Hölder Class and Two-Parameter Good-$λ$ Inequality over Hausdorff Content](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16405)
*Long Huang,Yangzhi Zhang,Ciqiang Zhuo*

Main category: math.CA

TL;DR: This paper establishes a weighted two-parameter good-λ inequality under the Hausdorff content setting, identifying a reverse Hölder class and proving its self-improving property to extend harmonic analysis beyond maximal operators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Classical harmonic analysis relies on Lebesgue or general measures, which are insensitive to lower-dimensional and fractal subsets (zero n-dimensional measure). Hausdorff content offers a dimension-sensitive notion of size, but harmonic analysis in this setting has been largely limited to maximal operators.

Method: The authors first identify a suitable reverse Hölder class R H_{q,δ} associated with Hausdorff content, then prove its self-improving property by establishing a fundamental equivalence between reverse Hölder weights and capacitary Muckenhoupt weights. This yields a weighted two-parameter good-λ inequality.

Result: As direct applications, they obtain a weighted Fefferman–Stein inequality and boundedness results for general operators over Hausdorff content.

Conclusion: The methodology extends beyond maximal operators under Hausdorff content. Even when reduced to the Lebesgue measure case, it offers novel insights, showing the good-λ principle is not intrinsically tied to an underlying measure but can extend to a non-additive framework.

Abstract: Classical harmonic analysis is built upon Lebesgue (or more general) measure. Such a framework is remarkably successful for sets of full ambient dimension, but becomes intrinsically insensitive to lower-dimensional geometry: curves, surfaces, and more generally fractal subsets of \(\mathbb R^n\) with zero \(n\)-dimensional Lebesgue measure. In contrast, Hausdorff content provides a dimension-sensitive notion of size for lower-dimensional sets or fractal sets, but harmonic analysis in this setting is still largely limited to maximal operators. In this paper, we establish a weighted two-parameter good-\(λ\) inequality under Hausdorff content setting, providing a route toward other general operators from maximal operators over Hausdorff content. To do this, we firstly identify an appropriate reverse Hölder class $\mathcal R\mathcal H_{q,δ}$ associated with Hausdorff content, and then prove the self-improving property of $\mathcal R\mathcal H_{q,δ}$ by establishing the fundamental equivalence between reverse Hölder weights and capacitary Muckenhoupt weights. As direct applications, we obtain weighted Fefferman--Stein inequality and some boundedness on general operators over Hausdorff content. The methodology developed in this paper, even when reduced to the Lebesgue measure case, also provide novel insights, which shows that the good-\(λ\) principle is not intrinsically tied to an underlying measure, but can extended to a non-additive framework.

</details>


<div id='quant-ph'></div>

# quant-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [283] [Thermal aliases in quantum noise: sampling clocks and pulse widths](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16889)
*Zi-Xuan Wang,Xiu-Hao Deng*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives a sharp bandwidth threshold for an exact "thermal alias" on integer-time sampling lattices, showing that one fractional-time fluctuation observation (or a calibrated second pulse width) suffices to rule out every common-temperature fit when the coupling spectrum is unknown, with quantified sample costs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Collective quantum-noise measurements cannot access individual mode temperatures, and when the coupling spectrum is unknown, distinct mode temperatures can be mimicked by a single common temperature (a "thermal alias"). The paper asks what minimal additional observation makes thermal-model testing informative without mode-resolved access.

Method: For stationary Gaussian oscillator environments, the authors derive a sharp bandwidth threshold for exact thermal aliasing on the integer-time sampling lattice, dependent on temperature order via the quantum response. They use fractional-time fluctuation observations, a calibrated second pulse width at the same collective port, continuous-frequency certificates, and a binary-readout protocol with randomly interleaved matched references, collective-variance and coherence checks.

Result: Within the first alias period, sufficient integer-time correlations plus one additional fractional-time fluctuation observation exclude all common-temperature fits. The two-width route requires both widths probe one spectrum and retain measured variance scales. Certificates cover all common temperatures and shared pulse-ramp uncertainty. Fractional-clock and two-width designs need ~3.8×10^7 and ~1.6×10^8 outcomes for ≤5% type-I error and ≥95% power.

Conclusion: The work identifies which new observation makes thermal-model testing informative when mode-resolved access is unavailable, under stated assumptions of independently validated preparation, spectral support, and control specifications (validation costs excluded).

Abstract: Can collective quantum-noise measurements distinguish different mode temperatures from one common temperature when the coupling spectrum is unknown? For stationary Gaussian oscillator environments, we derive a sharp bandwidth threshold for an exact thermal alias on the integer-time sampling lattice. The threshold depends on temperature order through the quantum response. For frequencies within the first alias period, fixing sufficient integer-time correlations makes one additional fractional-time fluctuation observation enough to exclude every common-temperature fit. A calibrated second pulse width provides another route through the same collective port: both widths must probe one spectrum and retain their measured variance scales. For a specified two-mode example, continuous-frequency certificates cover all common temperatures and uncertainty in a shared pulse ramp. A binary-readout protocol combines randomly interleaved matched references with collective-variance and coherence checks to handle Gaussian oscillator loss and predictable readout drift. The selected fractional-clock and two-width designs require approximately $3.8\times10^7$ and $1.6\times10^8$ outcomes for at most 5\% type-I error and at least 95\% power on the stated alternative class. These theoretical and numerical acquisition guarantees assume independently validated preparation, spectral support and control specifications; validation costs are additional. The result identifies which new observation makes thermal-model testing informative when mode-resolved access is unavailable.

</details>


<div id='cond-mat.mes-hall'></div>

# cond-mat.mes-hall [[Back]](#toc)

### [284] [WannierSTM: Efficient superconducting scanning-probe spectroscopy from maximally localized Wannier functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17943)
*Oscar Moreno Segura,Dorye L. Esteras,Andrei Shumilin,Gonzalo Rivero-Carracedo,Riku Tuovinen,José J. Baldoví*

Main category: cond-mat.mes-hall

TL;DR: A Python program combining Wannier-based first-principles DFT with NEGF and Nambu-spinor superconductivity enables efficient STM simulations with closed-form transport expressions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Efficient simulation of STM on superconducting hybrid nanostructures with spin-orbit coupling, magnetic textures, and spin polarization requires realistic materials modeling without costly numerical integration.

Method: Combines maximally localized Wannier functions (from standard DFT packages) with nonequilibrium Green's function formalism. Superconductivity via effective pairing potential in Nambu-spinor formalism; tip and substrate coupling via wide-band limit approximation. Transport quantities (I-V, differential conductance) evaluated in closed analytical form, avoiding explicit energy integration.

Result: Rapid spectroscopic calculations over broad parameter spaces; demonstrated on spin chains, 2D and molecular magnets on superconductors, for model and realistic ab initio structures.

Conclusion: Provides a unified, computationally efficient framework for modeling superconducting hybrid nanostructures relevant to modern STM experiments.

Abstract: We present a Python-based program that combines maximally localized Wannier functions-based first-principles electronic structure with the nonequilibrium Green's function formalism to enable efficient simulations of scanning tunneling microscopy (STM). The workflow builds upon standard density functional theory packages to construct compact transport Hamiltonians that preserve the properties of realistic materials. Superconductivity is incorporated via an effective pairing potential within the Nambu-spinor formalism, while coupling to the STM tip and substrate electrodes is treated using the wide-band limit approximation. Within this formulation, transport quantities such as current-voltage characteristics and differential conductance are evaluated in a closed analytical form. This avoids explicit numerical energy integrations and enables rapid spectroscopic calculations over broad parameter spaces. The framework provides a unified and computationally efficient approach for modeling superconducting hybrid nanostructures with spin-orbit coupling, magnetic textures, and spin polarization. Representative applications in spin chains, two-dimensional and molecular magnets on a superconductor demonstrate its robust capabilities for both simple model systems and realistic ab initio molecular structures relevant to modern STM experiments.

</details>


<div id='math.PR'></div>

# math.PR [[Back]](#toc)

### [285] [Weak universality of global dynamics for the fractional hyperbolic $Φ^4_3$-model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15135)
*Ruoyuan Liu*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: This paper extends weak universality results for the fractional hyperbolic Φ^4_3 model to a regime where the Gibbs measure is mutually singular with the underlying Gaussian measure, using Gibbs measure convergence, stochastic quantization, and an extended second-order expansion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior weak universality work (Tzvetkov, Wang, and the author, 2023) did not cover the regime where the Gibbs measure is mutually singular with respect to the underlying Gaussian measure, leaving that case open.

Method: Weak convergence of Gibbs measures of macroscopic models to the fractional Φ^4_3-measure; structured representation via stochastic quantization; dynamical weak universality via extended second-order expansion and Gibbs measure invariance.

Result: Establishes weak universality in the mutually singular regime, including convergence of macroscopic Gibbs measures and a structured representation of these measures.

Conclusion: Dynamical weak universality for the fractional hyperbolic Φ^4_3-model holds even when the Gibbs measure is mutually singular with the underlying Gaussian measure.

Abstract: We continue the study of weak universality for the fractional hyperbolic $Φ^4_3$-model initiated by Tzvetkov, Wang, and the author (2023). In this work, we establish weak universality in a regime where the associated Gibbs measure is mutually singular with respect to the underlying Gaussian measure. We first investigate the weak convergence of the Gibbs measures associated with macroscopic models to the fractional $Φ^4_3$-measure constructed by Tzvetkov, Wang, and the author (2023). We then obtain a structured representation for these Gibbs measures via the stochastic quantization method. Finally, we prove dynamical weak universality for the fractional hyperbolic $Φ^4_3$-model by using an extended second order expansion and exploiting the invariance of the Gibbs measures.

</details>


### [286] [Uniform-in-time propagation of chaos for singular $L^k$ interaction kernels with rates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15852)
*Nicoleta Cazacu,Alexandre Richard,Milica Tomasevic*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: This paper proves quantitative uniform-in-time propagation of chaos for two interacting particle systems with singular $L^k$ interaction kernels and Lipschitz confinement, using a semigroup approach and nonlinear Fokker-Planck regularity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Interacting particle systems with singular interaction kernels are important in physics and probability, but quantitative uniform-in-time propagation of chaos results are scarce for singular (non-smooth) kernels. The paper aims to fill this gap for kernels of type $L^k$, $k>d$, including both moderate (mollified) and classical non-mollified systems.

Method: A semigroup approach is used, relying on: (i) regularity properties of the solution to the associated nonlinear Fokker-Planck equation; (ii) estimates of the Duhamel formulation of the mollified empirical measure; and (iii) partial Girsanov transforms for the non-mollified particle system. The mollified kernel is $K\ast V^N$ with $V^N=N^{d\alpha}V(N^\alpha\cdot)$, $\alpha>0$.

Result: For the moderate (mollified) system, under smallness of $\|K\|_{L^k}$, the uniform-in-time rate is $N^{-\min(\alpha,\frac12-\frac{d\alpha}{2}-\varepsilon)}$ plus an exponentially small in time term from the initial condition. For the classical non-mollified system, uniform-in-time propagation of chaos holds with rate $N^{-\min(\alpha,\frac12-\frac{d\alpha}{2}-\varepsilon)} + \|K-K\ast V^N\|_{L^k}$, plus an exponentially small in time contribution of the initial condition.

Conclusion: The paper establishes quantitative uniform-in-time propagation of chaos for singular $L^k$ interaction kernels, covering both mollified and non-mollified particle systems, with explicit rates depending on the mollification parameter $\alpha$, dimension $d$, and initial condition decay.

Abstract: Based on a semigroup approach, we prove quantitative uniform-in-time propagation of chaos for two systems of interacting particles with singular interaction kernels $K$ of type $L^k$, $k>d$, and a contractive Lipschitz confinement force field. First, we consider a system with moderate interaction, which means that the particles interact through the mollified kernel $K\ast V^N$, for some approximation of identity $V^N = N^{dα} V(N^α\cdot),\, α>0$. Assuming that $\|K\|_{L^k}$ is sufficiently small, we obtain that for any $\varepsilon>0$ the uniform-in-time rate is of order $N^{-\min\left(α,\frac12-\frac{dα}{2}-\varepsilon\right)}$, plus an exponentially small in time contribution of the initial condition. Second, for the classical, non-mollified particle system we establish uniform-in-time propagation of chaos with rate $N^{-\min\left(α,\frac12-\frac{dα}{2}-\varepsilon\right)} + \|K-K\ast V^N\|_{L^{k}}$, plus an exponentially small in time contribution of the initial condition. The method relies on the regularity properties of the solution of the associated nonlinear Fokker-Planck equation combined with estimates of the Duhamel formulation of the mollified empirical measure, together with partial Girsanov transforms for the non-mollified system of particles.

</details>


### [287] [Large deviation principles for 2D stochastic $g$-Navier--Stokes equations on unbounded channel-like domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16381)
*Gang He,Ji Shu,Huixia Wen,Longyu Wu*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: Establishes a Freidlin–Wentzell large deviation principle for 2D stochastic g-Navier–Stokes equations with multiplicative noise on an unbounded channel-like domain.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The 2D stochastic g-Navier–Stokes equations on unbounded domains are important, but large deviation theory for them is challenging due to the lack of compactness of Sobolev embeddings on unbounded domains, which obstructs standard approaches.

Method: Proves existence and uniqueness of probabilistically strong solutions under small noise via the local monotonicity method; establishes well-posedness of the control equation and convergence in distribution as noise intensity vanishes; then derives the Freidlin–Wentzell LDP using the weak convergence method. Uniform tail estimates are obtained via a scalar stream function and a truncation technique to handle the missing compactness.

Result: Establishes existence/uniqueness of strong solutions, well-posedness of the control equation, convergence in distribution of solutions as noise tends to zero, and the Freidlin–Wentzell type large deviation principle. Overcomes the lack of compactness on unbounded domains through stream function and truncation techniques yielding uniform tail estimates.

Conclusion: The Freidlin–Wentzell large deviation principle holds for 2D stochastic g-Navier–Stokes equations on unbounded channel-like domains, and the truncated stream-function approach effectively handles the unbounded-domain compactness obstacle.

Abstract: This paper investigates the large deviation principle for 2D stochastic $g$-Navier--Stokes equations driven by multiplicative noise and defined on an unbounded channel-like domain. We first prove the existence and uniqueness of probabilistically strong solutions for the stochastic equation under small noise perturbations by means of the local monotonicity method. Subsequently, we show the well-posedness of solutions to the corresponding control equation and the convergence in distribution of the solutions for the stochastic equation as the noise intensity tends to zero. Finally, we establish the Freidlin-Wentzell type large deviation principle for the solutions of the stochastic equation by using the weak convergence method. To overcome the lack of compactness of Sobolev embedding on unbounded domains, we employ a scalar stream function together with a truncation technique to derive uniform tail estimates for the solutions of the control equation.

</details>


### [288] [Higher-Order Paracontrolled Calculus for Geometric Rough Paths](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17307)
*Tim Van Hoose*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: This paper constructs solutions to a rough differential equation driven by distributional noise of regularity in C^{α-1} for α ∈ (1/4, 1/3), achieving lower regularity than prior work using a high-order paracontrolled approach and a new harmonic-analytic framework for iterated commutator estimates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Previous solution theories (Gubinelli-Imkeller-Perkowski, Prömel-Trabs) required higher regularity on the noise; the authors aim to push the solution theory down to lower regularity for rough differential equations driven by distributional noise.

Method: They construct solutions at lower regularity than previous work, drawing on the high-order paracontrolled viewpoint of Bailleul-Bernicot. The key innovation is a harmonic-analytic framework to prove a set of iterated commutator estimates required by the solution theory. They also demonstrate the enhancement procedure for noise ζ = dZ, where Z is fractional Brownian motion.

Result: Solutions are constructed for α ∈ (1/4, 1/3), at lower regularity than prior work, and the enhancement procedure is established for ζ = dZ with Z a realization of fractional Brownian motion with Hurst parameter H ∈ (1/4, 1/3).

Conclusion: The paper extends rough differential equation solution theory to lower regularity than previously possible, providing new iterated commutator estimates via harmonic analysis and validating the enhancement step for fractional Brownian motion.

Abstract: We study the rough differential equation
  $$
  \frac{du_t}{dt}= F(u_t) ζ_t, \ \ u(0) = u_0 \in \mathbb{R}^d,
  $$
  where $F : \mathbb{R}^d \to L(\mathbb{R}^d, \mathbb{R}^d)$ is a sufficiently regular vector field, and the noise $ζ$ is a distribution in $\mathscr{C}^{α-1}(\mathbb{R})$ for $α\in (\frac{1}{4}, \frac{1}{3})$. We construct solutions to this equation at lower regularity than previous work of Gubinelli-Imkeller-Perkowski and Prömel-Trabs using ideas from the high-order paracontrolled viewpoint of Bailleul-Bernicot. The key tool in our work is the development of a harmonic-analytic framework for proving a set of iterated commutator estimates which are necessary for the solution theory. We also show the procedure of enhancement when the noise $ζ= dZ$, where $Z$ is a realization of fractional Brownian motion with Hurst parameter $H \in (\frac{1}{4}, \frac{1}{3})$.

</details>


### [289] [Kinetic SDEs and kinetic Fokker-Planck equations with critical Morrey-type drifts, and local blow-ups](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17319)
*Jean-François Jabir,Damir Kinzebulatov,Chengjun Yue*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: The paper develops a Sobolev theory for the kinetic Fokker-Planck equation with a singular Morrey-form force field, constructing weak solutions to Brownian-driven kinetic SDEs via a fractional Duhamel series and kinetic Adams-type estimates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Force fields of the singular Morrey-form L-infinity + M^q_K type can produce local blow-ups due to strong attraction to a submanifold, which standard kinetic theories do not handle.

Method: The approach uses a fractional representation of the Duhamel series for the equation and establishes kinetic Adams-type estimates.

Result: The authors construct weak solutions of Brownian-driven kinetic SDEs in a critical Morrey setup, obtain kinetic Krylov-type estimates and a strongly continuous Feller propagator, and prove uniqueness criteria for a large class of weak solutions.

Conclusion: A Sobolev theory and well-posedness framework are established for the kinetic Fokker-Planck equation under singular Morrey-form force fields.

Abstract: We develop a Sobolev theory for the kinetic Fokker-Planck equation subject to a singular Morrey-form $L^\infty+M^q_K$ force field which can produce local blow-ups due to strong attraction to a submanifold. Our approach is based on the fractional representation for the Duhamel series of the equation and on establishing kinetic Adams-type estimates. Accordingly, we construct weak solutions of Brownian-driven kinetic SDEs in a critical Morrey set-up, obtain kinetic Krylov-type estimates and a strongly continuous Feller propagator, and establish uniqueness criteria for a large class of weak solutions.

</details>


### [290] [Large Deviations of Invariant Measures of Fractional Reaction-Diffusion Equations Driven by Multiplicative Noise on R^n](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17491)
*Bixiang Wang,Lu Yang*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: This paper establishes large deviations of invariant measures for fractional stochastic reaction-diffusion equations with polynomial reaction and unbounded multiplicative noise on R^n.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The equation features multiplicative noise on an unbounded domain R^n, where Sobolev embeddings are non-compact, posing major challenges. No prior results existed for large deviations of invariant measures of such equations with multiplicative noise on unbounded domains.

Method: Under a dissipativity condition, the authors prove a Freidlin-Wentzell LDP for solution trajectories uniform over all initial data in L^2(R^n), via a positive-time L^\infty-estimate for differences of controlled solutions. They then combine uniform trajectory estimates, contraction, and a second-moment estimate to derive large deviations of invariant measures with a good quasipotential. Uniform spatial tail-ends estimates circumvent the non-compactness of Sobolev embeddings.

Result: The paper proves the Freidlin-Wentzell large deviation principle uniformly over all initial data, and establishes large deviations of invariant measures with a good quasipotential, without weighted spaces or exponential tightness, on R^n.

Conclusion: This is the first result on large deviations of invariant measures for fractional stochastic reaction-diffusion equations with multiplicative noise on unbounded domains.

Abstract: In this paper, we establish large deviations of invariant measures for fractional stochastic reaction-diffusion equations with polynomial reaction term driven by unbounded multiplicative noise on R^n. Under a dissipativity condition, we first prove the Freidlin-Wentzell large deviation principle of solution trajectories uniformly over all initial data in L^2(R^n). The key ingredient is a positive-time L^\infty-estimate for differences of controlled solutions which is independent of all initial data and hence yields uniform convergence of controlled stochastic solutions over the entire tphase space. Then using the uniform trajectory estimates, contraction and a second-moment estimate, we further prove the large deviations of invariant measures of the equation with a good quasipotential without introducing weighted spaces or establishing exponential tightness. The non-compactness of Sobolev embeddings in R^n is circumvented by uniform spatial tail-ends estimates. This is the first result on large deviations of invariant measures of the equation with multiplicative noise on unbounded domains.

</details>


### [291] [Eigenvalue fluctuations for the crushed ice problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18013)
*Marek Biskup,Ryoki Fukushima*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: The paper proves a multivariate Central Limit Theorem for simple eigenvalues of the Dirichlet Laplacian on a domain with N small removed balls, under a scaling where total capacity stays fixed. It extends 1980s $d=3$ results to all dimensions $d\ge2$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Earlier CLT results for eigenvalues of the Dirichlet Laplacian with randomly removed small balls existed only for $d=3$ (Figari–Orlandi–Teta; Ozawa, 1980s) and relied on resolvent expansions. A general-dimensional proof was missing, and the resolvent approach was restrictive.

Method: Instead of resolvent expansions, the authors use martingale representations of the eigenvalue fluctuations and control the one-ball perturbation asymptotics via harmonic analysis methods.

Result: For eigenvalues that are simple in the homogenization limit, a multivariate Central Limit Theorem holds in all dimensions $d\ge2$, with centering by expected values.

Conclusion: The martingale-plus-harmonic-analysis approach generalizes and unifies the CLT for homogenized Dirichlet eigenvalues to all dimensions $d\ge2$, overcoming limitations of the older resolvent-based $d=3$ proofs.

Abstract: We study the eigenvalues of the negative Dirichlet Laplacian $-Δ$ in domain $D_{ε,N}$ obtained by removing $N$ Euclidean balls of radius $ε$ from a bounded open set $D\subseteq\mathbb R^d$ ($d\ge2$). It is known that, for the balls centered at points drawn independently from a distribution with probability density $ρ$ and for $N\to\infty$ and $ε\downarrow0$ so that the aggregate Newtonian capacity of the removed balls tends to some $κ>0$, the eigenvalues of $-Δ$ in $D_{ε,N}$ homogenize to those of the Schrödinger operator $-Δ+κρ$ in $D$. Here we prove that, for the eigenvalues that are simple in the limit, a multivariate Central Limit Theorem holds in all $d\ge2$ under centering by expected values. Unlike earlier work in $d=3$ by Figari, Orlandi and Teta and, independently, Ozawa from 1980s that was based on resolvent expansions, we rely on martingale representations and one-ball perturbation asymptotic controlled by methods of harmonic analysis.

</details>


### [292] [Branching explosion of Brownian Fleming-Viot systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18802)
*Yaohui Lin,Tiankui Lu*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: The paper resolves the non-extinction problem for Brownian Fleming-Viot systems in bounded domains by constructing domains in R3 where branching explosion occurs with positive probability before any prescribed time, establishing the sharp population threshold for bounded-domain Brownian explosion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The long-standing non-extinction problem for Brownian Fleming-Viot systems in bounded domains remained unresolved, specifically whether explosion (branching) can occur for finite populations in bounded geometries.

Method: The mechanism is directed transport generated by uniform copying in a periodic absorbing environment. The authors compute a positive cubic current for a two-harmonic killing profile, develop a marked genealogical transfer theorem retaining Euclidean displacement at each copy, use common ancestry to reconstruct integer winding from phase records, employ a boundary restart law with Polya weights, and apply diffusion coefficients adapted to a common Kelvin clock.

Result: For every N >= 3, a bounded connected domain in R3 is constructed where branching explosion occurs with positive probability before every prescribed positive time, from every interior initial configuration. On this event all particles converge to one boundary point. A positive cubic current is computed with explicit population coefficient and controlled fourth-order remainder. Disjoint spherical holes accumulate only at the explosion point, with boundary smooth elsewhere and area within distance r of the singularity of order r.

Conclusion: Together with two-particle non-extinction, this gives the sharp population threshold for bounded-domain Brownian explosion. For three particles, the transfer theorem covers every smooth positive periodic killing rate.

Abstract: We resolve the long-standing non-extinction problem for Brownian Fleming-Viot systems in bounded domains. For every fixed N >= 3, we construct a bounded connected domain in R3 in which branching explosion occurs with positive probability before every prescribed positive time, from every interior initial configuration. On this event all particles converge to one boundary point. Together with two-particle non-extinction, this gives the sharp population threshold for bounded-domain Brownian explosion. The mechanism is directed transport generated by uniform copying in a periodic absorbing environment. We compute a positive cubic current for the same two-harmonic killing profile at every N >= 3, with an explicit population coefficient and a controlled fourth-order remainder. To realize this transport by spherical absorption, we develop a marked genealogical transfer theorem that retains the Euclidean displacement inherited at each copy. Common ancestry makes the integer winding reconstructible from phase records and yields convergence of the stationary signed current. A boundary restart law with P'olya weights supplies the continuity needed to turn that current into stable escape. Diffusion coefficients adapted to a common Kelvin clock preserve the joint Brownian law and uniform donor marks while compressing escape into finite lifetime. Disjoint spherical holes in a ball accumulate only at the explosion point. The boundary is smooth elsewhere, with finite area and perimeter. Boundary area within distance r of the singularity is of order r. For three particles, the transfer theorem covers every smooth positive periodic killing rate.

</details>
